一

CHAPTER I

On the Solar and Lunar Kings

सूत उवाच

शुत्वैतां तापसाहिष्यां कथां राजा मुद्रावित्रतः ।

व्यासं पप्रच्छ धर्मात्मा परीक्षितसुतः पुनः ॥ १ ॥

Sūta said : Glad to hear the excellent divine stories of the Solar and Lunar races. The virtuous King Janamejaya, the son of Parikṣit, again asked:

जनमेव जयाच

स्वामिन्युंचायंना च राजां वंशस्य विहतम् ।

तथा सोमाव्यवानां च श्रोक्रामोउसि सर्वथा ॥ 2 ॥

कवयानय सर्वत्र कथां पापपूर्णाशिनीम् ।

चरितं भूपतीना च चितस्ताईशपोहोयोः ॥ ३ ॥

ते हि सर्वे पशाशिफभक्ता इति मया शुतम् ।

देवीभक्तस्य चितरं शृणवन्नोजसि विरक्तिभाक् ॥ 4 ॥

“O Lord! I am now very eager to hear the increase of the two lines of Kings. O Sinless One! You know everything. So kindly describe, in detail, the pure histories, capable to destroy sins, of the kings and their characters. The kings of the Lunar and the Solar races were great Bhaktas of the Highest Śakti, Śri Bhagavati Devi; this I have heard. O Muni! Who wants not to hear further on the glorious anecdotes of the Bhaktas of the Śri Devi?

इति राजर्षिण पृष्ठी व्यास: सत्यवतीसुतः ।

तमुवाच मुनिश्रेषः प्रसन्वदनो मुनि: ॥ 5 ॥

When the Rājarsi asked thus, the Muni Krṣna

Dvaipāyana, the son of Satyavati began to narrate gladly the several deeds of the Kings.

व्यास उवाच

निशामय महाराज विस्तारादृते मप ।

सोमसूर्यान्वयनां च तथाउन्ये समुद्धम ॥ 6 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! I am now narrating to you in detail the origin, etc., of the Lunar and the Solar dynasties as well as of other kings in their connection. Hear attentively.

शिष्योनाभिसारोजाडे ब्रहासभूच्छतुरनाः ।

तपस्तत्वा समाराध्य महावैदीं सुवुर्भाम् ॥ 7 ॥

तया दत्तवो धाता जगल्हनुं समुद्धातः ।

नागकन्मानुर्षी सृष्टि कर्तुं लोकपितामहः ॥ 8 ॥

The four-faced Brahma sprang from the navel of Visnu; engaged in practising Tapasyā, he began to worship the Mahā Devi Durgā, extremely hard to conceive. Mahā Devi, pleased at his worship, granted boon to Brahma; Brahma, the Grandsire of all the Lokas on thus getting the boon, became ready to create the world; but he could not create all on a sudden the human beings.

विद्यार्थ बहुधा चिते सुह्यर्थं चतुरानतः ।

न विस्तारं जगमाग्यु रचितापि महात्मना ॥ 9 ॥

सरबर्ग मानासान्युझानसर्वेश्वाग्रजापति: ।

मरीचिरंगिराउविश्र बसिष्यः पुलहः क्रतुः ।

पुलस्यश्रोति विद्याता: सप्तैते मानासा: सुता: ॥ 10 ॥

Though the creation was eternally fashioned by the Parmātmā Bhagavati, the four-faced Brahmā thinking over in his mind variously, could not quickly spread it out and accomplish it as a veritable fact. Therefore He, the Prajāpati, first created mentally the seven mind-born sons. These were known by the names of Marici, Atri, Anigirā, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu and Vasiṣṭha.

रूदे तोषात्मुजो2प्युर्सगात्रादो2पवत् ।

दक्षांगुगताय4-2प्यैय मानस: सनकादय: ॥ ११ ॥

वागंगुहाइक्षपन्ती जाता स्वर्गीमुन्तरी ।

वीरिणी नाम विख्याता पुगणेषु महीपते ॥ १२ ॥

असिक्षिति च नान्मा सा यस्यां जातोअथ नादरः।

देवर्षिप्रवरः कामं बढ़णो मानस: सुतः ॥ 1 3 ॥

Next sprang Rudra from the anger of Prajāpati, Nārada from his lap; Dakṣa from his right thumb. Thus Sanaka and the other Rṣi were also his mind bornson. O King! The wife of Dakṣa was born from the left thumb of Prajāpati; this all-beautiful daughter is well known in all the Purāṇas by the name of Virinī and Asiknī. Nārada, the chief of the Devarsis, was born, on some other occasion in her womb.

जननेजय उवाच

अत्र मे सैरयो बहुत् वघुकं भवता चचः ।

वीरियां नराटे जातो दक्षाविति महातपः ॥ १ ४ ॥

कयं दक्षस्य पन्चां तु वीरियां नराटे पुनि: ।

जातो हि बहुण: पुत्रो धर्मसंतपासोनमः ॥ १५ ॥

विचित्रमिद्याग्रतां भवता नारदस्य च ।

दक्षाजन्माय भार्थायां तद्वरव सवित्तम् ॥ १६ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Brahman! A great doubt arises in me to hear that the great ascetic Nārada was born of Dakṣa in the womb of Virinī. The Muni Nārada indeed, was the son of Brahmā; moreover he was the foremost of the ascetics and especially endowed with the knowledge of Dharma; how, then, can he be born of the womb of the Dakṣa's wife Virinī. Well, if that be so, then describe, in detail, that wonderful story of the birth of Nārada in the womb of Virinī.

पूर्वद्रेहः कथं मुक्तः शापात्कस्य महात्मना ।

नारदेन बहुजेन कस्माज्ञन्म कृतं मुने ॥ 17 ॥

O Muni! Under whose curse, the high-souled Nārada, though very wise, had to leave his first body and be born again.

व्यास उवाच

ब्रहणासौ समाविष्टो दक्ष: सृष्टथर्मादितः ।

प्रतः सूजेते सुभुगं शुद्धितोः स्वयंपुं च ॥ १ ॥ ॥

ततः पझसह्वांश्र उनयामास वीर्यन्ति ।

दक्षः प्रजापतेः पुत्रान्वीरिणयं बलवत्तग्न ॥ 19 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King, Brahmā, the Self-born, with a view to create offspring, ordered first Dakṣa: “Go and multiply innumerable children for the increase of the world.” Thus ordered by his father, the Prajāpati Dakṣa produced five thousand powerful and heroic sons in the womb of Virinī.

दूर्वहा तातारः: पुत्रान्सर्वान्वर्यिषयुप्रजाः ।

उवाच प्रहस-वाचं देविषि: कातनोदितः ॥ 20 ॥

भूवः प्रपाणमाताला सहुकामाः प्रजाः कथम् ।

लौकानां हास्यतां यूयं गमिष्यव न संशयः ॥ 21 ॥

पृष्विथ्या वै प्रपाणं तुं जात्वा कार्यः समुधामः ।

कृतो3सी सिद्धिमायाति नान्यथेति विनिश्रयः ॥ 22 ॥

Seeing all the sons of Dakṣa, desirous to multiply, the Devarsī Nārada, urged on, as it were, by Fate, began to laugh at them. How do you desire to multiply when you know not the dimensions and capacity of earth; so you will, no doubt, be put to ridicule and laughter. Rather, if you proceed on work, knowing beforehand the earth's capacity, your efforts will be fruitful. Other wise, your attempt will, no doubt, end in failure.

वातिगण वत युयं वै यद्धान्ताव भुवलस्तम् ।

सभुघातः प्रजा: कर्तुं कर्थ सिद्धिर्भविष्यति ॥ 23 ॥

Alas! You are awfully illiterate! Not knowing the dimensions of the world, you are ready to multiply your progeny; how, then, can you meet wit success!

व्यास उवाच

नारदेनैवमुक्तासे हर्षशा देवयोगतः ।

अन्योन्यमूचः सहसा सत्यगाह मुनि: किल ॥ 24 ॥

Book VII Chapter 1

जाला प्रमाणमुख्यांस्तु सुखं सक्ष्यामड़े प्रजा: ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing, all on a sudden, these words, Haryaśva and other sons began to speak with each other “What this Muni has told, is very true.” Let us then ascertain the earth’s dimensions; we can easily multiply afterwards.

इति संचिन्य ते सर्वे प्रयाता: प्रेक्षितुं पुच: ॥ 2 5 ॥

तलं सर्वे परितुतं वचनात्रादस्य च ।

प्राययं केचित्रतः कामं दक्षिणस्यां तथायरे । २६ ॥

प्रतीच्यानुतरस्यां तु कृतोत्साहा: संमततः ।

दक्ष: पुजानान्द्व्य वा पीडितस्तु शुचा धृष्णम् । २७ ॥

Thus saying, they all went out to reconnoitre the earth. Thus excited at Nārada's words, some went eastward, some southwards, some towards the north and some went to west all simultaneously and, as they liked, to make a survey of the earth. When the sons went away, Dakṣa became exceedingly sorry on their absence.

अन्यानुत्पाद्यामास प्रजार्थ कृतनिध्रयः ।

तेपि तनोघातः कर्तु प्रजार्थयुधमं सुत्ति ॥ 28 ॥

नारदः प्राह तान्द्वया पूर्वे यद्वचनं भूति: ।

बालिशा बत यूयं वै यद्धताला भुवः किल ॥ 29 ॥

प्रमाणं तु प्रजाः कर्तुं प्रवृत्ता: केन हेतुतः ।

शुवा वायवं मुनेतेर्पि मत्वा सर्व विमोहिताः ॥ 30 ॥

जमु: सर्वे यथापूर्वे भातरक्षितितास्ताव ।

तान्सुतान्यस्थितान्द्वहा दक्षः कोवसमन्त्रिनः ॥ 31 ॥

शणाप नारदे रोषाच्यशोकसमूढ़वात् ।

Bent again on multiplying, he begat other sons; those sons again wanted to procreate. Seeing them, Nārada again laughed and said: "Alas! What fools are you! Not knowing the dimensions of the earth, why are you ready to procreate?" They were deluded by Nārada's words, took them as true, and went out as their elder brothers did. Not being able to see those sons, Prajāpati Dakṣa became very sorrowful for them and cursed Nārada in rage.

दर्श उवाच

नासिला मे सुता यस्यास्तमाश्रयावपूर्वि ॥ 32 ॥

पायोनानेन दुर्बुद्धे गर्भावसं प्रजेति च ।

पुत्रो मे भव कामं तं चतो मे धर्षिता: सुतः ॥ 33 ॥

Dakṣa said: “O Evil-minded One! You have destroyed my sons; so be yourself destroyed; you will have to be born in the womb for your sin in causing the death of my sons; you have caused my sons to go abroad; so you must be born as my son.

इति शपतस्तो जातो वीरियणां नारदो मुनि: ।

पहिर्धूंधोरूपजल्कन्य वीरिष्यामिति नः श्रुन्ति ॥ 34 ॥

Thus cursed by Dakṣa, Nāradā had to take his birth in the womb of Virinī. I heard also that the Prajāpati Dakṣa begat afterwards sixty daughters in her womb.

शोकें विहाय पुजाणां दक्षः परमधर्मवित् ।

तासां जयोद्वा प्रादात्करणयाय महात्मने ॥ 35 ॥

द्वा धर्माय सोमाय सलविशंगित भुपते ।

दे चैव भृगवे प्रादाचलतरोप्रत्येभिने ॥ 36 ॥

दे चैवांगिरसे कन्ये तर्वेबांगिरसे पुनः ।

तासां पुत्राक्ष योगाक्ष देवाक्ष दानवासला ॥ 37 ॥

जाता बलसमायुक्ता: परस्परियोक्षतः ।

रागद्वान्विततः सर्वे परस्परिविरितः ।

सर्वे मोहावृताः शूरा हृषयबन्तिमाधिनः ॥ 38 ॥

इति शीदैवीभगवते महापुराणे सतमास्कन्ये

प्रभवोउध्याय: 11 1 11

O King! Dakṣa, the great knower of Dharma, then gave up the sorrows for his sons and married his thirteen daughters to the high-souled Kaśyapa, ten daughters to Dharma, twenty-seven daughters to the Moon, two to Bhrgu, four to Ariṣṭanemi, two to Krśāśva and the remaining two to Aṅgirā. Their sons and grandsons, the Devas and Dānavas, became powerful but antagonistic towards each other. All of them were heroes and very Māyāvīs; so, deluded by greed and jealousy, they quarrelled amongst each other.

Here ends the First Chapter in the Seventh Book on the beginning of the narrative of the Solar and the Lunar lines of kings in the Mahā Purānam Śrīmadveṭbīhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER II

On the Piercing of the Eyes of Cyavana Muni

जनमेव जय चाच

मामाख्याहि महाभाग राजां वंर्णे सवित्तग्न ।

सूवी-नवयमुत्तानां धर्मज्ञानां विशेषतः ॥ १ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Highly Fortunate One! Kindly narrate in detail the spread of the families of those kings in the Solar line who were born and who were especially endowed with the knowledge of Dharma.

व्यास उचाच

शृण भारत वक्ष्यापि रविवंशस्य विस्तरम् ।

यथा श्रुतं मया पूर्वे नारदाप्रविस्तमान् ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O Bhārata! I now speak to you exactly what I heard of yore, from Nārada, the best of the Rṣi, how the Solar race spread.

एकादा नारदः श्रीमान्सरत्याल्पतेरे रुषे ।

आजागामाओं पुण्ये विचार-संदेषच्छया मुनिनः ॥ 3 ॥

प्रणाथ्य शिरसा यादी तस्योः संशित्यतस्ता ।

तततस्वासनं दत्ता कृत्वाहर्णणमधारतृ ॥ 4 ॥

Once, on an occasion, the Muni Śrimān Nārada, on his tour, came at his will to my holy hermitage on the beautiful banks of the Sarasvati river. On seeing him I bowed down at his feet and then remained standing before him.

विविधत्पूर्ववित्या ततुष्कावान्चनं तिवद् ।

पातितोई भूमिश्रेष्ठ पूज्यस्यामानेने चे ॥ ५ ॥

I then gave him a seat and worshipped him with great esteem. I then said to him: "O Best of Munis! You are worshipped by the whole universe; my retreat is sanctified by your coming.

कथां कथय सर्वत्र राज्ञां चरितसंयुतान् ।

राजानो ये समाख्याताः सपतेप्रतिम्मानोः कुले ॥ 6 ॥

तेषामुवर्तितुला चरितं परमाद्धुतम् ।

श्रोत्कामोउम्यहं ब्रह्म-सुर्ववैशस्य विशतरम् ॥ 7 ॥

O All-knowing One! Kindly narrate the histories of the Kings that were famous in the family of the seventh Manu; they were unequalled as far as their

origin was concerned and their characters as well wonderful. Therefore I am very eager to know, in detail, the history of the Solar race.

समाजालाई मुनिश्रेष्ठ समास्यामसुर्वकम् ।

इति पृष्टो मया राजत्रादः परमार्थवित् ॥ 8 ॥

उवाच प्रहसन्धितः समाभाष्य मुद्राउ-वयम् ।

O Muni! Describe shortly or in detail as the circumstances may require." O King! When I made this question, Nārada, the knower of the Highest Reality, gladly smiled, and, addressing me, began to describe the history of the Solar race.

नारद उवाच

श्रणु सत्यवतीसूने राजां वेगमन्तुनम् ॥ 9 ॥

पावनं कर्णिकुखदं धर्मानाविद्धियतूनम् ।

बहाा पूर्वे जगत्कर्ता नाधियंकजसभ्यः ॥ १ ० ॥

विष्णोरिति पुराणेषु प्रसिद्धः परिकीर्तितः ।

सर्वतः सर्वकर्तासी स्वधभूः सर्वशिक्षणम् ॥ १ १ ॥

तपस्तवा स विश्वाता वर्णिणाम्बुतं पुरा ।

सृष्टिकामः जिलो ध्याना प्राप्य सक्रिनमुतनाम् ॥ १२ ॥

युज्ञतुर्पाद्यायास माननाशुभतक्षणान् ।

Nārada said: O son of Satyavati! The history of the royal families is very holy and pleasant to hear; the more so when they are heard, one acquires Dharma and wisdom; therefore do you hear. In ancient times, Brahmā sprang from the navel-lotus of Viṣṇu and created the world. This is well-known in every Purāṇa. That self born, all-powerful, all-knowing, the Doer of all, the Universal Soul practised Tapas in ancient times for Ayuta (ten thousand) years. By virtue of that Tapas, He got special powers to create the world. He meditated on the Auspicious Mother, and getting from Her the excellent powers, He created first the mind-born sons, all endowed with auspicious signs.

मतीश्व: प्रयिततक्षेपाभवल्छिरूकर्गी 11 13 11

तस्य पुत्रोत्त्रिविखाताः: कर्णय: सर्वेस्मात: ।

Book VII Chapter II

Of them, Marici became well known in this act of creation. His son Kaśyapa was respected by all and he was of great celebrity.

यदोदरील तत्यासन्यार्यां द्वसुस्ता: किल 114 14

देवा: सर्वे समुदाया दैत्य द्वक्षा प्रवाना: 1

पशव: पक्षिणश्व तस्मात्यस्थितु काययपी ॥ 15 ॥

देवानां प्रधितः सूर्यां विश्वस्वाजाम तस्य तु ।

He had thirteen views, all daughters of Dakṣa Prajāpati. The Devas, Daityas, Yakṣas Pannagas, beasts and birds all sprang from him. Therefore this creation is called the Kāśyapī creation. Amongst the Devas, the Sun is specially famous; his other name is Vivasvān.

तस्य पुत्र: स विख्यातो वैवस्यतमनुर्पे: ॥ 16 ॥

तस्य पुत्रतदीक्ष्वाकु: सूर्यवेगिविदयर्न: ।

नवाभवनुस्तासस्य मनोरिद्वाकुपूर्वजाः ॥ 17 ॥

तेषां नामानि राजेन्द्र शृष्णुधैकमन्ता: पुनः ।

इक्ष्वाकुर्व ताभाग्यो धूर: शयातिरेव च ॥ 18 ॥

नरिथन्तस्था प्रश्रुर्जगी विद्धु सपना: ।

कलष्ठ पृषध्छ नवैते मानवा: स्मृता: ॥ 19 ॥

इक्ष्वाकुस्तु मनो: पुत्र: प्रयम: संमाजवात ।

त्यय पूजराते चारीज्ञेछो विकूक्षितार्यवान् ॥ 20 ॥

नवानां वंशवित्तरां संक्षेपण निशापाय ।

ग्रूरणां मनुपुवाणां मनोरंतरजन्मान् ॥ 21 ॥

नाभागस्य तु पुत्रोभूद्वंशीयः प्रतपावान् ।

His son was named Vaivasvat Manu; he was a famous king. Besides, Manu had nine more sons. Iksvāku was the eldest. Their names are :(1) Nābhāga, (2) Dhṛṣṭa, (3) Śaryāti, (4) Narisyanta, (5) Prāṃśu, (6) Nrga, (7) Diṣṭa, (8) Karūsa, and (9) Rṣadhara. Iksyāku, the son of Manu was born first. He had one hundred sons, and Vikukṣi was the wise and the eldest of these. I am now narrating how the nine sons, born afterwards of Manu, spread their families. Ambarīṣa was the son of Nābhaṇa; he was very truthful, powerful, and religious.

धर्मः स्वसंधः प्रजापानन्तनतः ॥ 22 ॥

धृत्यु धर्धकं क्षत्र बह्यभूतमजायत ।

स्रैमागकातं स्मयबह्याकर्मेत तथा ॥ 23 ॥

He always governed his subject justly. Dhārṣṭaka

was the son of Dhṛṣṭa; though he was a Kṣaṭtriya, he attained to Brāhmanaphood. He was naturally weak in fighting; always he was engaged in the works relating to the Brāhmanas.

गर्धातैतलनयक्षापूवतनों नाम विश्रुतः ।

सुकन्या च तथा पुत्री कप्लतावणयसंपुता ॥ 24 ॥

च्चवनाय सुता दत्ता राज्ञाधंाय सुन्दी ।

मुनि: सुतोच्नो जाततस्वा: शीतनुर्णेन ह् ॥ 25 ॥

विद्धितो रविपुत्राध्यामश्रिध्यात्मिन त श्रुतम् ।

Ānarta was the well known son and Sukanyā was the beautiful daughter of Saryāti. The King Saryāti gave his beautiful daughter in marriage to the blind Cyavana Rsi; but the Rsi, though blind, got his beautiful eyes again by the good character of the daughter. We heard that the Asvins, the Twins, the sons of the Sun, gave him back his eyesight.

जनमेव ज्ञवा

संदेहोर्ड महान्द्रकृ-काव्यायो कवित्तस्या ॥ 26 ॥

यज्ञात मुनेयेऊन्याद यदा युयी सुलोचना ।

कृकूपा गुणहीना वा नारी लक्षणवर्जिता ॥ 27 ॥

पुत्री यदा भवेत्रजा ततंधाय प्रवच्छति ।

जात्वार्धं सुमुखी कस्माहतवाव्युपसतमः ॥ 28 ॥

कारणं बृहि मे बहान्नुगाढोरित्य सर्वदा ।

Janamejaya said: O Brāhmaṇa! How is it that the King Śaryāti married his lovely-eyed daughter Sukanyā to the blind Cyavana Muni? I have got a great doubt on this point. The King gives his daughter in marriage to a blind person, if she be deformed, ill-qualified, or void of female signs. But the daughter, in this case was beautiful. How then Śaryāti, the Chief of Kings, gave over his daughter, knowing that the Rṣi was blind? O Brāhmaṇa! I am always an object of favour to you; so explain to me the cause of it.

सूत उवाच

इति राजा चकः शुत्वा परीक्षितसुनस्य वै ॥ 29 ॥

द्वैपायन: प्रसजात्या तमुवाच हरिसज्ञ ।

Sūta said: Glad to hear these words of Janamejaya, the Muni Dvaippāyna smilingly said:

<table><tr><td>व्यास उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>वैवस्वतसुतः श्रीमाङ्गधातिनिधा माधिवः ॥ 30 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>तस्य क्लीणां सहस्वाणि चत्वार्यसन्यप्रिक्राः।</td></tr><tr><td>राजपुच्यः सरूपाधु सर्वेलक्षणसयुतः ॥ 31 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>पत्थः प्रेमयुतः सर्वः प्रिया रातः सुर्मंततः ॥</td></tr><tr><td>Saryāti, the son of Vaivasvata, had four thousand married wives. All of them were endowed with auspicious signs and beautiful, all of them were daughters of kings. They all were very obliging and dear to their husbands.</td></tr><tr><td>एका पूत्री तु तासां वै सुकन्या नाम सुन्दरी ॥ 32 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>चितुः प्रिया च मातृणां सर्वांसां चाह्हासिंत ।</td></tr></table>

But, out of all of these, the King had only one daughter exceptionally lovely and beautiful. The father and all the mothers loved exceedingly that sweet-smiling daughter.

<table><tr><td>नगगातिन्हेरुप्रभूतरो</td><td>मानससधिभभ</td><td>॥ 33 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>ब्रद्रासोपानमर्गे च स्वच्छयानीयपृत्तिम् ।</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

Not very far off from the city, there was a beautiful lake of clear waters, like the Mānasasarovara lake. A Ghāt way (steps made of stones) descended into the lake.

<table><tr><td>हंसकारंखवाकीर्णं चक्रवाकोपोर्णिभत् ॥ 34 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>दात्यूढ़सारसाकीर्णं सरपेध्वीर्णाणतून् ।</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Swans Kārandavas, Cakravākas, Datyūhas,, Sārasas and other birds used to play on its waters. पछधा कमलोपेतं चछ्रीकासुसंवित्न ।। 35 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>पार्थंतछ द्रुमाकीर्णं वेहितं पादपैः सृषेः ।</td></tr><tr><td>सलैलसामलै: सरलै: पुरागागोकमधिडन् ।। 36 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>वटाधव्यकंदोंढे कदलीखण्डराजित्न ।</td></tr><tr><td>जंबीरैर्बिंजपूरेंछ खड़ैरैः पनसैलथा ।। 37 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>कमुकैन्निकेलैंकु केतकैः कांचनुदैः ।</td></tr><tr><td>युधिकाजालकें: गुधे: संवृंत मलिलकाणीः ।। 38 ॥</td></tr></table>

Five varieties of lotuses were there in full bloom, bees were humming there all around. Various trees, Śala, Tāmāla, Sarala, Punnāgas, Aśokas, Banyans, Peepuls, Kadambas, rows of banana trees, Jambiras, dates, Panasas, betelnut tree, cocoanut trees, Ketakas, Kāñcanas, and other various beautiful trees encircled round the lake. Within

these, the white Yāthikās, Mallikās, and other creepers and shrubs were seen beautifying the scenery.

जंब्याप्रतिनिणीभिश्र करंजकुटजायुत् ।

पलाशनिवल्यदिविवल्यानलकमपिडत् ॥ 39 ॥

बभूव वोकिलावा: केकास्वनिवारजित्न् ।

तस्मारीय गुधे देरेगे पादपानां गणायुते ॥ 40 ॥

भागवंश्यवन: शान्ततावास: संश्विता मुनि: ।

Especially there were, amongst them, jack trees, mango trees, tamarind trees, Karanjas, Kutakas, Palāsas, Neem trees, Khadiras, Bel trees, and Āmalaki trees; and peacocks were sounding their notes, cuckoos were cooing their beautiful voices. Close to that place, there was, in a sacred grove covered over by trees, staying Cyavana Muni, the Bhrgu's son, of a tranquilled mind, and the chief of the ascetics.

जालाउसी विजर्भ स्थान तपसेने समाहितः ॥ 4 1 ॥

कृत्वा दुवासने मौनमाधाय जित्नातकतः ।

इन्द्रियाणि च संयम्य त्यक्ताहारतपोनिधि: ॥ 4 2 ॥

जलपानादिरितो ध्यायज्ञाने पराधिकम् ।

सवल्मीको3पवद्धजल्लताभि: परिवहितः ॥ 4 3 ॥

Thinking the place lovely and free from any obstacles, the Muni took his firm seat there and, collecting all his thought within himself, took the vow of non-speaking and controlling his breath became engaged in practising tapasyā. Restraining his senses and foregoing eating and drinking, that Muni constantly meditated on Bhagavatī of the nature of Sat, Cit and Ānanda. O King! While he was thus meditating, the anthill grew up round and covered his body and nice creepers covered that also all round.

कालेन महता राजनसामाकर्णी चिपीलिके:।

तथा स संवृतो धीमानुत्पण्ड इव सर्वतैः ॥ 44 ॥

कदाचित्म स्मीपालः कामिनीगणसंवृतः ।

आजगाम सरी राजन्वहतुमिदमुतामम् ॥ 45 ॥

O King! Long intervals passed away and it was covered over with ants; so much so that that intelligent Muni was covered entirely and looked

Book VII Chapter II

like a heap of earth. O King! Once the King Śaryāti wanted to play in an artificial wilderness and came there to the lake with his wives.

श्यापिति: सुन्दरीवृदंसयुतः सलिलेउमले ।

क्रीडासनो महीपालो बधूव कमलाकरे ॥ 46 ॥

सुकन्या वनमासाधा विजहार सखीवृता ।

सुमनांसि विधिन्यन्ती चझताना चझलोगम ॥ 47 ॥

Śaryāti became at once deeply engaged in playing on the clear waters of the lake, surrounded by the beautiful females. On the other hand, the quick beautiful daughter Sukanyā, picking up flowers here and there with her companions also began to play.

सर्वाभिरयासंयुक्ता राणचरणान्युपा ।

घंकमभागा वलकीच क्ववनस्य समादद्त् ॥ 48 ॥

Dressed in ornaments, Sukanyā walked to and fro; her anklets making a beautiful tinkling sound, till she came to the ant-hill of Cyavana Rsi.

क्रीडासभोपविष्ठा वरवीक्यास समीपतः ।

दर्शं गायस्य रथे वे ख्योते इव ज्योतिगे ॥ 49 ॥

किमेतादिति संचित्य समुद्रुं मनो दूधे ।

गृहीता कंटकं तीक्षा त्वसागण कृगोदरी ॥ 50 ॥

She sportingly sat close to that ant-hill and instantly saw a shining substance inside through that, like fireflies. “What is this?” She thought and wishing to take it, took a thorn and became very eager to prick it up.

सा बृद्धया मुनिना बाला समीपस्था कृतोद्धगा ।

विवारन्तो सुकेशान्ता मन्थध्येवेक कामिने ॥ 51 ॥

Slowly she went close to it and no sooner she got ready to prick it, than the Muni saw the beautiful, good-haired daughter as if to one's liking.

तां बीक्ष्य सुत्ती तत क्षामकण्ठसोपोर्दिति: ।

ताभाषावत कल्याणि किमेदोदिति भारणि : ॥ 52 ॥

The ascetic Bhārgava, seeing that auspicious nice lady with nice teeth, spoke out in a feeble voice: "What are you doing? O thin-bellied One! I am an ascetic; better go away from here.

दूरं गच्छ शिलालाक्ष तापसोर्शे घरानने ।

मा भिंदस्वाघ वर्षीक कणटकेन कृशोदिर ॥ 53 ॥

You have got such big-eyes, yet you do not see me. I therefore forbid you in your this attempt; do not pierce the anthill with a thorn.

तेनेदे प्रोष्ट्यमानापि सा धारस्य ने शुणोति वै ।

किमु खल्सिदमितुस्कृता निर्विधेत्यल लोचेन । ५४।।

Though prevented, the daughter could not hear his words and asking “what was that?” pierced his two-eyes with thorns.

दैवेन नोदिता भिन्चा जागम नृपकन्यका ।

क्रीड़नी शंकमाना स किं कृतं तु मवेति च ॥ 55 ॥

Thus impelled by Fate, the princess sportingly pierced his eyes; but she suspected and thought "What have I done?"

चुकीधि स तथा विद्वानेः परममन्युभान् ।

वेदनाध्वितिः कामं पतिरापं जगाम ह ॥ 56 ॥

गकृष्मूरनिरोधोमे भूलैमिकानां तु तक्षणात् ।

विशेषेण तु भपस्य सामानस्य समंततः ॥ 57 ॥

गजोष्ट्रतुगगाणां च सर्वेषां प्राणिनां तदा ।

ततो क्रूंदे गकृम्भुत्रे गर्शितिदुःवित्तोभवत् ॥ 58 ॥

Thus becoming afraid she returned from that spot. His two eyes being pricked, the great Muni exceedingly pained, became very wrathful; he incessantly gave vent to sorrows and remorse, being restless with pain. At that instant it happened that the king, ministers, soldiers, elephants, horses, camels, so much so that all the beings that were there, had all their evacuations (passing their urines and faeces) stopped. Seeing thus happened all on a sudden, the King Śaryāti was very much pained and became very anxious.

सैनिकः कथितं तस्ये शकुन्मूत्रनिरोधनम् ।

चिन्तयामास भूपालः कारणं दुःखसम्पदे ॥ 59 ॥

All the soldiers came to the King and informed him of the stoppage of their evacuations. The King thought over the cause why this had happened.

विश्विष्यह ततो राजा सैनिकान्यवजनासंखा ।

गृह्माग्य चिन्तातैः केनेदे दृष्टतु कृतम् ॥ 60 ॥

Cogitating thus, the King returned home. Becoming very much troubled with cares and anxieties, He asked his soldiers and kinsman “Who amongst you has done such an heinous act?”

<table><tr><td>सरसः पश्रिमे भागे वनमध्ये महातपाः ।</td></tr><tr><td>ध्यवनस्तापसरत्न तपश्रति दुक्षगम् ॥ 6 1 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>केनाययपकृतं तत्र तायसेग्रितस्मगभे ।</td></tr><tr><td>तस्मातीघा समुत्पत्रा सर्वेषामिति निश्रधः ॥ 6 2 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>तपोवृद्धस्य वृद्धस्य वरिष्ठस्य विगेषतः ।</td></tr><tr><td>केनाययपकृतं मन्ये भागवस्य महात्मनः ॥ 6 3 ॥</td></tr></table>

On the west side of the lake the Maharsi Cyavana is practising the great tapasyā in the midst of the forest; I think some one has done mischief to that king of ascetics, blazing like a fire; and therefore we are overcome with this disease. The high-souled aged son of Bhrgu has become specially proficient in his asceticism and has become supreme; I think someone must have injured him.

ज्ञातं वा यदि वाज्ञातं तस्यैंद फलमुत्तमम् ।

कैक्षु दुहैः कृतः तस्य हेतनं तापस्यस्य ह् १।64 ॥

Though I do not know who is that mischievous person that has shown him contempt or like that, this our state at present clearly shews that this is the fit punishment of that."

इति पृड़सामुक्ते सैनिका वेदनादिततः ।

मनोवाक्कायजितेन न विवोउपकृतं ववम् ॥ 65 ॥

इति श्रीमद्दैभीभागवते महापुरुषो सतमन्कन्धे

हितीयोउवायः 112 11

Hearing this, the soldiers said: None of us has committed any mischief by word, mind or body; we know this very well.

Here ends the Second Chapter of the Seventh Book on the piercing of the eyes of Cyavana

Muni in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam the Mahā

Purānam, of 18,000 verses by

Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER III

The King Śaryāti's Giving His Daughter in Marriage to Cyavana Muni

यास उवाच

इति पप्रच्छ तान्सर्वाज्ञा चिन्ताकुलतस्तथा ।

पर्यष्छ्छसूढ़ाद्री सामान ओरातनाउपि च ॥ १ ॥

पीड्वामानं जनं बीक्ष्य पितर्ते दुःखितं तथा ।

विश्चित्य शूलभेदे सा सुकान्या चेदमब्रवीत् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus the King, troubled with cares, asked his soldiers, in an angry mood. Next he asked his friends in sweet words. The princess, seeing his father and his soldiers sorrowful, thought of her piercing the two eyes of the Muni with a thorn and thus spoke to the King:

वने मया पित्तस्त वल्यीको क्रीभावृतः ।

क्रीड़ल्या सुद्वो दृष्टिश्छद्वयसमित्वः ॥ 3 ॥

"O Father! While sporting in that forest, I came to see a very hard anthill covered with creepers and shrubs wherein I found two holes.

तत्र खडोतद्वीर्णत्नोतीषी वीक्षिते मा ।

सूच्या विद्रे महाराज पुनः खडोतरांका ॥ 4 ॥

O King! Through those small openings, I saw the two shining things as if they were fireflies and thinking them so I pierced them with thorns.

जलविक्ना तदा सूची मया द्वाहा पितः किल ।

हाहेति च श्रुतः शब्दो मन्तो वल्पीकमध्यतः ॥ 5 ॥

तदाहं विक्सिता राजन्किमेति दर्शका ।

न जाने किं मया विद्धं तस्मिन्वल्यीकमण्डले ॥ 6 ॥

At this time a faint voice I heard coming from that anthill. “Oh! I am killed!” I then took out my thorns and found them wet with water. “What is this! I asked myself and was thunderstruck with fear; but I could not know that what I pierced in that anthill.”

राजा शुत्वा तु शयिति: सुकन्यावचनं मूढु ।

मुनेतद्धोर्न नात्वा वर्चीक किंग्रमप्यभात् ॥ ७ ॥

Hearing these gentle words of his daughter, the King Śaryāti thought that that act had no doubt insulted the Muni and went at once to the anthill.

ततापयतपोकूद्ध च्यवनं दुःखतं धूगम् ।

स्कोटयामास वर्चीक मुनिवेदाबृतं ततः ॥ ८ ॥

He broke the anthill that covered the Muni and saw the suffering Cyavana aged in practising Tapasyā, very much in pain.

Book VII Chapter III

प्रणाथ्य देडवब्दूमौ राजा तं भागर्वं प्रति ।

तृधाव विनयोपेतस्मुवाच कृताजुस्ति: ॥ 9 ॥

पुर्या मम महाभाग क्रीडीर्या दुकृतं कृतम् ।

अज्ञानाढूलया बढ़ान्हुत तन्धनुर्महींस ॥ 10 ॥

The King prostrated flat before the Muni and then with folded hands, praised him with hymns and humbly said to him: "O Intelligent One! My daughter has done this wrong act while sporting; therefore O high-souled One! What she had done unknowingly, do you forgive out of your own high-heartedness and liberality.

अकोधिता ही मुनयो घर्वतीति मया श्रुन्ति ।

तस्माचमयि बालायाः क्षेतुमहींस संग्रन्न ॥ 11 ॥

I have heard that the ascetics are always void of anger; therefore now you have to forgive this daughter of the offence and thus shew your kindness.

व्यास उवाच

इति शुत्वा वचस्तस्य च्यवनो वाक्यमब्रवीत् ।

विन्योपानंतं चूद्वा राजानं दुःखितं घृष्णम् ॥ १२ ॥

Vyāsa said: The Maharṣi Çyavana, hearing thus the King's words and specially seeing his humble and distre:sed nature, said:

च्यवन उवाच

राजत्राई क्वार्चिद्धे क्रोपि मेधोभयपवी ।

न ययाजदीथ ग्रणतस्तं दुहिता पीडने कृते ॥ १३ ॥

नैसे पीडा समुदायम मम चाह निरागरास: ।

तेन पापे न जानामि दुःखितस्तं महीपते ॥ १४ ॥

“O King! I never was angry a bit; your daughter had pained me; yet I am not angry and have not cast on her any curse; you better see, that I am innocent; much pain is felt by me due to my eyes being pricked. O King! It seems that you are sorry and troubled for that sin.

अपराधं परं कृत्वा देवीभकस्य को जनः ।

सुखं लभेत यदपि भवेत्नात शिवः स्वयम् ॥ 15 ॥

Who can acquire happiness in this world who has committed a great offence to a Bhakta of the Devi, in spite he gets even Śiva as his Protector.

कि कारोम महीपाल नेत्रहीनो जरावुतः ।

अन्यस्य परिचर्यां चकः कर्तिष्यात पार्थिवः ॥ 16 ॥

O King! On the one hand, I am now worn out by old age, and then, on the other hand, I am deprived of my eyes; what shall be now my means? Please say, who will take care of the blind man?

राजोवाच

सेवका बहूवः सेवां कर्तिथित त्वातिशम्भ ।

क्षमस्व पुनिराष्ट्रैल स्वल्पक्रोषा हि तपासः ॥ 17 ॥

The King said: “O Muni! The anger of the ascetics is transient; you are in practice of your tapasyā; so your anger is a thing of impossibility. So kindly forgive the offence of the daughter. I have got many persons who will incessantly take care of you.”

च्यन्त उवाच

अन्धोर्ह निर्जनी राजसंतपतर्णुं कक्ष क्षमः ।

त्वदीयः सेवकाः किं के तक्रिथन्ति मम प्रिग्यम् ॥ 18 ॥

Cyavana said: "O King! There is none of my relations with me; then I am now made blind; how shall I go on with my tapasya? I do not think that your servants will take care of me.

क्षमाप्यसि चैनம் त्वं कुं कुं मे वचनं नृप ।

देहि मे परिचर्याँई कन्यां कलमलोचनम् ॥ 19 ॥

तुध्ये-उनया महाराज युथा तव महानते ।

करिष्यामि पत्थहांह सा मे सेवां करिष्यति ॥ 20 ॥

O King! If you think it your duty to please me, then do my word, give me your lotus-eyed daughter to serve me and take care of me. O King! I will be very glad if I acquire your daughter; she will serve me when I will be engaged in my tapasyā.

एवं कुते सुखं मे स्थानच वैव भविष्यति ।

सन्तुई ययि राजेन्र सैनिकानां न संशयः ॥ 21 ॥

विश्वित्व मनसा भूप कन्यातानं समाचर ।

न चात्र दूषणं किंचितापासोउहं यतन्ततः ॥ 22 ॥

O King! This, if observed, will satisfy me and all the troubles that are now with you and your army will no doubt disappear. O King! Think and grant me your daughter to me, you will not incur any sin nor any fault.

व्यास उवाच

श्यातिवर्चनं शुत्वा मुनेश्रितातुरोःपवत् ।

न दास्येउपयवा दास्ये किंचिज्ञोवाच भात । २३ ॥

Vyāsa said: O Bharata! Hearing thus the Muni's words, the King Śaryāti was immersed in care and could not say anything whether he would or would not give over his daughter to him.

कवेमंधाय वृद्धाय कुरूपाय सुतामिमान् ।

देवक-योगमां दत्ता सुखी स्यामानस्पमवान् ॥ 24 ॥

The King thought, “my daughter is very fair like a Devakanyā and this Muni is aged, ugly and specially he is blind; how then can I be happy if I give over my daughter to him.

को वात्मन: सुखार्थीय पुत्रा: संसारतं सुखम् ।

इतेतैल्यमिति: पाणो जानत्रिय शुभाणुभम् ॥ 25 ॥

Who is there so stupid and vicious that knowing his good and bad, he for his own selfish happiness wants to deprived his beautiful daughter of the enjoyments of her married life.

प्राय सा च्यवनं सुधूः पञ्जवाणगरित्ति ।

अर्थं वृद्धे पति प्राय कं कलं नयिध्यति ॥ 26 ॥

How will that fair eyebrowed daughter of mine pass her days happily in the company of this aged Muni when she will be overpowered by passion.

यौवने दुर्जेय: कामो विशेषेण सुरूपया ।

आमतुल्यं पति प्राय किंयु वृद्धं विलोचनम् ॥ 27 ॥

गौतमं तापसं प्राय रूपवीवनसंरुता ।

अहल्या वासेवेनाणु वक्षिता वरवर्गिनी ॥ 28 ॥

The more so when the young beautiful ladies are not able to conquer their passions though possessed of husbands of their own standard and liking, how then can my daughter conquer her passion on getting this old blind husband! The exquisitely beautiful Ahalyā married Gautama; but, seeing the youthful beauty of that lovely lady, Indra deceived her and took away her chastity.

ग्राता च पतिता पशुज्ञाता धर्मविपर्यम् ।

तस्माद्वतुर्ने चे दुःखं न द्वाद्वि सुकंत्यकम् ॥ २९ ॥

Till at last, her husband Gautama finding that action contrary to Dharma, cursed him. Now

through the severe curse of that Brahmarṣi many troubles many arise; so I cannot in any case give my daughter Sukanyā over to him.

इति संचित्य श्रान्तिविभ्ना: स्वगृहं यथो ।

सचिवांश समादाय मन्चे क्रोस्तिश्वितः ॥ ३० ॥

Thus thinking and absent-minded the King went back to his home and, being very distressed, called his ministers to form a council.

भो मिन्णो बुवल्पा कि कत्वेलं मायापुना ।

पुत्री देवारण विधाय भोक्त्यं चुःखेवा न ॥ ३१ ॥

विचारायधं मिलिता हितं स्यामय वे कवम् ।

O Ministers! What am I to do now? Is it advisable to give over my daughter to the Muni? Or is it better to suffer these pains? Judge and say what is the best course for me."

मन्त्रिण कचुः

किं बुभोर्यसम्भृतराज संकंठेगतिनुतारये ॥ ३२ ॥

दुर्भागाय सुकन्यैषा कर्थे देवासितमुन्नरी ।

The Ministers said: “O King! What shall we say in this critical juncture? How can you bestow your exceedingly beautiful daughter to that ugly unfortunate ascetic?

व्यास उवाच

तदा चिन्ताकुलं वीध्य पितरं मज्जान्सादा ॥ 33 ॥

सुकन्या त्वितिगतं जाला प्रहस्येदमुवाच ह ।

चितः कसम्बुद्वानचं चितन्याकृतितेज्व्य: ॥ 34 ॥

मकृतते कु:खसिंविनो विषणवादनोअ सि ।

Dvaippāyna said: At this moment, seeing her father and ministers troubled very much with care, Sukanyā understood at once everything by signs and hints; she then smilingly said to her dear father: "O Father! Why are you looking so sad with cares? Perhaps you are very much troubled and sad for me.

अहं गाला मुनिं तत्र समाश्रास्य भयार्दितम् ॥ 35 ॥

करिष्याम प्रसत्र तमात्यदानेन वे सितः ।

इति राजा वचः शुत्वा भाषितं वत्कुकन्याय ॥ 36 ॥

तामुवाय प्रसताला सचिवानां च श्रवताम् ।

O Father! I have pained that Muni; so I will go

Book VII Chapter III

and console him; what more than this that I will give up myself at his feet and please him." Hearing these words of Sukanyā, the King spoke to her very gladly before all the ministers.

कयं पुत्र त्वमंध्य परिचर्यं चनेउक्ता ॥ 37 ॥

क्रियसि जरातंस्य क्रोधनस्य विशेषतः ।

“O Daughter! The Cyavana Muni is blind, aged and of a worn figure, especially of a very irritable temper; and you are a mere girl; how will you be able to serve him in that dreadful forest?

कवमन्याय चानेन रूपेण रतिस्त्रिभाण् ॥ 38 ॥

तद्योय वरया प्रस्तद्वाहा सुखवांछया ।

You are like Rati in beauty and loveliness; how can I bestow my daughter to that aged worn out, blind Muni for my own pleasure!

पिता पूर्वी प्रदातव्या वयोज्ञानितेवावता च ॥ 39 ॥

धनधान्यसमुद्धाय नान्धाय कदाचन ।

The father marries the daughter to him who has got relations, who is a proper age, strong, who has got unequalled grains and wealth, gems and jewels; never to a man void of wealth.

व्व ते रूप विशालाक्षि व्वसौं वृद्धो वनेचरः ॥ 40 ॥

कयं देवा मया पुत्री तस्मै चातिवराय च ।

O broad-eyed One! You are exquisitely handsome; and that ascetic is very old; see what an amount of difference lies between you two. The Muni, moreover, has past his marriageable age; so how can I give over my daughter.

उठजे निवर्ते वासो यस्य नित्यं मनोहरे ॥ 4 1 ॥

कमधेनुवगवश्रिक कल्पनीये मय तव ।

O Lotus-eyed! You always dwell in beautiful places; how can I now make you dwell for ever in thatched huts?

मरणों में वरे प्राणं सैनिकानां तबैव च ॥ 4 2 ॥

न ते प्रदामांधाय रोचते पिकभापिण ।

O Cuckoo voiced one! Rather will I and my soldiers die than to bestow you to that blind husband.

भवितयं भवलेव एंयै नैव त्याज्यम्हम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

सुविधा भव सुश्रेणि न दास्येऊन्याकर्हिंचित ।

Let whatever come it may, I will never lose patience; therefore, O One of good hips! Be quiet. I will never give you to that blind man.

राज्यं त्रिष्ठुं वा यातु दैहेडेयं च तदीये मे ॥ 44 ॥

न त्वां दास्यास्छई तम्पै नेह्रानीय बालिके ।

O my Daughter! I do'nt care a straw whether my kingdom and my body live or die, but I will never be able to give you over to the ascetic."

सुकन्या तं तदा प्राह श्रुत्वा तद्धचनं पितुः ॥ 45 ॥

प्रसवद्वतानीत स्नेहायुक्तिमितं वचः ।

Hearing thus the father's words, Sukanyā began to speak with a glad face the following sweet and gentle words:

सुकन्योवाच

न मे चिंता पित: कार्यं दौड़े मां मुनयेरुध्न । ४ ४ ६ ॥

सुखं भवतुं सर्वैषां लोकानां मकृतेन है ।

संवशियाभि सन्तुषा पति परमपावनम् ॥ ४७ ॥

भक्न्या परमया चाधि वृद्धि च विजने वने ।

सतीधार्यात चाहं चार्थिमा सुसंमतम् ॥ ४८ ॥

न भोगेच्छाउसि ने तात सत्यर्थ चित्तं मानाव ।

"O Father! Do not trouble yourself for nothing with cares on my account. Give me over now to that best of Munis; then all the persons will be happy, no doubt. I will derive my intense pleasure there in that dense forest by serving with intense devotion my old husband, who is very holy. I have got not the least inclination towards these worldly enjoyments which are the sources of all troubles for nothing. My heart is now quiet. Therefore, O Father! I will become a chaste wife to him and act according to his liking."

यास उवाच

तब्धुर्वा भाषितं तस्य मन्निणो विस्तयं गताः।। ४ १ ॥

राजा च परमप्रीतो जगाम मुनिसप्रिधो ।

गत्वा प्रणय्य शिरसा तमुवाच तोत्पधन् ॥ 50 ॥

स्वामिन्यूहाण पूर्वी मे सेवार्थ विंधितद्विषो ।

इन्युक्त्वाप्रमै ददी पूर्णी विवाहविद्धिना नृपः ॥ 51 ॥

प्रतिगृहा मुनिनः कन्या प्रसजो भागवीक्षणवत् ।

पारिवह्नं न जग्रह दीयमानं नृपेण ह ॥ 52 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

कन्यामेवाग्रहीत्कामं परिचरार्थमात्मनः । प्रसजेडसिमनुनी जातं सैरिकतनां सुखं तदा । ५३ ।। राजश्व परमाहुादः संज्ञातस्तक्षणाद्वि । दत्त्वा पुत्रीं यदा राजा गमनाय गृहं प्रति ॥ ५४ ।। मर्ति चकात तन्वंनी तदोवाच नृपं सुता ।

Vyāsa said: O King! The Ministers were greatly amazed on hearing these words and the King also become greatly pleased and took her to the presence of the Muni. Going before him, he bowed down to the Muni and said: "O Lord! Please accept duly this daughter for your Sevā." Thus saying, the King betrothed his daughter to him according to rules. Cyavana Muni also become very glad to receive her. The Muni took the daughter willingly for his Sevā but refused other dowries that the King presented. Thus the Muni became pleased; immediately the soldiers began to evacuate and were very glad. Seeing this, the King's heart became filled with joy. When the King, thus finishing the betrothal ceremony of his daughter wanted to return home, the thin bodied princess then told her father:

सुकन्योवाच

गृहाण मम वासांसि प्रपणानि च मे चितः ॥ 55 ॥

बल्कलं परिधानाय प्रपच्छाजिनमुन्तमम् ।

वेयं तु मुनिपत्नीना कृत्वा तपसि सेवनम् ॥ 56 ॥

करिष्यामि तथा तात यथा ते कौतिंरच्छुता ।

भविष्याति भुवः पुढ़े तथा स्वर्ग साताले ॥ 57 ॥

परतोककरुप्यायहं चरिष्यामि दिवानिरगम् ।

दरवांधाय च बुद्धाय सु-तरीं युवती तु माम् ॥ 58 ॥

चिन्ता त्वया न कर्तव्य शनिनाशसमुद्धता ।

अकन्यती वसिष्ठस्य धर्मपनी यथा भृति ॥ 59 ॥

तर्वेहाई भविष्यामि नात्र कार्या विचारणा ।

Sukanyā said: “O Father! Take away all my ornaments and clothing and give me for my use an excellent dear skin and one bark. O Father! I will dress myself like the wives of Munis and serve my husband in such a way as will bring to you the unparalleled undying fame in Heaven, Earth and

the Nether regions; also I will serve my husband's feet so that I can derive the highest happiness in the next world. I am now full of youth, especially beautiful; do not think a bit that as I am wedded to an aged ascetic, that my character will be spoilt. As Vasiṣṭha's wife Arundhati has attained celebrity in this world, so also I will attain success; there is no doubt in this.

अनसूया यथा साखी भार्यांत्रेः प्राणिता भृति ॥ 60 ॥

तर्वेहाई भविष्यामि पुत्री कर्जितकी ततः ।

सुकन्यावचनं श्रुत्वा राजा परमधर्मवित् ॥ 61 ॥

दत्वास्त्रिनं करोदणु वीक्ष्य तां चाहसृहासीनि ।

त्यलव्या भूषणवासांसि मुनिवेषधरां सुतान् ॥ 62 ॥

विवर्णवदनो भूत्वा स्थिततरत्रेव पार्थिवः ।

राज्ञ: सर्वी: सुतां दृष्टवा वर्कलाजिनधारिणीम् ॥ 63 ।

रूकदृष्टिशणोकांतत विमाना इवाधवन्त ।

तामापुरच्छा महीपालो मन्द्रिभि: परिवारितः ।

ययी स्वनगरं राजन्मुक्ता पूर्णे शुच्ञार्थिताम् ॥ 64 ॥

इति श्रीमदैवीभागवते महापुराणे सत्यमकन्धे

तृतीयोउधायः 11 3 11

As the chaste wife Anasūyā of Maharṣi Attri has become widely known in this world so will I be known also and establish your fame. The exceedingly religious King, hearing all these words of Sukanyā, gave her decr skin and all other articles wanted. The King could not help weeping, when he saw that his daughter had dressed herself like the daughter of a Muni. He stood fixed, very sad, on that very spot. All the queens were exceedingly filled with sorrow to see the daughter dressed in bark and deer-skin. Their hearts quivered and they began to weep. O King! Then the King Śaryāti bade goodbye to the Muni, leaving there his daughter. He went with a grievous heart and returned to his own city, accompanied by the ministers.

Here ends the Third Chapter of the Seventh Book on the bestowing of the daughter of the King Śaryāti to the Cyavana Muni in Śrimaddevitbhāgavatam, the Mahā Purāṇam of 18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsā.

Book VII Chapter IV

CHAPTER IV

On Sukanyā and the Aśvīns

व्यास उवाच

गते राजनि सा बाला पतिसेवापरायण।

बधूव च तधारुनीर्ना सेवेधे धर्मतसरा ॥ 1 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the King Śaryāti departed, that virtuous lady devoted her time in serving her husband, and the Fire.

फलान्यादाय स्वानूनि मूलानि विविधानि च ।

ददौ सा मुनये बाला परिसेवापरायण ॥ 2 ॥

पर्ति तपोदकेनागृ स्वायधित्वा मृगत्वचा ।

परिवेश्य शुभायां तु तुच्यां स्थापितवल्यपि ॥ 3 ॥

तितान् यवकुशगानेय पौकल्य कमण्डतुम् ।

त्युवाच तिवकर्म कुरुच मुनिसत्ता ॥ 4 ॥

She gave to the Muni for his food various delicious roots and fruits. She made him bathe with warm water; then making him put on the deer skin, she made him sit on the Kuśāsana. (Seat made of the Kuśa grass.) Next she used to place in his front Kuśa, Til and Kamandalu and speak to him “O best of Munis! You are now to perform your daily rites (Nitya Karma).”

तमुत्थाय करे कृत्वा समापते नियतकर्भिन ।

दूर्यां वा संस्ते बाला घतरिं संन्येशशत् ॥ 5 ॥

When the Nitya Karma was finished, the lady used to catch him by his hand and make him sit on another seat.

पशुतादानीय पव्वानि फलानि च नृपात्मज ।

भोजयामास च्यवनं नीवारात्रं सुसंस्कृत् ॥ 6 ॥

Next the princess brought fresh ripe fruits and cooked rice, grown without cultivation and gave to the Muni for his food.

सुक्नवंत पति तृणत दुत्वा3प्रमन्मादात् ।

पश्राच्च पूरं प्रयाणि दटौ चारतसंयुता ॥ 7 ॥

When the husband finished his meals, she gave him devotedly water for cleansing his mouth; then washing his hands and feet gave him the betelnuts and pān leaves.

गृहीतमुखवासं तं संवेश्य च शुभासने ।

गृहीत्नाजी शरीरस्य चकार साधनं ततः ॥ 8 ॥

Next he made him sit on an excellent seat, and with his permission, performed her own bodily purifications.

फलाहांर स्वयं कृत्वा पुर्जनांचा च सश्रिधो ।

प्रोवाच प्रणयोपेता किमाजापयसे प्रभो ॥ 9 ॥

She then, used to eat the remnants, fruits and roots of the dishes of her husband; and coming next to her husband addressed him affectionately “O Lord! Order me what can I do now for you?

पादस्यहान्ते नेउष्ठ क्रोगेय यदि मन्यसे ।

एवं सेवापान नित्यं कपूर पतितलग्न ॥ 10 ॥

If you like, I may champoo your legs and feet"; thus every day that princess devotedly spent her time in serving her husband.

सार्य होमावसाने सा फलान्याह्य सुन्दरी ।

अर्पयामास मुनवे स्वाजून च पूरून्च ॥ 11 ॥

At evening when the Homa ceremony was finished, she collecting delicious and soft fruits presented to him to eat.

ततः शेभाणि खुचुसे प्रेमयुक्ता तद्वान्या ।

सूयशणितरणं कृत्ता शाययामास तं पूदा ॥ 12 ॥

With his permission she, then, ate that were left of the fruits; next she prepared bedding soft to the touch and gladly made him sleep on it.

सुते सुखं प्रिये कान्ता पादसंहानं तदा ।

चकार पृच्छती धर्म कुतस्थाणं कुशोदरी ॥ 13 ॥

When his dear husband thus laid himself on the bed, she champooed his feet and legs and in the interval, asked him about the religious duties of the chaste wives of the family.

पादसंहानं कृत्वा निशि शफित्सरायण ।

निद्रितं च मुनि नात्वा सुवाया चरणातिके ॥ 14 ॥

गुची प्रतिहितं बीक्ष्य तालवृतेन भामिति ।

कुवाणी शैनितं वायुं सिवेषे स्वपति सदा ॥ 15 ॥

Śrimaddevithāgavatam

When the Muni fell asleep in the night, she devotedly laid herself close to his feet and slept. During the summer season when her husband was perspiring, the lady used to fan him with cool breeze. She took off his troubles and thus served her husband.

हैमंते कामहंभारं कृत्वादिभिजलनं पुरः ।

स्थापयिता तथाउपृच्छसुखं तेउसीति चासकृत् ॥ 16

In the cold season, she collected firewood and lit them before him and used to ask him frequently "O Muni! Are you feeling pleasure in this?"

बाहो मुहूर्ते चोलाया जलं पात्र च मृतिकाम् ।

समर्पयित्वा शोचार्थी समुत्थाय पति क्रिया ॥ 17 ॥

स्थानादूरे च संस्थाय दूरं नाला स्थितभक्ति ।

कृतग्रेषचं पति ज्ञाला गत्या जग्राह तं पुनः ॥ 18 ॥

That lady, dear to her husband, used to get up from her bed in the Brāhma Muhūrta before Sunrise, next made her husband get up and took him some short way off and there made him sit for calling on nature. She kept ready water and earth and stood in readiness at a suitable distance apart.

आनीयाममव्यगा चोपवेश्यासने रुषे ।

भुज्जलाथा च श्रबाल्प पादास्य यथाविधि ॥ १९ ॥

दत्वाचमपात्रं तु दन्तधावनमाहत् ।

सत्यं दलकाई च यथोक्ते नृपनिद्वनी ॥ २० ॥

चक्राशोषु जलं शूढ़े समानीते सुपावनम् ।

स्नानार्थ जलमाहूलय पघछ्छ प्रणायचिता ॥ २१ ॥

किमाजापयसे बढ़ान्तुतं थै दन्तधावनम् ।

उथोटके सुसमयं कुक सन्तां समन्वकम् ॥ २२ ॥

Knowing that he had finished his calls for nature, she took him back to the Asrama and washed his hands and feet with water duly making him sit on a convenient Asana. She then gave to him the pot for Acamana and then began to collect fuel. She used to bring pure clear water and made it hot; then she asked her husband fondly "O Husband! Have you finished cleaning your teeth? Warm water is ready; may I bring it to you? You better bathe with that, uttering your Mantrams.

वर्तते होमकालोउयं सन्यापा पूर्वां प्रवर्तते ।

विधिवघद्वानं कृत्वा देवतापुजनं कृफ ॥ 23 ॥

एवं कन्या पर्ति लक्ष्या तप्तविशमर्पमिदिता ।

नित्यं पर्यंचराग्रीत्या तपसा नियमेन च ॥ 24 ॥

The time is now for performing the morning Sandhyā and for performing Homa. Do the Homa regularly and worship the Devas." The princess, whose nature was pure and clean as anything, kept herself engaged daily in serving her husband, Cyavana Muni, with perfect gladness, austerity, and observing all the rules duly.

अन्नीनामितीधीनं च गुरूप्यां कुर्वीत सदा ।

आराध्यामास मुदा च्यवनं सा शुभानना ॥ 25 ॥

Thus that beautiful-paced princess worshipped gladly Cyavana Muni, serving Fire and the guests daily.

कर्मिश्रिक्षय काले तु रविज्ञाविशान्यभी ।

च्यवनत्याभाप्यायो क्रीघानी समाराती ॥ 26 ॥

Then, once, on an occasion, the Asvin twins, the sons of Sūrya came sporting and at their pleasure, close to the hermitage of the Cyavana Muni.

जले सत्ताव तु तां कन्यां निवृत्ता स्वाध्यमं प्रति ।

गच्छतीं चारुकसर्वीं रविवुगवायपर्यतम् ॥ 27 ॥

At that time the princess, beautiful in all respects, was returning home after her bath in a pure clear stream and came to the sight of the two Aśvins.

तां नुह्या देकक्यां तां गत्वा चांतिकामदात् ।

कचतुः समधिद्वून नास्यावलियोगेहिति ॥ 28 ॥

Being enchanted by her extraordinary lovely beauty, they thought she might be a Deva Kanyā, quickly went to her and fondly questioned her: "O slow moving One like an elephant! Look! We are the sons of Devas; we have come to you to ask some question.

क्षणं तिड् वरागेहे प्रडुं त्वां गजगामिनि ।

आगां देवसुती प्राप्ती बृहि सत्यं शुचिसिते ॥ 29 ॥

O Excellent One! Wait for a moment; we request thus to you. O Sweet-smiling One! Please answer our questions truly and properly."

Book VII Chapter IV

पूरी कसय पति: कसे के कथमुहानमागता ।

एकाकिनी तद्योर्यम्बन्नानार्थे चारकोचोचे ॥ 30 ॥

द्वितीय श्रीराघाभासि कांवा कमलतोचने ।

इच्छामसु वयं ज्ञातु तत्चवामाखीह शोभने ॥ 31 ॥

O Lovely-eyed! Whose daughter are you? Who is your husband? Why have you come here alone to bathe in this tank? O Lotus-eyed! You seem to be a second Lakṣmī; O Beautiful One! We want to know-something; please reply exactly.

कोमती चरणी कान्ते स्थिती भूमावानुक्ती ।

हदये कुरतः पीड़ां चलर्ते चललोचने ॥ 32 ॥

O Beloved! Your feet are exceedingly gentle; why have you not put on any shoes; why are you walking barefooted? Our hearts are being troubled to see you walking thus barefooted?

विमानाहासि तर्वीण कांध पढ़्यां जयस्वः ।

अनावृतात्र तविपि किमर्थे गमान तव ।। 33 ॥

O Thin bodied One! Your body is very soft that you ought to have gone in car; why are you thus walking on foot and in such an ordinary dress in this forest?

दारीशितसमयुक्ता कयं न त्व विनिर्मिता ।

राजपुत्रप्रसा वासि वद सर्व चरानेने ॥ 34 ॥

Why have not hundreds of maid-servants accompanied you? O lovely faced One! Speak truly whether you are a princess or an Apsarā.

धन्या भाता यतो जाता धन्योजी जनकस्तव ।

वक्तू त्वां नैव शगतो च भर्तुभार्य त्वाना ॥ 35 ॥

O Sinless One! Blessed is your mother from whom you are born; blessed is your father. Specially the person with whom you are married, we are unable to describe his fortune.

देवलोकाधिका भूमिर्ये चैव सुतोचने ।

प्रचलंड्गरणतेउ द्य स्माववति भूतलम् ॥ ३६ ॥

O Lovely eyed! This earth is being sanctified by the movements of your feet; consequently this garden is now purer today than the Devaloka.

सीभाग्याद्य मृगा: कामं ये त्वां एपवित् वे वे ।

ये चान्ये पक्षिणः सर्वे भूरिये चातिरियातना ॥ 37 ॥

Boundless is the fortune of these deer and birds

who can see you whenever they like; what more can we say than this that this forest is rendered very pure.

सत्यार्जत तब चात्यर्थ सत्यं बृहि सुतोचने ।

पिता कल सपी: क्वासी ग्रहुधिज्ञात्स सादर ॥ 38 ॥

O Fair One with fair eyes! It is needless to praise your beauty; speak truly who is your father and who is your husband; we like very much to see them.

व्यास उवाच

तयोरिति वचः श्रुत्वा राजाकन्याजितिपुस्ति ।

तत्वुवाच जयाक्रान्ता देव्युजी नृपात्मजा ॥ 39 ॥

गर्जाप्तितनयं मां वां विज्ञं भार्यां मुनेत्रि ।

च्यवनस्य सर्ती कान्ता पिंग द्रतां चयुच्छया ॥ 40 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! On hearing their words, the exquisitely beautiful princess bespoke to the twin Devas with much bashfulness: "I am the daughter of Sāryāti; father has given me over, under the directions of the Daiva, to the Maharṣi Cyavana.

पतिरधोर्षिति मे देखे वृद्धशातीव तापसः ।

तस्य सेवामहोत्रयं करोमि प्रीतमानसा ॥ 4 1 ॥

I am his chaste dear wife; the Maharṣi is staying in this very place. O Twin Devas! My husband is a blind ascetic and he is very aged. I gladly serve him day and night according to the rules of chastity amongst women.

को युवा किंभिह्यातो परीतिरीक्षित चाओं ।

तजागल प्रकुस्तमाओं चाछा पावनम् ॥ 42 ॥

Who are you? And why have you come here? My husband is staying in the Asrama; kindly come and sanctify the Asrama."

तदाकरणं वचो दसावुचतुस्तां नाराधि ।

कर्थ त्वमसि कल्याणि विंत्रा दत्ता तपविचेने ॥ 43 ॥

O King! The two Aśvins heard her and said: "O Auspicious One! Why has your father betrothed you, such a gem, to an old ascetic?

धानसोर्पस्मिनोहेशे विद्युल्लीसादामी यथा ।

न देखेवधि तूल्य हि तल दृष्टास्ति पभामी ॥ 44 ॥

लं दिव्यांवरायेयासि सोभसे नाजिनैवृता ।

सर्वाभरणसंयुक्ता नीलालकवर्धिधी ॥ 45 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

It is very strange. Indeed! In this solitary forest you are shining like a steady lightning; what more can we say than this that we hardly find a beautiful lady like you, even in the Devaloka! Alas! The Deva dress and a full set of ornaments and blue dyes look well on you; this deer-skin and barks of trees in no way fit you.

अहो विधेईकलितं विवेहितं

यद्रच रफ्मोक वने विश्वीदिस ।

विशालानेउन्यमितं पति प्रिये

मुर्नि समासाछ जाततूरं भृणम् ॥ 46 ॥

वृथा वृतलेन पृष्ठं न शोभसे

नवं वयः प्राय्य मुनेत्यपिछते ।

मनोभवेनाशु शगः सुर्मंधिताः

पत्ति कसिन्यतितीदुशस्तव ॥ 47 ॥

O Beautiful One! Your eyes are very large; yet the Creator has given you a blind husband; specially a very aged one; and you are wearing away by constantly dwelling with your blind husband in this forest. What more can be wrong for the Creator than this? O deer-eyed One! In vain you have selected him for your husband.

त्यमेधभार्यों नवरोयान्याचिता

कृतासि धात्रा ननू मंदबुद्धिना ।

न वैनमहैस्यसितायतेक्षणो पत्ते

त्यमर्थ कुरु भारकोचने ॥ 48 ॥

At this period 'of your and beauty it does not look at all well to see you with your blind husband. You are versed in dancing and music; but your husband is blind and aged; when in dancing you will shoot your darts of love, on whom then, will those arrows fall?

यूथेव ते जीविकेम्बनुजेशणे

पर्ति च सम्पाय्य मुनिं गतेक्षणम् ।

वने निवासं च तथासजिनांवर-

प्रधारणं योग्यतं न मवमहे ॥ 49 ॥

O large-eyed One! Oh! The Creator is certainly of a very little understanding! Else why would he have made you, so full of youthful vitality, the wife of a blind man? O lovely-eyed One! You are never fit for him; select another husband.

अतो5नवद्योग्यभयोस्त्वमेकं

वरं कुरुध्यावहिता सुलोचने ।

किं यौविनं मानिनि संक्रोपेथि

द्वथा सुर्दि सुन्दरी सेवमाना ॥ 50 ॥

O Lotus-eyed One! Your husband is not only blind but an ascetic; so your life is quite useless; we do not consider it fit that you reside in this forest and put on this bark and deer-skin. O dark-eyed One! Your body and every limb thereof is very beautiful; judge well and make one amongst us your husband.

किं सेवसे5भाग्यविजितं तं

सापुस्तां पोषणार्थणाथम् ।

त्वकत्वा भुनीं सर्वसुखापवर्जितं

भजानवहायंगुभयोरूत्वमेकम् ॥ 51 ॥

O Proud One! Why are you being so very beautiful, spending your youth in vain in serving this Muni? No good signs are visible in this Muni; he cannot maintain nor protect you even; why are you, then, serving him in vain? O spotless One! Leaves at once this Muni, quite incapable in giving any sort of pleasure, and marry one of us.

लं नन्दने चैत्रध्ये चने च

कृष्णव कानते प्रथितं विहाराम ।

अन्नेय भूद्रुने कर्थ हिं कारतं

विजेच्चे मानिनि मानहीन् ॥ 5 2 ॥

O Beloved! Then you will enjoy in the Nandana Kānana or in the forest of Caitraratha. O Proud One! How will you spend your time with the aged husband, being brought to so much humiliation and without any dignity and self-respect.

भूपात्मजा तर्व शुभ्नक्षणा च

जानासि संसारविद्धारभावम् ।

भायेत्र हीना विजने वनेत्र

कालं कवं वाहस्ये चृषा च ॥ 53 ॥

You are endowed with all auspicious signs; moreover you are a princess; you are not ignorant of all enjoyments in this world; why then you like to live such an unfortunate life in vain in this forest? O Princess! Your face is exceedingly beautiful;

Book VII Chapter V

your eyes are wide and your waist is thin. Your voice is sweet like a cuckoo.

तस्माद्रजस्य चिकभाविणि चाक्ववले

एकं झ्योत्सव सुखाय विशालानेते ।

देवालयेषु च कुशोदिरं पृथ्व

भोगोरत्थत्वा मुनि जरठमाणू नृपैंश्रुपत्रि ॥ 54 ॥

Who is more beautiful than you? Quite now your aged ascetic husband and marry one of us for your happiness; then you will be able to enjoy excellent celestial things in the heavens.

किं ते सुखं चात्र वने सुकेशि

ब्रुद्रेन सार्थ विजने मृगाशि ।

सेवा तर्पाधस्य नवं चयछ्न

किं ते मर्तं भूपतिषुवि दुःखम् ॥ 55 ॥

O good-haired one! What pleasure can you derive by your staying in this forest with your blind husband! O deer-eyed One. It is very painful for

you to serve at this young age of yours, to remain in this forest and serve this aged man. O Princess! Is it that you like troubles and nothing else.

नववयः सुखोभोगसमीहितं

चटुलपक्ष्मधरे वराविधिन ।

शशिमुखि त्वमतीव सुकोमतत

फलजनाहरणं तव नोचिनम् ॥ 56 ॥

शीत श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणो सत्यमरक्ये

चतुर्वी5ध्याः: 11 4 11

O One with a face lovely like the Moon! We see that you are of a very soft body; so to collect water fruits is never a duty fit for you."

Here ends the Fourth Chapter in the Seventh Book on the conversation between the two Aśvins and the

Princess Sukanyā in Śrimaddeviḥhāgavatam, the

Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses, by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VII Chapter V

CHAPTER V

On Cyavana's Getting His Youth

यास उवाच

तयोस्तूद्रावितं श्रुत्वा वेयमाना नृपात्मवा ।

धैर्यमालंख्य तौ तत्र बथाये पितभाषिणी ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing their words, the princess began to tremble; but holding on patience she spoke to them in reserved terms thus:

देवौ वां रविष्युजी च सर्वजी सुरुसमतो ।

सर्ती मां धर्मोहितां च नैवं वदितुमईः ॥ २ ॥

"You are the sons of Sūrya and you are the acknowledged deities amongst the gods; specially you know everything, I am a chaste virtuous woman. You ought not to speak to me in the above manner.

विज्ञा दत्ता सुरप्रेष्ठ मुनवे योगाधर्मिणे ।

कयं चेष्ठधीय तं मार्ग पुंड्रतरीगणसेवितम् ॥ ३ ॥

श्रडणसं सर्वलोकस्य कर्मसाक्षी दिवाकरः ।

क्रयपाच्चैव सम्पूर्ति नैवं भाषितुमईः ॥ ४ ॥

O Twin Devas! Father has betrothed me to the Muni practising the Yoga Dharma; besides I am chaste; how can I behave like a prostitute! This

Sun is the Witness of the actions good or bad of all people; He is therefore looking on our actions also. Besides you both are born in the family of the high-souled Kaśyapa.

कुलकन्या पर्चितं चलकत्या कवम्यां भजेजरम् ।

असारोउस्मिन्त संसरो जानंतं धर्मपिर्यम् ॥ 5 ॥

यथेच्छं गच्छतं देवौ शपां दास्यामि वान्नयो ।

सुकान्याद्रं च शपतिः पतिभक्तिपरायण ।। 6 ॥

Thus it is utterly wrong for you to utter such (irreligious and infamous) words. You know well the course of Dharma, what is religious and what is irreligious in this world which has got nothing substantial; how can a family woman leave her husband and serve another? Go wherever you like, O Sinless Devas! I am the daughter Sukanyā of the King Śaryāti, devoted to my husband. Otherwise I will curse you,”

व्यात उवाच

जूल्याकार्यं वचस्तस्य नास्त्वी विन्सितो भूगुम् ।

तावबूतां पुनस्त्वेजां शंकमानौ भयं मुने: ॥ 7 ॥

राजपुत्र प्रसत्री तु धर्मेण वरवर्गिनि ।

वरं वरय सुश्रोषि दास्यावः श्रेयसे तव ॥ 8 ॥

Vyāsa said: O Bhārata! Hearing these words, the Aśvīns were greatly surprised, and, afraid of the Muni, spoke again: “O Princess! We are very much pleased to see your chastity; therefore, O Beautiful Woman! Ask boon from us; we will grant for your welfare.

जानीहि प्रमदे नूनमावां देहिभिषयते ।

युवानं रूपसम्यत्र प्रकुर्बय पत्ते तव ॥ 9 ॥

O honoured Woman! We, the physicians of the gods, will certainly make your husband exceedingly beautiful and young.

ततख्यायामासंक पतिमेकतमं वुणु ।

समानरूपदेहानां मध्ये चातुर्पयिच्छते ॥ 10 ॥

सा तदोर्वदंचनं शुत्वा विक्सिता स्वपति तदा ।

गलोबारच तयोबाक्यं ताथयापुकर्तं यद्धतुम् ॥ 11 ॥

O Smart and Intelligent One! When we three will be exactly the same in figure, age and lustre! you better can make one us your husband." Hearing their words Sukanyā became greatly amazed and went to her own husband and spoke everything what they, the God's physicians, said.

सुकन्योचार

स्वामिन्यूयुक्ती देवी सम्पाती च्यवान्याने ।

दृष्टी मया विद्यदौंने नासली भूगुनन्दन ॥ १२ ॥

Sukanyā said: “O Husband! The Aśvins, the Sūrya’s sons, have come close to our Āśrama. I have seen the two Devas and their bright bodies.

वीक्ष्य में चास्वसईर्दी जातौ कामातुरवृष्णे ।

क्रवितं घवर्न स्वामियन्ति ते नवत्येवनम् ॥ 13 ॥

दिव्यदेहं कल्प्यवहशुक्यन्तं मृनिं किल ।

एतेन समयेनाङ्त तं शृणु तं मयोदितम् ॥ 14 ॥

समावयवक्षरं च करियावः परितं तल ।

तत्र जयाणामयमाके परितेकतमं वृषु ॥ 15 ॥

Seeing me beautiful they were overpowered with passion and told me. “We will, be sure, make your blind husband, young, bright and give him two eyes again; but you will have to make one condition. Hear it: Your husband will be exactly

like us and you will have to select your husband amongst three of us."

तच्छूवाउह्मिहायाता प्रहुं त्वां कार्यम्बुद्धान् ।

कि कतन्यवमतः साधो बृह्यास्म-कार्यसंकटे ॥ १ ६ ॥

O Holy One Hearing this as strange, I now come to inform you. Now judge and say what I am to do now.

देवयायाउपि दुर्जिया न जाने कपटं तयोः ।

यद्राजाय सर्वेत्र तत्कारोमि तद्वीक्षितम् ॥ 17 ॥

The Deva's Māyā is very difficult to comprehend; the more so, when I do not know their intentions! O Omniscient One! I will act as you desire."

च्यवन उवाच

गच्छ कान्तेउषा नास्त्वी वचनान्मय सुवते ।

आनास्य समीपं मे शीघं देवाधिपचर्ये ॥ १८ ॥

क्रियतात्मागु तद्वाक्यं नात्र कार्या विचारणा ।

Cyavana said: “O Beloved! Go just now, at my word, to the Aśvins and bring them, O auspicious One! before me. What more shall I say than this: Go and observe, what they say, as early as possible. There is no need to think over this matter.”

व्यास उवाच

एवं सा समनुसात तत्र नावा बचोउझवीत् ॥ १९ ॥

क्रियतामागु नासकी समयेन सुतेतमी ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus getting the permission from her husband, Sukanyā went immediately to them and said: "O Aśvins! You are the chief gods; now do as you say. I agree to observe what you desire."

तच्छूचा चाश्रिणी वाययं तस्याती तत्र चागती ॥ 20 ॥

उचतू ततवपुरी तां पतिस्तव शिशलपः ।

रूपार्थं च्यवनस्पूर्ण ततोउम्म: प्रविशेग्र ह ॥ 21 ॥

Hearing her words, the two Devas then went to the Muni's Aśrama and told the princess: "Let your husband enter in the midst of the water. The aged Cyavana Muni went down quickly in the midst of waters to attain a good form.

अधिनावपि पश्रान्त्रविष्टी सर उत्तमम् ।

ततस्ते निःसृतात्मासारसस्तक्षणात्वयः ॥ 22 ॥

Book VII Chapter V

Next the two Aśvins entered into the water of that tank. A few minutes after, the three persons came out of the tank.

तुल्यरूपा दिव्यदेहा युवानः सदुगाः किल ।

दिव्यकुष्ठतन्यभावाशा: समानावयवास्तताः। 2 3 11

All were equally bright, equally beautiful, equally young and their limbs were decorated equally with earrings and various other ornaments. तेउबुचनाहिता: सर्वे वृषीध वरवर्धिनि ।

अस्माकभीक्षिति भईे पत्ते त्वमलानाने ॥ 24 ॥

यमस्नावधिका प्रीतस्ते वृषुध्व वराने ।

They all spoke simultaneously: “O Auspicious One! There is no other woman beautiful like you, especially your face is very clean and fair; therefore select any one of us three as your husband. O Fair One! Whomever you love most, choose him.

व्यास उतच

सा दृष्ट्वा तुल्यक्रपत्नास्मानवयसस्ता ॥ 25 ॥

एकस्वारामूल्यवेधोंकी-वे देवसुतोपायन् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Sukanyā then saw their bodies are equally bright and beautiful; not the least difference is to be found in beauty, age, voice and dress.

सा तु संशयापात्रा बीक्ष्य तान्सद्धगुक्तीन् ॥ 26 ॥

अजानाती पति संम्यन्त्याकुता समन्वितन्त् ।

She became doubtful on seeing their equal appearances. The princess, not being able to distinguish her husband, became very anxious and thought:

किं कंपोक प्रयस्तुल्याः कं कुणोभि न वेषण्डव्छ् ॥ 27 ॥

पत्ति देवसुता होते संशोय पतितास्म्यहम् ।

“What am I to do now? Whom to choose? They are exactly the same. I cannot distinguish who is my real husband?

इउजलादिंमं सभ्यदोषाभ्यामिक कलित्यम् ॥ 28 ॥

कर्तेल किं किया चाचा मरणं समुपानातम् ।

This may be the magic set up by the two Aśvins. However, I am put to a great crisis. I won't ever select another who is not my husband.

न मया पतिमुसूझ बरणीयः कर्धंचन ॥ 29 ॥

देवसत्यापुनिकः कक्षिद्वित्येभा मम धरणा ।

Therefore my death is well nigh; what to do now? The third form seen now may be also a Deva's son."

इति संचित्व मनसा परां विधेश्धां शिवान् ॥ 30 ॥

दस्ये भगवती देर्वी तुधाव च कृणोदरी ।

Thus cogitating, she resolved to meditate on the Highest Prakrti, the Lady of the Universe, the most Auspicious One. Then the thin-bellied princess began to sing the hymns of the Bhagavatī.

सुकन्योवाच

शरणं त्वां जगमातः प्राणतप्रेम भृणतु:खिता ॥ 31 ॥

रक्ष मेउषा सतीधर्मं नामृमि चरणी तव ।

Sukanyā said: “O World-Mother! Under most painful circumstances I take refuge; preserve my chastity; I bow down to thy feet.

नम: पद्योद्धृदे देविनः नः शंकरात्वणे ॥ 32 ॥

विष्णुपिये नयो नलिम्य वेदमातः सरस्वति ।

इदं जगच्चया मुंई सर्वे स्थावारजमाम् ॥ 33 ॥

पासि लविमदम्यथा तथाान्ति सलोकानाले ।

O Devi! Salutations to Thee, born of lotus. O Thou, the dear consort of Śañkara! Salutations to Thee. O Thou favourite to Viṣṇu, O Mother of the Vedas! O Sarasvatī! Salutations to Thee. Thou hast created the world, moving and unmoving; Thou art preserving it without being least excited; again Thou art swallowing it for the peace and well-being of all.

ब्रह्मविष्णुमहेशानां जननी त्वं सुर्मेमता ॥ 34 ॥

बुद्धिद्वासि सल्मानां ज्ञानिनां मोक्ष्यत सदा ।

What more, Thou art the Most Worshipful Mother of Brahmā, Visju, and Maheśa. Thou always illumined the understanding of the illiterate and Thou always grantest liberation to the Jñāins.

अज्ञा तं प्रकृति: पूर्णी पृंक्षपियदर्शना ॥ 35 ॥

भृक्तिमुक्तिप्रदास्ति त्व प्राणिनं विश्वतदानम् ।

अज्ञानं दुःखदा कामा सत्त्वानां मुखसाधानं ॥ 36 ॥

सिद्धिद्वा योगिनामंब जयदा कीर्तिंदा पुनः ।

Thou art the Prime Prakṛti in fulness and the Beloved of the Prime Puriṣa. Thou grantest Bhukti

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

(enjoyment) and Mukti (freedom) to the souls that are cleansed and pure; Thou givest pain to those that are entirely void of knowledge and Thou grantest happiness to those that are endowed with Satva Guna. O Mother! Thou bestowet Siddhi (the success, the eight supernatural powers), fame, and victory to the Yogis!

शरणं त्वं प्रवासास्मि विस्तयं घरमं नाति ॥ 37 ॥

पर्ति दृषीव मे मातर्मनास्मिछोकासारते ।

देवाभ्यां रचितं कूटं कं बुणोमि विमोहिता ॥ 38 ॥

पर्ति दृषीय सर्वेत्रे विदित्वा मे सतीत्रतम् ।

Being merged in an ocean of bewilderment, I come now to take refuge unto Thee. O Mother! The two Devas are playing hypocrisy with me; thus puzzled I ca'nt fix my mind whom to select; therefore I am merged in an ocean of sorrow. Save me by shewing my real husband. O Omniscient One! Knowing my vow of chastity dost Thou enlighten me to so that I can know my husband.

व्यास उवाच

एवं सुत्ता तदा देखी तथा त्रिपुसुन्दरी ॥ 39 ॥

इदयेप्रस्थानसदा ज्ञानं दद्वावधु सुखोदयम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus pleased by the Sukanyā's prayers, the Devi Tripura Sundarī then imparted to her the pleasant Sattva Jñāna (knowledge pertaining to Sattva Guna).

निश्रित्य मनसा तुल्यवयोरूपधार्यसाति ॥ 40 ॥

प्रसभीम्य तु तात्मवर्चिब बाला स्वकं पतिन् ।

She then looked again at the three personages, and though they were similar in appearance and beauty, instantly she recognised mentally her husband and chose him.

ब्रुतेश द च्यवने देवो ननष्ट्री तो बभूवतुः ॥ 4 1 ॥

सतीभर्षे समालोक्य संप्रीति ददतुर्वेम् ।

When Sukanyā selected the Muni Cyavana, the two Devas became greatly pleased to see that.

भगवतः प्रसादेन प्रसत्री तौ सुरोत्तमो ॥ 42 ॥

सुनिमामंत्र तसा गमनायोहात्वाषी ।

The two Devas were pleased by the grace of

Bhagavati; they were further pleased to see the Dharma of chastity and granted her the boon.

लब्धा तु च्यवनो रूपं नेत्रे भार्याँ च यौवनम् ॥ 43 ॥

द्वी3वबीभ्मतोजास्ती नासत्याविद्वे वचः ।

उपकान: कृतोउयं मे युवाभ्यां सुस्सत्मी ॥ 44 ॥

किं बबीम सुखं प्राप्तं संसारोस्मिन्तुने ।

प्राथ भार्यी सुक्रिजानतो कु:ख मे पेभवद्वल्हम् ॥ 45 ॥

They then bade good-bye to Cyavana and were ready to start to their own place when Cyavana being very much pleased to get through their grace, his beauty, youth and wife, interrupted them, saying "O high-minded Devas! You have done much good to me. I used to feel pain every day, in spite of my having this wife having good hairs!

अनस्य चातिकुब्छ्य भोगहीनस्य कानने ।

युवाभयं नयने द्रते यौवन्तं रूपमद्धुतम् ॥ 46 ॥

सम्पादितं ततः किंचिवुक्नुर्गमहं बुवे ।

But owing to your mercy, I cannot describe how happy I am now in this world of woes and troubles. I was very aged and blind and was without any enjoyment but it is you that coming to this forest have brought to me eyes, youth and exquisite beauty. Therefore, O twin Devas! I desire to do something good to you in return.

उपकारिति मित्र थो नोपकुर्णत्कर्धचन ॥ 47 ॥

तं धियासून नरं देवी भवेच्च ऋणवान्युवि ।

तस्मादां वाहितं कैकिवहानुभिच्छाम साम्यन्ति ॥ 48 ॥

Fie on him, who does not return anything for the good that he has received from a beneficent friend. That man remains indebted for ever in this world; therefore I am desirous to give your two now whatever you want.

आत्माने क्रणभोक्षारा देवेशी नृतनस्य च ।

प्रार्थितं वां प्रतास्यामि यदत्पश्य सुगतुर्भेः ॥ 49 ॥

बुबाबां वां मनोदिष्टं प्रीतोरिस सुकुतेन वाम् ।

O twin Devas! Even if the Devas or the Asuras find it difficult to attain, I will give that to you to free myself of the debt I owe to you. I am greatly pleased at your good deed; therefore be kind enough to speak out whatever you desire."

Book VII Chapter VI

སྟན་པ་ཀྱི་ཀྱི་སྟན་པའི་རྒྱལ་མཚོགས་ཀྱི་སྟན་པ་ཀྱི་སྟན་པ་ཀྱི་སྟན་པ་ཀྱི་སྟན་པ་

मुने पितु: प्रसादेन सर्वे नो मनसेशित्मा ॥ 51 ॥

उत्काठा सोमपानस्य वर्तैं नी सुरैः सह ।

भिपजाविति देेवेन निपिडूची चमसघट्रे ॥ 52 ॥

They began to consult with each other, and spoke to the Muni Cyavana who was seated with Sukanya beside him: "O Maharsī! We have got all our desires by the grace of Father! Still it is difficult for us to drink some libation along with the other Devas and we thirst after that very strongly.

गढ़ेगा वित्ते चेउ ब्रहणः कनकावले ।

तस्माचमपि धर्मे यदि शफेर्स ताप्त ॥ 53 ॥

कार्यमेतिद्ध्र कत्थंव वाज्छतं नी सुसंतम्न ।

At the great sacrifice of Brahmā in the Golden Mountain (Kanakācala), Indra, the King of the Devas, ordered us not to drink Soma, as we were physicians.

तृतिड्राय वां बह्न-कुरु वां सोमपायिति ॥ ५४ ॥

विपासाउ्तित सुयुषाया तन्तः समुपायास्यति ।

Therefore, O Knower of Dharma! O Ascetic! You will certainly do us a great favour if you can make us drink the Soma juice; we would be very glad and have our desires satisfied."

च्यवनस्तु तयोः प्राह तच्छुत्वा वचनं मुचु ॥ 55 ॥

यदहं रूपसमयजो वयसा च सम्पिततः ।

Thus hearing the words of the Aśvins, Maharṣi Cyavana gladly spoke to them the following gentle words.

कृतो भवद्ध्वां वृद्धः सन्थायां च प्रणताविति ॥ 56 ॥

तत्माद्युवां कतिष्यामि प्रीत्याङ्ग सेभापयिति ।

O Twin Devas! I was blind and aged; but now I am become a young and beautiful man, and it is by your grace that I have got back my wife.

मिषातो देवराजस्य सत्यमेततुब्बीध्मसून ॥ 57 ॥

राजसूनं चितते यजे शर्जरोरितयहुतेः ।

Therefore I speak this truly before you that at the great sacrifice of the lustrous King Śaryāti, I will gladly make yōu drink the Soma in the presence of Indra, the King of the Devas.

इत्याकरणं वचो रहही ती विद्ध प्रति जममनुः ॥ 58 ॥

च्यवनस्तां गृहीतासु तुं नागामाशमपडतम् ॥ 59 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागते महापुराणे सतमकन्थे

पत्रजीयान: 11 5 11

Hearing these words of the Muni, the twin Aśvins were greatly pleased and went back to the world of the Devas. And the Muni Cyavana, too, returned to his own Āśrama with his wife Sukanyā.

Here ends the Fifth Chapter of the Seventh Book on

the getting of youth by Cyavana Muni in

Śrimaddevībhāgāvatam the Mahā Purānam of

18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VII Chapter VI

CHAPTER VI

On the Asvins Getting the Right to Drink Soma

जनमेव जय जय

यवयनेक कथं वैद्यों ती क्रतौ सोमपाधिनो ।

वचनं च कर्ण सत्यं जातं तस्य महातयनः ॥ १ ॥

मानुष्यस्य वर्तं कौरुपदेवाजवर्तं प्रति ।

निधिद्धौ भिपब्जौ तेन कृतौ तौ सामपाधिनो ॥ २ ॥

धर्मनिष्ठी तताश्रयं वित्तरेणा वद प्रभो ।

चारितं च्यवनस्याङ श्रोतुकामो5स्म वसंधा ॥ ३ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Muni! How did Maharsi Cyavana make these with twin Devas drink Soma and how his words came out to be true. Human

strength is insignificant compared to Indra's strength. Indra forbade the physicians, the Asvins, to drink the Soma juice. How then could the Muni give the right thereof. This is very wonderful. Therefore, O Thou, devoted to Dharma! O Lord! Describe in detail the doings of this Maharsi Cyavana. I am very anxious to hear it.

यास उवाच

निशगाय महाराज चरितं परमादुतम् ।

च्यवन्य मखे तत्मभूक्ष्योतर्भूथि भारत ॥ 4 ॥

सुकन्यां सुन्दरीं प्रायः च्यवन: सुरसक्षिणः ।

विवहार प्रसातमा देवकन्यामिवापर: ॥ 5 ॥

कदाचिद्ध शगवतिषभार्य चिंतातुरा भुगम् ।

पर्ति प्राह वेयमाना वचनं रहती प्रिया ॥ 6 ॥

राज्युत्री तथा दत्ता मुनयेड्याय कानने ।

भुता जीविते वा सा तु उह्छ्या संख्या तथा ॥ 7 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! In that famous sacrificial ceremony solemnised by of the King Śaryāti, Cyavana Rṣi did wonderful feats. O Bharata! I am now narrating to you his entirely wonderful character. Hear it attentively. Maharṣi Cyavana, illustrious like the Devas, began to enjoy with a cheerful mind and gladdened heart, with his beautiful wife Sukanyā who was like a Deva Kanyā. Now, once on an occasion, the wife of Śaryāti became very anxious and trembling said to her husband weeping: "O King! You have betrothed your daughter to the blind Muni Cyavana; now it is your duty to go and enquire whether the daughter is living or is dead. O Lord! What is my beautiful daughter doing with that husband.

मष्ठ नाथ मुनेस्तावदास्मे उद्युमदात् ।

कि कांती सुकान्या सा प्राय नार्य तथाविधम् ॥ 8 ॥

पुर्षिदुःखेन राजर्षे दरधान्सि सर्वेश हृदि ।

तापानय विश्राताखीं तपःशामां मदन्तिके ॥ 9 ॥

प्रजामि सर्वथा पुर्गि क्रगांशी वल्कलावृताम् ।

अंधों पतिं समासाठ दुःखभाजं कृगोदरिभ् ॥ 10 ॥

Please go just now to the Muni's Āśrama and see about it. O Rājarsī! I always burn in pain and agony when I think of her. She must have become very lean and thin due to the troubles of Tapasyā; please bring her quickly here to my presence. She is suffering an everlasting pain from having an aged blind husband; and it is quite likely she has become very lean and thin. I am anxious, therefore, to see my daughter lean and thin who is wearing barks of trees as her dress.

श्वास्तिकाव

गच्छामोह विशालाधिक सुक्रवो रेडुमादरात् ।

घियमुप्री वरातोहे मुनि तं संशितान्तम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Śaryāti said: “O broad-eyed One! I am going gladly just now to see my dear daughter and that Muni of severe vows.

व्यास उवाच

एवमुकत्वा तु शयिति: कामिनी शोकसंकुलाम् ।

जगाम रथामहाच स्वरिताश्रमं मुने: ॥ 12 ॥

गत्वासप्रसामसीपे तु तमपश्यन्हीपितः ।

नवयीवनसममर्जे देवपुत्रोपमं पुनिम् ॥ 13 ॥

तं चितोकव्यागराकां विस्तयं नृपतर्गतः ।

किं कृतं कृत्तिसतं कर्म पुथा लोकविर्गहितम् ॥ 14 ॥

निर्होजसी मुनिकुढ़ल्चनयाअय: पति: कृतः ।

कार्मपीडितया कामं प्रशांतोःप्यतिनिर्भिनः ॥ 15 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus saying to his wife, distressed with sorrow, the King Śaryāti mounted on his chariot and quickly went towards the Śārama of the Cyavana Muni. On reaching there, he saw the Maharṣi Cyavana like a Deva's son. Seeing his body like that of a Deva, the King became bewildered and began to think thus: "What! Has my daughter done such an ugly act, blameable in the society! That Muni was very calm and quiet, penniless and very old; my daughter, perhaps, being overpowered with passion, killed him and has taken, no doubt, another husband.

दुःसहोर्धं युष्यधन्व विगोशेण च योवते ।

कुले कलंकः सुमहाननया मानवे कुतः ॥ 16 ॥

यिचक्स्य जीवित लोके ययव्युजी हे कुलिता ।

सर्वपार्यैसु दुःखाय पुत्री भवति देहिनान् ॥ 17 ॥

It is indeed difficult to control the God of Love, armed with his flowery bow; the period of youth is moreover very hard to conquer. So this daughter impelled by lust has thrown a dreadful stigma on the clear name of the family of the great Manu. Fie on him whose daughter in this world is of a vicious character! It seems that daughter are born for the expiation of all the sins committed by their fathers.

मया त्वनृचितं कर्म कृतः स्वार्थेत्य सिद्धे।

युद्धायांधाय या दत्ता पुरी सर्वांन्मान किल ॥ 18 ॥

Book VII Chapter VI

कन्या योग्याय दात्याय पिता सर्वांत्मना किल ।

तातृगं हि फलं प्राप्तं यातृगं वै कृतं मच ॥ 19 ॥

But what an unjust act have I committed for my own selfish ends? It is highly incumbent on every father to betroth his daughter with every care possible to a bridegroom suitable in every respect; but I did not do it and now have got the fruits equivalent to my doing.

हन्मि चेच्छा तनयं वु:शीलां पापाकीर्णिन् ।

स्त्रीहृता युस्तरा स्याने तथा युया विशेषतः ॥ 20 ॥

मनुवर्गस्तु विद्याला: सकलंकः कृते मया ।

लोकापवादो वलवानुस्पताज्ञा स्नेहग्रंघतला ॥ 21 ॥

किं कंभौमिति विजात्यो यदा मनः स पार्थिवः ।

सुकन्यथा तदा देवादुर्वाईशताकृतः पिता ॥ 22 ॥

If I kill my daughter, vicious and unchaste, I will incur sin due to killing a woman and moreover my daughter. I am the cause of this stain on Muni's line of descent. On the one hand, the scandal on one is very powerful; and the affection for a daughter is strong on the other, what am I to do now?" The King became merged in deep thoughts. At this time Sukyanā accidentally saw her father thus drowned in anxious thoughts.

सा दूध्दा तं जगामाशु सुकन्या वितृत्तिके ।

गत्वा पगच्छ भूपालं प्रेमपूर्तिनामसा ॥ 23 ॥

Seeing him, Sukanyā instantly came to her father's side and asked the King in sweet affectionate words.

किं विचारयसे राजाँक्ष्ताव्याकृत्तितान्नः ।

उपविष्ट मुनि वीक्ष्य युवानमबुजेक्षणम् ॥ 24 ॥

O King! How is it that your face has become so pale with anxious thoughts, seeing the Muni sitting in front of you, a young man with lotus-eyes.

राष्ट्रीक पुरुषव्यार्ध प्रणमस्य पत्ति मम् ।

मा विषादं नृपश्रेष्ठ सरोतं कुरू मानव ॥ 25 ॥

O Father! What are you thinking? You belong to the famous Manu's family; besides, you are a high-minded man; you ought not to be sad so suddenly; come quickly and bow your head down before my husband.

व्यास उवाच

इति पुत्रा वचः शुवा श्यांति: कौधीषितः ।

प्रोचा चवनं राजा पुरःस्थां तनयं ततः ॥ 26 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the daughter's words, the King became impatient with anger and began to speak to her:

ताजीवाच

क्व मुनिश्रयन: पुत्रि वृद्धोंधतापयोतम: ।

कोउंये बुवा मदोभन्तः सन्तेढोज महामान ॥ 27 ॥

"O Daughter! Where is that aged blind ascetic Muni Cavana and who is this youth intoxicated with lust? A great doibt has arisen in my mind.

मुनि: किं निततः पापे त्वया दृष्टुताकारिण ।

नृतानोउसी पति: कामावत्कृत: कुलविनारिणि ॥ 28 ॥

O Vicious Soul! Have you slain that Cyavana Muni and engaged yourself in such a sinful act? O You, a Disgrace to your family! Have you accepted another husband out of your desire of lust?

सोड़ई चिन्तातुरतं न पष्णायाङ्ममंशित्यम् ।

किं कृतं दुक्छृतं कर्म कृत्वादार्विति किल ॥ 29 ॥

निम्पनोड़ं दुराचारे शोकाक्यों त्वकृतो3पुना ।

दूधैर्वेने पुरुषं दिव्यमदूद्वा च्यवनं मुनिम् ॥ 30 ॥

My mind has become very much troubled on not seeing that Muni in this Śārama. O vicious One! Now I do't see the Muni; but instead of him, I see this bright person. And thus it is on account of your sinful behaviour that my mind is drowned in the ocean of cares.

विद्यस्य तनुपाचाण्यु सा भूत्वा चचनं पितुः ।

गृहीत्वानीय पितरं भूर्तीतकमादरात् ॥ 31 ॥

च्यवनोउसी भूमितला जामात ते न संशयः ।

अधिष्यामीद्रुग: कानतः कृतः कमलतोवनः ॥ 32 ॥

Then hearing her father's words, Sukanyā smiled and gladly took him at once to her husband and said: "O Father! He is your son-in-law; He is the same Cyavana Muni; there is no doubt here. The twin Aśvins have given him this beautiful lustre and lotus-like eyes.

यदृष्छयाज्ञ संप्राणती नस्मावाब्धमे मम ।

ताप्यां कर्णणया नूनं च्यनस्तादृष्ण: कृतः ॥ 33 ॥

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

The two Ašvini Kumāras came accidentally to my Āśrama and out of mercy no doubt they have made Cyavana such a nice young man.

नाहं तब सुता तात तथा तथा पापकरिणी ।

यथा त्वं मन्यसे राजन्मुखी क्वसंगेशे ॥ 34 ॥

प्राणा त्वं मुनिं राजन्मार्विं च्यवनं पितः ।

अगृष्टि कारणं सर्व कथायिति वित्तम् ॥ 35 ॥

O King! I am not your that daughter that will do a vicious act as you think, beguiled by this beautiful form of the Muni. Father! Bow down before the Cyavana Muni. Ask him and he will tell you everything."

इति श्रुचा वचः पुचा: श्रवीत्स्वतितस्तदा ।

प्रणानाम पुन्ति तत्र नाला पप्रच्छ सादरम् ॥ 36 ॥

Hearing thus the daughter's words, the King went instantly to the Muni and bowed down before him and asked him affectionately thus:

राजोबाव

कथयस्य स्वपृतांतं भागवायु यथोचितम् ।

नयने च कर्व प्राप्ते कव रगत ते जरा पुनः ॥ 37 ॥

संशयोर्ध महामेऋति रूपं दृष्ट्वातिपुत्रम् ।

वद विस्तत्ते बढ़ाव्छूलाड़े सुखामपुभम् ॥ 38 ॥

The King said: “O Son of Bhrgu! How have you got your eyes back? Where has your old age gone? Kindly narrate all your details as early as possible. O Brāhmaṇa! Seeing your exquisitely beautiful form, a great doubt has arisen in me; so tell me everything in detail; I will be very glad, no doubt.”

च्यवन उत्तरा

नास्त्यावत्र सम्पाती देवानां भिपजाजूषी ।

उपकारः कृतसाध्यो कृपया नृपास्तम ॥ 39 ॥

Cyavana said: "O King! The two Aśvins, the physicians of the Gods, came here on their own account and have done me this good out of their mercy.

मया ताययां वरो द्त उपकारस्य होते ।

करिष्यामि मखे राजो भवतीं सोमपार्थिति ॥ 40 ॥

Owing to the benefit thus received, I have granted them the boon that I will make them drink

the Soma juice in the Agniṣtoma Yajñā of the King Śaryāti.

एवं मया वयः प्राप्तं लोधने विमले तथा ।

स्वस्थो भव महाराज संविश्रासनरे गुधे ॥ 4 1 ॥

Thus I have got these beautiful eyes and the new youth; therefore, O King! Collect yourself and sit in the holy sacrificial seat.

इत्युक्तः स तु विश्रिण सभार्य: पृथिवीपति: ।

सुखोपथिह: कल्याणी: कवाध्यके महात्मना ॥ 42 ॥

When the Brāhmin Cyavana Muni spoke thus, the King Śaryāti and his dear wife sat with greatest pleasure and began to talk on auspicious topics with the high-souled Muni.

अर्थीन भापीव: प्राह राजानं परिसांत्वयन् ।

याजविष्यापि राजस्वां संभागानुपकल्यय ॥ 43 ॥
