Then the Bhārgava consoled the King and said I will perform your sacrifice; please collect all the necessary materials.

मया प्रश्रितुं ताथवां कत्लैंथो सोमपी युवाम् ।

तत्कर्तैंयं नृपश्रेष्ठ तव प्रोज्ञपितिवरो ॥ 44 ॥

I have promised to the Asvins that certainly I will make them drink the Soma juice. Therefore, O King! I will have to carry that out in your sacrifice.

इन्द्र निवारियधामि कूब्द्र तेजाबेलेन वै ।

पायियधामि राजेन्द्र सोयं सोममखे तव ॥ 4 5 ॥

O King! If Indra be angry, I will stop him by my Tapas face and in the Agniśtoma Yajñā I will make the Aśvins drink Soma.

ततः परमसन्तुषः शर्षाति: पृथिवीपति: ।

च्यवन्य महाराज तृताक्यं प्रत्यपूजन्त् ॥ 46 ॥

संमान्य च्ववनं राजा जगाभ नगरं प्रति ।

सभार्थ्वातिसन्तुष: कुर्वेल्वातिं मुने: किल ॥ 47 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Śaryāti, the lord of the earth, then gladly approved the proposals of Cyavana Muni. The King then shewed respect to the Muni and, with a pleasant attitude of mind, returned to his city with his wife, all the while talking of the Muni in the way.

प्रशास्तोर्शनि महीये सर्वकाममपुटिभान् ।

कार्यायमास शर्शितैर्वंत्रायतनमुतमम् ॥ 48 ॥

Book VII Chapter VI

On a good auspicious day he, possessed of enormous wealth and prosperity, prepared an excellent place for the performance of the sacrifice.

समानीय मुनीन्युखान्वसिष्टमुखानसी ।

भार्यो वाजयामास च्यवन: पृथिवीपिति् ।। ४९ ॥

The Muni Cyavana then invited Vasistha and other respectable Munis and initiated the King Saryāti for the performance of the sacrifice.

वितते तु तथा यजे देवा: सर्वे सवासवा: ।

आजमुखाशिकी तन सोमार्थिपुजमन्तुः ॥ 50 ॥

At the commencement of the sacrifice, Indra and the other Devas and the two ASvins all came to the sacrifice to drink Soma.

इत्रस्तु शंकितस्तव वीक्ष्य तावधिगावृषी ।

पग्रष्ठ च सुरान्सर्विक्मतेो सुपगतां ॥ 51 ॥

Seeing the ASvins, Indra became afraid and asked the other Devas, "Why have the ASvins come here?

चिक्सिकसी न सोमाहाँ केनानीताविहित च ।

नावुद्वमासतत्र राजस्तु वितते मखे ॥ 52 ॥

They are the physicians and, therefore, never fit to drink Soma. Who has brought them hither at this great Agnistoma Sacrifice?"

अग्रहाच्छ्ववनः सोममधिज्ञोंदेवसोस्तव ।

ग्राहकतं वारयामास मा गृहाणैतयोह्न ॥ 53 ॥

The Devas remain all silent. Cyavana Muni then became ready to offer Soma to the Asvins and Indra immediately stopped him saying: "They are already prohibited to drink Soma as a sacrificial share; so do not accept the vessel of Soma for them."

तमाह च्यवन्तरत कथमोते रवे: सुते।

न प्रहाई च नास्तवी बृहि सर्व शचीपते ॥ 54 ॥

Cyavana then said: "O Lord of Śaci! These are the Sūrya's sons; tell, then, truly why these are rendered unfit to drink Soma. They are not of mixed blood; they are born of the legal wife of Sūrya Deva.

न संकरी समुवर्धो धर्मपत्नीसुतो रहेः ।

केन दोशेण देवेन्द्र नाहों सेमं विषयली ॥ 55 ॥

O Lord of the Devas! What is the fault, then, of

the Aśvins, the physicists, that they be prohibited to drink Soma juice.

निर्णयोउर मखे शक कतिथ्वः सर्वेद्वतैः ।

ग्राह्यिण्याम्बहं सोमं कृती तौ सोमपो मया ॥ 56 ॥

Please reply. O Indra! This point must be settled by all the Devas here. I have promised to make them drink the Soma juice in this sacrifice.

प्रेरितोउसी मया राजा मखाय मघवान्कित ।

एतदर्थ कक्रियामि सन्यं मे वनं विषो ॥ 57 ॥

To keep my word I have initiated the King in this sacrifice. I will have my word fulfilled; there is no doubt in this.

अभ्यापुकृतं शक्र यथा दर्तं नवं वयः ।

तस्मात्युपकातरतु कंतवः सर्वथा मया ॥ 58 ॥

O Indra! They have given me my youth and bestowed my eyes and have done me great good. I will also do good to them to my best."

इन्व जवाच

चिकित्सकी कृतावेति नास्वी निदिति सुर्गै: ।

उभावेति न सोमाई मां गृहाणैलयोर्भून ।। ५९ ॥

Indra said: “The Devas have appointed these Devas their Physicians; therefore they are looked down upon in the society; so they are unfit to drink Soma. You need not make them drink Some.”

च्छवन उवाच

अहल्याजार संवच्छ कॉपं चाठा निर्वर्कम् ।

खुञ्ज किं हिं ती सत्यौं न सोमाहौं सुतामकजी ॥ 60 ॥

एवं विवादे समुपस्थिते च

न कोर्पि वाचं तमुवाच भूप ।

ग्रहं तवोभार्वितिमन्तेजाः

संग्रहवायास तोवावेलेन ॥ 61 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागते महापुराणे सतमककन्ने

पशो3भाव: 11 6 11

Cyavana spoke: “O Indra! You have adulterated yourself with Athalyā; why are you, then, giving vent to your anger thus in vain. You have treacherously murdered Vṛtāsura; it is it is quite inappropriate for a vicious person like you to say that the Aśvins cannot have the right to drink Soma.

This is quite impossible." O King! On the springing of this dispute, no body spoke to Indra. The illustrious Bhārgava, then, made them accept the Soma juice.

Here ends the Sixth Chapter of the Seventh Book on granting the Aśvins the right to drink the Soma juice in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Śrimaddevbībhāgavatam

CHAPTER VII

On the Asvins Drinking the Soma cup

ध्यास उवाच

द्रते ग्रहे तु राजेन्द्र वासवः कुपितो भृणम् ।

प्रोवाच च्यवन्त तन दर्शनबलमात्मतः ॥ १ ॥

मा बढ़ावन्यो मयाद्वामियां त्वं कतुर्महसि ।

विध्यामि दिषं तं त्वां विश्वरूपमिवापरम् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the vessel filled with the Soma juice was given to the two Aśvins, Indra became very angry and showing his strength, spoke thus to the Muni Cyavana. “O Brāhmaṇa! Never will you be able to endow him with such a high honour. When you have shown towards me your enmity, I will kill you, no doubt, exactly like Viśvarūpa.”

च्यवन उवाच

मावमंस्या महात्मानी रूपद्रविषणवर्चौ ।

यो चक्रतुर्मी मयवन्युदारकमिवायपरम् ॥ ३ ॥

त्रते तां विबुधाश्राये कवं वाददते ग्रहम् ।

अंशभावपि देदेन्द्र देवी विद्धि परन्ती ॥ ४ ॥

Cyavana said: “O Indra! Do not insult the two high-souled Asvins. They have given me beauty, youth and lustre and made me look like a second Deva. O Lord of the Devas! Just as the other Devas can take the vessel of Soma excluding you, so the two powerful Asvins can do the same towards you.

इन उदय

भिपजी नाहंतः कामं ग्रहं येते कंक्षेचन ।

यदि दिल्सिय मन्त्रान्न शिरणछेद्यायिय सांग्रतम् ॥ 5 ॥

Indra said: “They are the physicians; so they cannot in any way have the right to accept the Soma cup. I will just now sever your head.”

व्यास उवाच

अनानुद्य तु तुढाक्यं वासवस्य च भागंवः ।

प्रहं तु प्राहयामास भतसंधित्व नं भृणम् ॥ 6 ॥

Vyāsa said: O Ornament to the race of Bharata!

At these words of Indra, the Muni made the Asvins accept the Soma cup, thus highly insulting, as it were, Indra and not taking any notice of his words.

सोमपात्रं वटा तान्यां गृहीतं तु पिपायमा ।

समीयेक वलापिष्टेव इयं चवनमब्बित् ॥ ७ ॥

आभ्यामर्थाय सोमां तं ग्राहीत्ययसि चेत्स्वयम् ।

वज्जं तु प्रहिष्यामि विश्वरूपमिवायम् ॥ ८ ॥

When the two Aśvins accepted the cup with a desire to drink the Soma thereof, the powerful Indra saw it and said: "If you make them drink Soma out of your own necessity, I will hurl thunderbolt on your head exactly in the same way as I did towards Viśvarāpū."

वासवेदैवमुक्तसु भार्थश्रातिराविंतः ।

जग्राह विधित्वत्सोममाधिश्छ्यामतिभूयन्ति ॥ 9 ॥

The Muni became violently angry at this and made the Aśvins drink the Soma according to due rites and ceremonies.

इन्होरिण प्राक्षित्कोपाद्धजमर्चे ख्यमायुधम् ।

पश्यतां सर्वैदेवानां सूर्यकोटिममुष्पम् ॥ 10 ॥

प्रेरितं चाणर्शि प्रैक्ष्य च्यवनस्तपास ततः ।

स्तंभयामास वज्जं स ग्राकस्यामिततेजसः ॥ 11 ॥

Indra, too, angrily hurled thunderbolt on him in the presence of all the Devas. The weapons shed lustre like million Suns. Seeing the thunderbolt hurled on him, the powerful Muni made the Indra's thunderbolt stand stock still by virtue of his Tapas.

Book VII Chapter VII

कृत्यया स महाबाहुरिंहं हन्तुमिहोत्थः ।

जुहावानी शृतं हृद्यं मनेण मुनिससना: ॥ 12 ॥

तत्र कृत्या ससुन्धया च्यवनस्य तपोवलात् ।

प्रबलः पुरुषः कृरी बृहकाक्यो महासुरः ॥ 13 ॥

The powerful Muni then adopted black magic to kill Indra and offered oblations of clarified butter and grains, purified by Mantrams, in the Fire. By the Tapas of Cyavana, of unbounded lustre, there sprang from the sacrificial hearth Krtyā (A female deity to whom sacrifices are offered for destructive and magical purposes). And out of Krtyā originated a very strong person, very cruel and of huge body, a great Demon.

मटो नाम महावोरो भयदः प्राणिनाम्हि ।

शरीरे पर्वताकारसीधारदोषुं घयानकः ॥ १४ ॥

चतरक्षायता दंद्युं योजनानां शर्त शन्मू ।

इते त्वस्य दर्शना बचपुदर्शयोजनाः ॥ 15 ॥

The horrible Demon, named Mada, was terrifying to all the beings. His body was huge like a mountain, teeth very sharp and terrible. Four teeth were hundred Yojanas long each, and his other teeth were ten Yojanas long.

बाहू पर्वतसिक्षानायाती क्ररूर्गीनों ।

जिङ्धा तु भीषणा कृता लेलिहाना नभस्तलम् ॥ 16 ॥

His arms were like mountains, very long and fierce, tongue, horrible, rough and so very long that it reached up to the heavens.

श्रील तु गिरियुकुमा काटिना भीषणा घुणम् ।

नखा व्यानपत्रख्यतः केनाश्रातीव भीषणाः ॥ 17 ॥

His throat was like a mountain peak hard and of a furious appearance; nails resembled tiger's, hair is horrible.

शरीरं कजलाभ च तस्य चारयं भयानकम् ।

नेत्रे दावानलग्रख्ये भीषणेउतिभयानके ॥ 18 ॥

His body was jet black like lamp black; face very terrible, eyes bright like the conflagration fire and awful.

हनुरोका स्थिता तस्य भूमावेका दिवं गता ।

एवंविधः सपुचमो मदो नाम बहुनगु: ॥ 19 ॥

One of his jaws touched the ground and the other

touched the heaven. Thus was born the Demon, named Mada of hug form.

तं विलोक्य सुगा: सर्वे भयमाजामूर्तेमा ।

इन्द्रिपि भयसंज्ञतो यूद्वाय न मनो दधे ॥ 20 ॥

Looking at him, the Devas became suddenly frightened; Indra, too, got very much terrified at the sight of him and did not want to have any more fight. दैत्योर्पि वदने कामं वज्ञमात्य संस्थितः ।

ध्यानं नभो घोरपृटिष्सित्व जगतवर्ग्य ॥ 21 ॥

The Demon swal owed Indra's thunderbolt, looked at the sky, and stood up as if to swallow at once the whole Universe.

स भक्षयिष्यन्सैनुक्दः शनक्रतुभुपार्वत् ।

चुकूगुशु सुग: सर्वे हा हातः: स्मेति संशित्यः ॥ 22 ॥

इत्रः सर्पितबाहुतु मुभुक्षुर्वेग्रमेतिकात् ।

न शशाक पचिं तत्स्मप्रतूत् पाकमासनः ॥ 23 ॥

He became infuriated with rage and pursued Indra to devour him. Seeing this, the Devas cried aloud: "Alas! We are disabled by Mantrams and the outerhood thought he wanted to do so.

वज्रस्तः सुरैणास्तवी बीक्ष्य कालसाधिमम् ।

सस्मार मनसा तत्र गुर्भ समयकोविदम् ॥ 24 ॥

The Lord of the Devas, then, with thunderbolt in his hand, looked on the Demon as Death personified and remembered his Guru, skilled in the knowledge which is the proper time to perform a certain thing.

सरणाद्वाजगामायु बृह्म्यतिहदार्थी: ।

गुरुस्तत्मयं दूद्वा विमतिसदुर्ग मत् ॥ 25 ॥

The liberal-minded Brhaspati knowing the time of imminent danger, and that he is remembered by Indra, at once came to the spot.

विद्यार्थ मनसा कृत्यं तभ्याच गर्जपतिम् ।

दुःसाध्योरयं महाम-नैस्वयं वज्जेण वासव ॥ 26 ॥

He then judged what to do in the present crisis and told Indra: "O Indra! This cannot be averted even by Mantrams; what to speak of of thunderbolt! असुरी मदसंसृत्य यजकुष्ठात्समूषित्वतः ।

तपोबतमुषे: सम्यकन्यबन्य महावल ॥ 27 ॥

This powerful Asura Mada has arisen from the sacrificial hearth by virtue of the Tapas of the Cyavana Muni. The Muni's power is especially felt here. O Lord of the Devas! Nobody, You, I, nor any other Deva will be able to resist him. Know this.

अनिवार्य हुयं शजुस्ववा देखैतथा प्रथा ।

गरणं याहि देतेश च्यवनस्य महात्मनः ॥ 28 ॥

Even Brahmā cannot thwart the anger of one who is devoted to the Śakti, the Highest Force; Cyavana is the Bhakta of the Highest Śakti. So no other body is able to defeat him.

स निवारविता नूतं कृत्यामात्मकृतां किल ।

न विवारवितुं शक्ताः शक्तिभक्तधं ववित् ॥ 29 ॥

He is the man himself to take back the Krtyā that he has originated. There is no doubt in this. So it is better for you now to take the shelter of the Muni."

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तो गुरणा शक्रसदागच्छनुपिं प्रति ।

प्रणाय शिरसा नप्रस्तमुवाच भयास्वितः ॥ 30 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus from his Guru, Indra went to the Muni and bowed down shuddering, before him.

भ्रमस मुनिशाष्टूल शमयासुरमुच्छात् ।

प्रसजो भव सर्वैल वचनं ते कौण्यमूढ् ॥ 31 ॥

सोमाहर्षिक्षिनवेतावरूपभृति भारी ।

भविष्यतः सत्यमेतद्धो विध प्रसीद मे ॥ 32 ॥

O Muni! Forgive me and stop the Asura from his intention to slay the Devas. O All knowing One! Be pleased, I will keep your words. O Bhārgava! The two Aświns will, from this day, have the right to drink the Soma juice. This I speak out to you in truth. O Brāhmana! Be graciously pleased unto me.

मिथ्या ते नोह्यो होय भवत्वेल लोपोन ।

जाने त्वमपि धर्मत्र मिथ्या नेव कार्यस्थि ॥ 33 ॥

सोमपार्विश्नोलौ तल्कृतौ च सदैव हि ।

भविष्यतक्षा शपतिं: कॉर्तिल्ते विपुला भवेत् ॥ 34 ॥

मया यद्रिकृतं कर्म सर्वथा मुनिसन्तम ।

परीक्षार्य तु त्विद्यं तव वीर्घकाशनम् ॥ 35 ॥

O Ascetic! Your intention will never be baffled. Especially I know you to be a knower of Dharma; so, you will never be able to make your word swerve from truth. The two ASvins will, by your grace, be able to drink always the Soma cup; and the King Saryatis fame will also know no bounds. O Muni! Know that what I have done is simply to test your prowess in Tapas.

प्रसादं कुक मे बहान्यदं संहर चोत्थितम् ।

कल्पणं सर्वदैवानां तथा घृते विधीयतान् ॥ 36 ॥

O Brāhmaṇa! Now do this favour to me and take back your, this Demon Mada sprung from your sacrificial hearth and thus do good to all the Devas. There is no doubt in this.

एवमुक्तसु शक्रेना चव्बनः परमार्थवित् ।

संजहार तपःकोपसमुपवं चितीधजम् ॥ 37 ॥

देवमाधाय्य संविन्न भार्थवसु मदं ततः ।

व्यभजल्लीषु पानेपु घृतेषु भुगायासु च ॥ 38 ॥

Thus spoken piteously by Indra, Cyavana, who knew the Highest Reality, drew back within himself the anger arising from the enmity with Indra. Then the Maharṣi Cyavana consoled the Devas that were very much perplexed and anxious out of terror of the Demon named Mada and divided the Asura into four parts (1) female sex, (2) drinking, (3) gambling and (4) hunting animals.

मदं विज्ञज्य देवेन्द्रप्राधास्य चकित्तं भिया ।

संस्थाय च सुरात्मवान्यं तस्य व्यावर्तिन् ॥ 39 ॥

When Mada was thus divided into four parts, the terror stricken Devas felt themselves relived and saved and got consoled.

ततस्तु संस्कृतं सोयं वास्वाय महात्मने ।

अधिष्यां सर्वेधर्तमाय पाययासम सर्पिनः ॥ 40 ॥

Cyavana then placed the Devas in their respective stations and completed the sacrifice. At last, the religious Bhārgava made first Indra and then the two Aśvins drink the Soma Cups.

एवं तो च्चवनेनायाविश्रुनी तदिपुत्रकी ।

विहिती सोमपी राजन-सर्वथा तपसो बलत् ॥ 4 1 ॥

सरसद्वीप विश्यातां जाते पूर्यविमणिकात् ।

आश्रमसु पुनेः सम्यक्युविध्वां विश्वतो5पवत् ॥ 4 2 ॥

Book VII Chapter VIII

O King! Thus Cyavana had the Aśvins their Soma Cups by virtue of his power of Tapas. Theue the tank with the sacrificial post Yūpa became famous and the Muni's Aśrama also was renowned and honoured in all respects on the surface of the earth.

श्यांतिराति संतुहो हाभवतेन कर्मण ।

यजं समाय्य नगरे जगाम सचिवेद्युं: ॥ 4 3 ॥

The King Śaryāti, too, became very glad at this sacrifice and completing the sacrifice returned with his ministers to his city.

राज्यं चकार धर्मज्ञो मनुपुरुः प्रतपायन् ।

आन्तरितस्य पुत्रोभूदानतरिद्धतेनोर्षवत् ॥ 4 4 ॥

The Manu's son, the powerful religious King Śaryāti governed his kingdom, free from any obstacles or other enemies. He had one son named Ānarta; and Ānarta had a son named Revata born to him.

सोउतः समुद्रे नगरी विमियांर कुशस्थतीम् ।

आस्थितो घृताक्रिययानानतौदीनरिदम् ॥ 4 5 ॥

Revata built the city of Kuśasthali in the midst of the ocean and began to live there. He enjoyed all the things in the countries named Ānarta and others.

तस्य पुरगतं जते ककुषिषजोष्ठमुतनम् ।

पूत्री च त्वेति नाना सुन्दरी शुभलक्षण ॥ 46 ॥

Revata had one hundred sons of whom Kakudmi was the eldest and of pure character. He had one daughter very beautiful named Revatī, endowed with all auspicious qualities.

वरयोग्या यदा जाता तदाराजा च रेततः ।

चिन्तयामास राजेन्द्रो राजपुत्रान्कुलोद्धवान् ॥ 47 ॥

When the daughter reached a marriageable age, the King began to think where he could get a prince of a good noble family.

रेततं नाम च गिरिमाश्रितः पृथिवीपति: ।

चकार राज्यं बलवानानंतैनु नराधिधः ॥ 4 8 ॥

That powerful King began to govern his people Anartas, with his headquarters at the Raivata hill. विधिन्य पनसा राजा कृस्ये देवा यम सुता ।

गाला पृष्ठथामि बहाण्यं सर्वत्रं सुर्पृजितम् ॥ 49 ॥

इति सश्रित्यं युपाल: सुतामादाय रेवतीम् ।

बहालोकं जगामाशु प्रडुकाम: चितामहम् ॥ 50 ॥

“Whom to betroth this daughter,” the King thought and settled that he would go to Brahmā and ask him, the venerable omniscient Prajāpati, worshipped by the Devas. Thus the King went with his daughter Revatī to the Brahmaloka.

यत्र देवाङ्ग यज्ञा श्रद्धासि पर्वतासथा ।

अध्यय: सरिताशीय दिध्यकरणयः स्थितः ॥ 51 ॥

There the Devas, Yajñas, Vedas, mountains, oceans and rivers all were shining with luminous bodies.

त्रश्य: सिद्धान्यर्वाः प्रजाग्राणास्तथा ।

तस्थु: प्रांजलयः सर्वे सुव्रन्तघु पुरातना: ॥ 52 ॥

इति श्रीमदैर्शिभाग्रते महापुराणो सत्यनकन्ये

सप्तमोउध्याः ॥ 7 ॥ 11

There the eternal Rsis, Siddhas, Gandharvas, Pannages and Čarṇapas were singing hymns to Brahmā, standing with folded hands.

Here ends the Seventh Chapter of the Seventh Book on the twin Aśvīns (trinking the Soma Cup in Srimaddevībhāgava) am, the Mahā Purānam of 18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

|

Book VII Chapter VIII

CHAPTER VIII

On the King Revata and the Solar Dynasty

जनमेक्य उवाच

संगयो5यं महान्ब्रह्मावर्तैं मप मानते ।

ब्रह्मलोकगतो राजा रेवतीरंसुक्ता स्वयम् ॥ १ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Brāhmaṇa! The King was a Kṣattriya; how could he go himself with his

daughter Revati to the Brahmaloka (the abode of Brahmā)? I entertain a great doubt on this point.

मया पूर्वे शूनं कृत्कनं ब्रह्मोण्यः कबान्ते ।

ब्राह्मणो ब्रह्माधिष्यन्तो ब्रह्लोकमवान्युन्त ।। २ ॥

राजा कर्वं गततरतर रेवतीसंयुतः स्वयम् ।

सत्योकें3तिद्यूपरे भूर्नैकादिति संशयः ॥ ३ ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

I heard of yore while conversing about matters connected with the Brāhmins, that the Brāhmin only who self-restrained and the knower of Brahman could alone go to the Brahmaloka. The Satyaloka is very hard for the worldly people to go; so I doubt how the king could have gone with Revatī from the Bhūrloka to the Satyaloka.

भृत: स्वर्गमवाणोति सर्वग्रासेषु निरणयः ।

मानुषेण तु देते बहतफोके गति: कथम् ।

Man, when he discards his body, can go to the Heavens. So is recited in all the Śāstras. How then, people, while, in their human bodies can go to the Brahmaloka.

खर्गाल्युनः कर्थ लोके मानुषे जायते गति: ॥ 4 ॥

एतन्ने संगयं विद्रेश्तेनुमहिंस संग्रान् ।

यथा गता गतस्तत्र प्रयुकामः प्रजापतिन् ॥ 5 ॥

So cut asunder my doubts how the King Revata could go to the Brahma loka to ask the Prajāpati on certain matters.

व्यास उवाच

मेरोस्तु शिजारे राजन्सर्वें लोका: प्रतिभिताः ।

इन्ट्रलोको वाहिलोको या च संबमपी पूर्णी ॥ 6 ॥

तवैव सत्यलोकश्र केलासक्ष तथा पुनः ।

वैकुण्डछु पुनस्तत्र वैष्णवं पदमुच्यते ॥ 7 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! On the top of the mountain Sumeru, are located the Indra's heavens called Amarāvatī (the abode of the Immortals) the Samyamani city of Yama, the Satyaloka, the Vahniloka, the Kailāsa, Vaikuntha the abode of Viṣṇu and others.

यथाजुनैः शकलोके गतः पार्थों धनुधरः ।

पशु वर्षाणि कौतोनः स्थितस्तत्र सुरालये ॥ 8 ॥

मानुपेणिव देहेन वासवस्य च सविष्टी ।

तथेवायैरिध भूपानतः ककुल्वयमुपुजा: किल ॥ 9 ॥

The great archer Arjuna, the son of Prthā, went to the Indraloka and spent five years there. In ancient times, the Kings Kakuttha and others went to Indraloka, in their human bodies.

स्वलोकानतः पशाद्विधाशी महाकावतः ।

जित्वेन्सदनं प्राय सँस्थितास्तत्र कामतः ॥ 10 ॥

महाधिषः पुरा राजा ब्रह्मलोकं गतः स्वाद् ।

आगच्छतीं नृयो गंगामपर्यच्चातिसुन्दीय ॥ १ १ ॥

Even the powerful Daityas used to conquer the Indraloka and resided there at their will and pleasure. In ancient times, when the sovereign of the earth, the King Mahābhisa went to the Brahmaloka, the most beautiful Gāngā also was coming to the Brahmaloka.

वायुर्नांबरम्यस्तु दैवादपहतं नृप । किंचित्रान्ना नृपेणाय दृष्टा सा सुन्दरी तथा ॥ १२ ॥ सितं चकार कामातैः सा च किंचिज्जहास् वै ।

ब्रहणा तौ तदा दृष्टी शणतो जाती वसुंधगम् ॥ १३ ॥

On the way the King saw Her. O King! Accidentally at that time her clothing were cast aside by the wind; the King saw her partly in her naked state, and, overpowered with lust, smiled; Gāngā also smiled. Seeing the states of them, Brahmā instantly cursed them; and they had to come in this world and take their births.

बैकुण्ठेडीप सुरा: सर्वे पीडिता देत्यदानये: ।

गत्वा हरि जनगाथयमलुच्कमलायातिम् ॥ 14 ॥

सन्देहो नात्र कन्धेल: स्वांगा नृपसतम ।

गयाम: सर्वेर्पि लोका: सूर्मनिवानों नारीधि ॥ 15 ॥

All the Devas, when oppressed by the Dānavas, went to Vaikuntha and sang hymns to Hari, the Lord of Kamalā. O King! Men can go to all the Lokas; in fact those high-souled men that perform Yajñas or severe asceticisms and thus have acquired great merits, those performers of Sacrifices and ascetics surely go to the Heavens.

अव्ययं कृतपुर्णनां तापसानां नाराधि ।

पुण्यसूद्राव एवारा गमने कारणं नृप ॥ 16 ॥

तथैव यजमानानां यजेन भाषितात्मनाम् ।

O King! It is only the abundance of good merits that is the only cause of going to Heavens. So you ought not to entertain any doubts on this.

जनमेव अन्य

रेवातो रेवाती कन्यां गृहीत्वा चाहलोचनाम् ॥ 17 ॥

बह्वालेकं गत: पश्ञाक्के कृतं तेन धृषुज्ञ।

Book VII Chapter VII

ब्रद्गण किं समादिष्ट कम्पै दत्ता सुता पुनः ॥ 18 ॥

तल्पवं विस्तारबुद्धान्काय त्वं मामधान्ना ।

Janamejaya said: O Muni! The King Revata went with his daughter Revatī to the Brahmaloka; but what did he do when he went there? What did Brahmā order him? And to whom did the King betroth his daughter, when ordered by Brahmā? O Brāhmanā! Speak out all these in details to me now.

व्यास उवाच

निशामय महीपाल राजा रेवतक: किल ॥ 19 ॥

पुर्या वरं परिषुं बढ़ालोकं नातो यदा ।

आवर्तमाने गान्यर्य स्थितो लक्ष्यक्षणः क्षणम् ॥ 20 ॥

भृणवत्रतृयद्धृघाता सभायां तु सकन्यकः ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hear. When the King went to Brahmaloka to ask about the proper bridegroom of his daughter, there was going on singing and music; so he waited a while to find an opportunity when the assembly would have a leisure; but he was so very pleased with music that he could not desist from hearing it till the end.

समाप्ते तत्र नाभ्यई प्रणाय्य परमेध्वरम् ॥ 21 ॥

हर्षीयिता सुता तस्ये साधिपार्यं न्यदेवत् ।

When the music was finished, the King bowed down to Brahmā and showed him his daughter and informed Him of his intention.

राजोवाच

वर्ग कथय देदेग कन्येय मप चुकिता ॥ 22 ॥

देया कस्मै मया ब्रह्न्यूषु त्वां समुपागतः ।

बहवो राजपुत्रा मे वीक्षिताः: कुलसंपवाः: ॥ 23 ॥

कर्मधीछ्य मेमः कामं नोपातिष्ठित चक्रतम् ।

The King said: “O Deva! This good daughter is mine; now kindly say who will be her bridegroom. O Brahmā! To whom shall I betroth this daughter? I have come to you to ask on this point. I have searched for many princes and seen also a good many of them and none of them is to my liking and so my mind is not at rest.

तस्माचां देवदेवेश प्रष्ट्रभागातोउस्मह्मूं ॥ 24 ॥

तद्वात्राय सर्वैत्र योग्यं राजसुतं वरम् ।

कुलीनं बलवन्तं च सर्वतक्षणसंयुतम् ॥ 25 ॥

दातातं धर्मशीलं च राजयुं समाधिरा ।

O Lord of Devas! Therefore I have come to you. Kindly select one bridegroom for her. He is to be a Kulina (of good family), powerful, religious, liberal, and a prince endowed with all auspicious qualities. This is my prayer."

ध्यास उवाच

तदाकरणं जगत्कर्तों बेचनं नृपतेस्तदा ॥ 26 ॥

तमुवाच हसन्वाययं यद्धवो कालस्य पयंवम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Brahmā, the lotus born, the Creator of the world, hearing these words, laughed, thinking that a very long interval had passed away. He then said:

ब्रहीावाच

राजपूत्रास्वया राजन्वरं ये हृदये कृतः ॥ 27 ॥

प्रस्ता: कालेन वे सर्वे सिवपूर्णैवबाधायः ।

O King! The princes that you thought would become the bridegroom of your daughter, all died; their sons and grandsons and their friends even have all passed away.

सप्तविंशरामोड़हीय द्वारस्तु प्रवर्तते ॥ 28 ॥

वंशजासे मृताः सर्वे पुरी दैर्वैर्वितुक्ति ।

सोमवर्शी.द्वावस्तर तथा राज्य प्रणास्ति हु ॥ 29 ॥

उप्रमेने इति ख्यातो मयुगाधिपति: किल ।

The twenty-seventh Manvantara of the Dvāpara Yuga is now going on, so none of the princes of your family are now existing. The Daityas sacked the King of Mathurā, is reigning in that place.

ययातिवंशसम्पृतो राजा माधुयमण्डले ॥ 30 ॥

उद्यनेतान्मतः कंस: सुरुद्धी महाबलः ।

दैन्येश: पितरं सोर्धि कारागारे न्येशगवत् ॥ 31 ॥

स्वयं राज्यं चकातसौ नृपाणां मदगवितः ।

मेदिती चातिभातां ब्रहाणं गरणं गता ॥ 32 ॥

दुष्टाराज्यसैन्यानां भारणांतिसमाकुला ।

अंगावततर्णं तत्र गतिद्त सुसततमैः ॥ 33 ॥ ॥

He belongs to the illustrious lunar family of Yayāti. His son, the powerful Karīsa, born of a

Dānava, began to do injuries always to the Devas; he threw his own father to the prison. Becoming very haughty, he began to govern himself the countries of other kings and began to tyrranise over the subjects. O King! The Earth became so much troubled by the armies of the wicked Demon Kings, that She became quite unable to bear further loads. So She went to seek refuge to Brahmā.

वासुदेव समुपघः कृष्णः कमललोचनः ।

देवक्वां देवसुधिण्यां योउसी नारायणोयुः नि 3 4 1 ||

तपश्वार दुःसाध्ये धर्मपुः: सन्तानतः ।

संगतीरां नसुरखा: पुण्ये बदिरिकाभेमे ॥ 3 5 ॥

Brahmā and the other Devas then began to say: "O Earth! To remove your burdens, the lotus-eyed Nārāyaṇa will incarnate Himself as part incarnation in the form of Śri Krṣṇa. He who is Nārāyaṇa practised in ancient times, as the son of Dharma, a very severe asceticism, in company with his brother Nara in the sacred hermitage of Badarī.

सोउवतीर्णं यदुकुले वासुदेवोप्र विश्रुतः ।

तेनासी नितहः पापः केसः कृष्णोस सन्ति ॥ 36 ॥

Now this very Deva is born in the great family of Yadu in the womb of Devakī by Vasudeva and is now celebrated by the name of Vāsudeva."

उप्रसेनाय राज्यं वै दर्तं दल्या खलं सुतम् ।

कंसस्य श्रुगुरः पापो ज्ञासंधी महावलः ॥ 37 ॥

O King! He has slain that vicious wicked Kanisa and has installed Ugrasena in his place. The very powerful Jarāsandha, the vicious King of Magadha, is the father-in-law of Karīsa. On hearing the death of his son-in-law, he became in infuriated with rage came to Mathurā, and raged a terrible war.

अगत्य मवृरा कोधाचकार संरं पूरा ।

कृष्णोनासी सितः संख्ये जरासीधे महावलः ॥ ३ ८ ॥

प्रेयवायास युद्धाय सवलं यवंन ततः ।

शुवायानं महूलूरं ससैच्यं यवतानिधपम् ॥ ३ ९ ॥

Vāsudeva defeated in a battle that Jarāsandha, proud of his mighty valour. Though defeated, Jarāsandha sent Kālayavana with his host of army to fight again with Krṣṇa. Bhārgava Vāsudeva,

when he heard that the King of Yavanas arrived, sent away all the members of his family and the Yādavas to Dvārkā and began to wait with his brother Balarāma for the Yavana King. Then he went alone to the camp of Yavana and led him away to a mountain cave where was sleeping the King Mucukunda and had then the Yavana King slain by Mucukunda.

कृष्णसुं मदुगतं त्यकत्वा पुरुरं द्वारवतीमगात् ।

प्रभानां तां पुरीं कृष्ण: शिलियपि: सह मंसी: ।

कार्यामास दुगांखी हडुशालाविमणिद्धात् ।

जीणोंद्वारं पुरः कृत्वा वासुदेवः प्रापवान् ।

उउसेनं च राजानं चकार वारणवर्तिनम् ॥ १ ॥

याद्वान्स्थाप्यामास द्वारलयं यदुतमः ।

वासुदेवस्तु तजाछ वर्तते बान्यदेः सह ॥ 4 0 ॥

Kṛṣṇa then went to Dvārkā. The city of Dvārkā was then in a dilapidated condition. Kṛṣṇa brought together the artists and got built exquisitely the beautiful palaces, forts, and markets and stalls, etc., and so added to the beauty of the place. That Vāsudeva, of mighty prowess, thus improving the city, made Ugrasena the King of that place; and Kṛṣṇa is now waiting there with his friends.

तस्याप्र: स विश्वगतो बलदेचो हलायुधः ।

गेशांगो मुसली बीगे वरोसंसु तव संमतः ॥ 4 1 ॥

His elder brother Baladeva, the carrier of the plough, is celebrated. Thus he with Muśala in his hands is a great warrior and the part incarnation of Ananta Deva.

संकर्षणाय देहागुण कन्यां कमललोचनाम् ।

रेकर्ती बलभघ्राय विवाहविधिना ततः ॥ 42 ॥

He is the fit bridegroom of your daughter. So give your daughter in marriage, without any delay, according to the rules of the marriage ceremony to Sankarşana Balabhadra.

दत्वा पुत्री तृपक्ष्र पाछ्छ लं बदारिकाओमम् ।

तपस्तनुं सुरातमं पावनं कामदं नृणम् ॥ 43 ॥

After giving your daughter in marriage, go to the hermitage of Badari and practise tapasyā. That sacred retreat is the (park) recreation ground of the

Book VII Chapter VIII

Devas, holy and yielding to human beings the objects of their desires.

व्यास उवाच

इति राजा समादिष्ठी ब्रह्माण पच्योनिना ।

जगाम तसरा राजन्द्राकों कन्यायन्ति: ॥ 44 ॥

ददौ तां बलदेवाय कन्यां वै शुभतक्षणाम् ।

ततसस्त्वा तपस्तीत्रं नृपति: कालपर्ये ॥ 45 ॥

जगाम तिःदशावासं त्यक्त्वा देहं सरित्ते ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus ordered by the lotus-born Brahmā, the King went to Dvārakā with his daughter. Reaching there he gave over his all auspicious daughter in marriage to Baladeva duly according to the rules and regulations. At last, according to Brahmā's injunction, he became engaged in severe austerities in the Badarikāśrama and, when the time of death arrived, left off his mortal coil on the banks of the river and went to the world of Gods.

जनमेव जवाच

भगवान्महाद्धार्थ भवता समुदाहतम् ॥ 46 ॥

रेवतस्तु चित्यतन्त्र ब्रह्मलोके सुतावंतः ।

युगानां तु गतं तत्र शनमघोत्रं चिकल ॥ 47 ॥

कन्या कुद्धा न संज्ञात तरका वाजिततां नु किम ।

एतावंतं तथा कालमायुः पूर्ण तयोः कवम् ॥ 48 ॥

Janamejaya said: O Bhagavan! You have uttered one wonderful thing. One hundred and eight Yugas passed away when the King of Revata with his daughter was deeply absorbed in hearing music in the Brahmaloka yet neither the good King nor the daughter did get sufficiently old. How could this be! How could they have lived so long! Were their longevities ordained to be such a long period!

यास उवाच

न जरा श्रुतिपासा वा न मृतूनं भयं पुनः ।

न तु लाना: प्रभवति ब्रह्मलोके सद्धान्य ॥ 49 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Brahmaloka is not touched by any vice nor sin; old age, hunger, thirst or fear of death nothing exists there; nor is there any other cause by which weariness comes. So what

doubt there can be that the people there will be long-lived, free from old age and death!

मेहं गतस्य शयिते: सन्ती राक्षसैहिता ।

गता: कुसास्यती लक्चल भवभीता इतस्ततः ॥ 50 ॥

मनोक्ष क्ष्वतः पुत्र उन्यको वीर्यवंततः ।

इक्षाकुपिति विद्यानतः सूर्येशंगकस्तु सः ॥ 51 ॥

When the King Śaryāti went up to the Heavens, his sons were all destroyed by the Rāksasas; those that remained, they, terrified left Kuśasthalī and fled on all sides. Vaivāsvata Manu sneized; owing to that, came out of his nose one powerful son; his name was Iksvāku.

वंशार्थ तय आतिरुक्छेरीं छायाला निरंतरम् ।

नारदस्योपदेशेन प्रायः दीक्षामनुतमम् ॥ 52 ॥

तस्य पुत्रगतं रावजिश्वकांकीरिति विश्रुतम् ।

विकृक्षिः प्रथमस्तेषां व्रतवीर्यमन्त्रितः ॥ 53 ॥

He spread the Solar dynasty and became celebrated. Getting excellent initiation from the Maharsi Nārada, he began to meditate the Devi constantly and practised severe tapasyā for the spread of his race. O King! Ikyāvāu had one hundred sons; Vūkṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣṭiṣटि.

अयोभ्यायां स्थिति राजा इक्यावुर्गिति विश्वतः ।

शकुनिमुभुक्ता: पुत्र: पंचागघृतवलतर: ॥ ५४ ॥

उत्तरायपदोशस्य रथिततः कृततः किल ।

Ikyāvāu became king and lived in Ayodhyā. He sent his fifty sons, the powerful Śakuni and others to Uttarāpatha (Eastern) provinces for governing those countries.

दक्षिणस्यां तथा राज्ञानिद्रासतेन ते सुता: ॥ 55 ॥

चत्वारिणशापाठी च रक्षणार्थ महात्मना ।

अन्यी द्वौ संस्थिती पार्थे सेवार्थ तस्य घूपते: ॥ 56 ॥

इति श्रीमद्धैर्षिगागते महापुराणे सप्तमकन्दोर्धुगोस्थाव: ॥ 8 ॥ 11

That high-souled monarch hath sent also other eight sons to govern the countries in the South. (Western). O King! He kept the remaining two sons by his side for his own service.

Here ends the Eight Chapter of the Seventh Book on

the story of the King Revata and the spread of the

Solar dynasty in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevī-bhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER IX

On the Story of Kākustha and on Māndhātā

व्यास उवाच

कदापिद्रष्टाकशाड़े विकृक्षिं पृथिवीपति: ।

आज्ञायवसतसंभुको मांसमानय सत्त्वम् ॥ १ ॥

मेरयं शाळ्यार्थभूना वने गत्वा सुतादरात् ।

इत्युक्तोउसी तथेत्याशु जगाम वनमस्त्रभृत् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Once on a time, the time for Aṣṭaka Śrāddha (the funeral ceremony in honour of the departed) arrived. Seeing this, the King Iśvāku ordered his son Vikukṣi: “O Child! Go immediately to the forest and bring carefully pure sanctified meat for the Śrāddha purpose; see, that there be no neglect of duty. Thus ordered, Vikukṣi instantly went to the forest equipped with arms.

गत्या ज्ञान बाणी: स वराहान्युकारान्यगान् ।

शगर्शधारि परिश्रोतो बभूवाथ बुभूश्रितः ॥ 3 ॥

विम्मृता चाहका तत्प शगं चादद्वी वने ।

गंपं निवेदवामास पिधे मांसमन्तनम् ॥ 4 ॥

He hunted in the forest lots of boars, pigs, deer, and hare. But he was so very tired with his journey in the forest and got so hungry that he forgot everything about the Asṭaka Śrāddha and ate one hare there in the forest. The remaining excellent meat he brought and handed over to his father.

प्रोक्षाय समानीत मांतं दृष्ट्या गुरुस्तदा ।

अनहिंमिति नज्ञाना चुकोप मुनिसत्तमः ॥ 5 ॥

When that meat was brought to be sprinkled for purification, the family priest Vasistha, on seeing it, at once came to know that some portion had already been eaten and it was the remaining part.

भुक्तशंत्रु न आद्रे प्रोक्षणीयमिति स्थिति: ।

राझे निवेदयामास वसिष्ठः पाकदूषणम् ॥ 6 ॥

The leavings of food are not fit for the sprinkling purpose; this is the Śāstric rule. Vasiṣṭha informed the King of this defect in the food.

पुत्रस्य कर्म तन्त्राला भूपतिंगुभोदितम् ।

चुकोप विधित्योपातं देशाप्रिः:सारयस्ततः ॥ 7 ॥

In accordance with the Guru's advice, the King coming to know thus the violation of the rule by his son, became very angry and banished his son from his kingdom.

शग्राद इति विद्यतो नाना जातो नृपात्मजः ।

गतो वने शग्रादस्तु चितृकोपादसंधमः ॥ 8 ॥

वय्ने वर्तयक्लं नीतावान्यमंतलर: ।

The prince became known from that time as Śaśāda; he did not become the least sorry for his father's anger; he went to the forest and gladly remained there.

चितपुरंने राज्य प्राणतं तेन महात्मना ॥ 9 ॥

शशाद्रवककोरात्रामयोधायाः पति: स्वयम् ।

He gladly passed his time absorbed in religion and sustained his life on forest fruits and roots. After sometime when his father died, he inherited his kingdom.

यज्ञाननेकशः पूर्णांशकार सर्यतुटे ॥ 10 ॥

शशाद्रयाभवत्सुः ककुल्य इति विशुतः ।

तस्यैव नामभेदाड़े इंद्रवाहः पुरंवयः ॥ 11 ॥

On becoming the King of Ayodhyā, Śaśāda had only one son; he became famous in the three lokas by the name of Kakutsha. He was known also by other names Indravāha and Puranājaya.

जनमेव जयार

नामधेदः कर्थ जातो राजपुरस्य चानप्र ॥

कारणं बृहि मे सर्वे कर्मणा येन चाधवत् ॥ 12 ॥

Janamejaya said: O Holy One! How and why was the prince named Kakutsha. Why was he known by the two other names? Speak all this to me.

व्यास उवाच

शगादे सर्वाती राजा ककुल्य इंत्ति चाभवत् ।

राज्य चकार धर्मज्ञ: विपुर्वीमार्थ बलात् ।

एतसिभजतेरे देवा दैन्यैः सर्वे पराजितः ॥ 13 ॥

जगमुक्तिलोकाप्रियत विज्ञा ग्रणमाव्यव्यम् ।

तान्योवाच महाविष्युक्तदा देवान्सनातनः ॥ 14 ॥

Book VII Chapter IX

Vyāsa said: O King! When Śaśāda went to the Heavens, Kakutstha became king. That religious king then began to govern the country of his father and grandfather with an authority supported by a powerful arm. At this time the Devas suffered a defeat from the Dānavas and took refuge to Viṣṇu, the Infallible and the Lord of the three worlds. The eternal great Viṣṇu full of intelligence and bliss then addressed the Devas:

विष्णुराच

पाणिण्याहं महीपालं प्रार्थवन्तु शाशाद्वान् ।

स हनिष्यति वे दैत्यानंभामे सुरसतम १ १ ५ ॥

आगथीयति धर्मोसा साहाय्यार्थ धनुधरः ।

पराशनैः प्रसादेन सामर्थं तस्य चालुम् ॥ १ ६ ॥

Visṇu said: “O Devas! Go and pray to the King Śaśāda. He will be your ally and kill all the Demons. That King is religious; especially he is a worshipper of the Highest Śakti. He is good archer and will come to help you. His strength is immense.

हरे: सुवचनादेवा ययु: सर्वे सवासवा: ।

अलोध्यायां महात्राज शगतदानंय प्रति ॥ 17 ॥

तानगतान्युराजाज पूजयामास धर्मतः ।

पण्छलामाने राजा प्रयोजनमतिई: ॥ 18 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Indra and the other Devas hearing the nectar like words of Hari went to Ayodhyā, to Kakuttsha, the son of Śaśāda. Seeing the Devas at his palace, the king worshipped them duly and with great care; and he asked them why they had come there.

राजोवाच

ध-योड़ं पावितशामि जीवितं सक्लं मम ।

यदागत्य गृहे देवा ददुक्षा दर्जानं महत् ॥ १९ ॥

ब्रुवंतु कृत्यं देयेशा दुःसाध्यमपि मानतेः ।

करिष्ठामि महत्कार्य सर्वेधा भवतां महत् ॥ २० ॥

The King said: O Devas! When you have favoured me by your presence here, I am blessed and sanctified; my life is crowned with success. Say what I can do for you; I will carry it out even if it be very hard for me to perform."

देवा कचुः

साहाय्यं कुरा राजेन्द्र सखा भव शर्पीपतेः ।

संग्रामे जय दैत्येंग्रानुर्जांशिक्रदारीपि ॥ २१ ॥

पराशक्तप्रसादेत वुतर्भे नासित ते क्वचित् ।

विष्णुना प्रेरिताश्वेमानातस्व सक्रियो ॥ २२ ॥

The Devas said: “O Prince! Please help and back us and defeat the Daityas, invincible by the Devas and form an alliance with Indra. O King! By the grace of the Highes Sakti, you have nothing unattained anywhere; so we have come to you by the order of Viṣnu.”

पंजीवाच

पाणिग्राहो भावमयद्ध दैवानां सुरसतमाः।

इन्द्रे मे वाहनं तन भवेहादि सुपाधिपः ॥ 23 ॥

The King said: "O Devas! I can back you and become your ally if Indra carries me on his back in the time of war.

संग्रामं तु कार्वियामि देवैर्वैकृतोर्धपुना ।

आक्रड़ोंर नायियाभि सत्योतद्वयोम्हमू ॥ 24 ॥

I will fight nor with the Daityas but for the Devas; but I will go to the battle-field on Indra's back; this I speak to you truly.

तदोषुर्वास्व देवा: कर्तव्ये कार्यम्बद्धुतम् ।

पत्र भव नरेन्द्रस्य त्वक्वल जलजा शर्चापते ॥ 25 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King The Devas then spoke to Indra: "O Lord of Śacī! It is now your bounden duty to do this; so quitting shame, be a carrier to this king."

लज्जमानरलत शक्र: प्रेरितो हरिण भुगम् ।

वधूव वृषभस्तूर्णं क्रस्येवापरो महान् ॥ 26 ॥

Indra got ashamed very much, but being requested frequently by Hari, at last assumed the appearance of a bull like the great Bull of Śiva.

तमाहरोह राज्ञसौ संधामगानाय वे ।

चित्रा: कुकुदि येनाय कुकुर्म्यसेन चापवल ॥ 27 ॥

The King mounted on that bull to go to the war; he fought while taking his seat on the hump on the shoulders of the bull (Kakuda); therefore he was named Kakustha.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

इन्ही वाहः कृतो येन तेन नामेन्द्रवाहकः ।

पुरो जितातृ दैवानां तेनभुच्छ पुरंजय: ॥ 28 ॥

जिवा दैवाभ्यदावाहुर्धनं तेषां प्रतचान् ।

पप्रच्छ चैवं राजर्षेति सख्यं बभूव ह् ॥ 29 ॥

The King was carried by Indra on his back hence he was named Indravāha; he conquered the Dānavas in battle; hence he was called Puranājaya. The powerful King defeated the Dānavas and gave away all their wealth to the Devas. He bade farewell to the Devas and returned to his own kingdom. Thus the alliance was formed with Indra.

कुतुस्थोक्षातिविख्यातो नृपतिसत्य संशजाः ।

काकुत्स्था भृति राजानो बभूवुर्बुईविश्रतः ॥ ३० ॥

O King! Kakuttsha became very celebrated on this earth; his descendants became kings and were known as Kakutsthas and were all very famous here on this Earth.

कुकुत्स्थ्याभवतुजो धर्मपत्न्यो महावलः ।

अनेन विश्रुतस्तस्य पृषुः पुत्रछा वीर्यान् ॥ 31 ॥

Kakutstha had one powerful son, named Kakutstha by his legal wife; Kakutstha had the son Prthu, of mighty prowess.

विष्योगेर्य मसूतः साक्षात्यरागतिन्पदाचंकः ।

विश्रधित्यु विज्ञेय: पृथोः पुत्रो नराधिणः ॥ 32 ॥

Prthu was the part incarnation personified of Visnu, and worshipper of the feet of the Supreme Śakti. His son was Visāvarandhi; he became king and governed the kingdom.

चन्द्रस्तस्य सुतः श्रीमात्राजा शंकरः स्मृतः ।

तत्सुती युवानश्रस्तु तेजस्वी बलवततः ॥ 33 ॥

His son was Candra; he came to be king, governed his subject and multiplied very much his issues.

ग्रावन्ती युवनाश्रय जजे परमधार्मिकः ।

ग्रावर्ती निमिति तेन पुरी शक्रपुरीसामा ॥ 34 ॥

Yuvanāśva was one of his sons; he was very powerful and spirited. Śavanta was the son of Yuvanāśva; he was very religious. He built a nice city named Śāvanti like the Paradise of Indra.

ब्रह्मधस्तु पुत्रोःभूष्ठावर्तेस यहातनः ।

कुलवायाथः सुतस्तस्य बभूव पृथिवीपति: ॥ 35 ॥

ध्रुभुभागा हतो देत्यसंतनासी पृथिवीतले ।

ध्रुभुभारोति विल्यालां नाम प्रभातिविश्रुन्ति ॥ 36 ॥

Brihadasâva was the son of the high-souled Śāvanta; he had a son Kuvalayâśva. He became the Lord of the earth by the power of his arms. He killed Dhundu Dānava; so he was very much celebrated by the name of Dhundumâra.

पुत्रसत्यय दुर्बाश्रनु पालायामास मेदैनीम् ।

दूदाश्रय सून: श्रीमान्वर्थ श्रुत कीर्तिन: ॥ 37 ॥

निकुभभत्सूनु: प्रोन्नो बयूपू पृथिवीपितः ।

वर्णाश्धी निकुम्भस्य कृशाश्रस्तय वे सूनः ॥ 38 ॥

His son Drḍhāśva; he governed the earth; His son was Śrimān Haryāśva. His son was Nikumbha; he became the King. Nikumbha had his son Varhaṇāśva. Kriśāśva was his son.

प्रसेनशिकृष्णाश्रय बलवानसत्यविक्रमः ।

तस्य उपो महापाणो यौवनार्थिति विश्रतः ॥ 39 ॥

His son was the powerful Paseanjit; his son's prowess knew no bounds. Parasenajit had the fortunate son Yauvanāśva.

यौवनाश्रुतः श्रीमान्यांधातेति महीपति: ।

अष्टोत्तमसहं तु प्रासादा येन निर्मितः ॥ 40 ॥

O fortunate One! The son of Yauvanāśva was Śrimān Māndhātā; he became the Lord of the Earth and for the satisfaction of the Devi Bhagavatī had one thousand and eight palaces built in Benares and in the other chief places of pilgrimages.

भगवत्यान्तु तुर्धध्यं महातीध्यु मानद ।

मातृगर्भे न जातो:सावुर्वज्ञो जनकोदो ॥ 41 ॥

नि:सारितस्ततः पुः: कृषिं मिन्चा मिछु: पुनः ।

Māndhāṭā was not born of his mother's womb but was born in the belly of his father. Then the ministers tore asunder the belly of his father and got him out.

राजोबाच

न शुतं न च दृष्टं वा भवता तदुदाहतम् ॥ 42 ॥

असंभावं महाभाग तस्य जन यथोदितम् ।

विस्तेण वद्यवाहा मंधातुर्जन्माकरणम् ॥ 43 ॥

राजोदेर यथोतयः पुत्र: सर्वांगसुन्दरः ।

Janamejaya said: O fortunate One! What you

Book VII Chapter IX

said was never seen nor heard ever before since. This sort of births is highly improbable. How was that beautiful son born in the belly of his father? Describe this in detail and satisfy my curiosity.

यास उचाच

यौवनाश्रीउपच्यो5पूरज्ञा परमधामिक: ॥ 44 ॥

भार्थिणां च शर्त तस्य बथूव नृपतैनृप ।

राजा चित्तापर: प्रायश्रित्त्यामास निवल्यः ॥ 45 ॥

अपत्यर्थं यौवनाश्री कुःवित्तनु वर्न गतः ।

ग्रहीणामाओंपुण्ये निर्विलणः स च पार्थिवः ॥ 46 ॥

मुगोच कु:वित्तः श्रासांतोपासनां च पश्यतान् ।

दूद्वा तुं कुःवित्त विग्रा बभ्यूढ़ा कृपालतः ॥ 47 ॥

तमूच्ज्रांणा राजकन्समाख्छोचिति पार्थिति ।

किं ते दुःखं महाराज बृह्नि सत्यं मनोतमान् ॥ 48 ॥

प्रतीकारं क्रियामो दुःखस्य तत्व सर्वथा ।

Vyāsa said: O King! The King Yauvanāśva had one hundred queens; yet he had no issues. The King always thought much about his son. Once the King, sorry and desirous of a son, went to the holy retreats of the Rṣis. On arriving there, he began frequently to respire heavily before the ascetics. The Rṣis became filled with pity on seeing his sorrowful condition. O King! The Brāhmins then said to him: O King! Why are you thus sorrowful and distressed? What is your sorrow that is troubling your heart? Speak truly. We will surely redress your grievance.”

यौवानश उवाच

राज्यं धनं सदश्रश्छ वर्णैतं मुनयो मम ॥ ४ १ ॥

भायाणी च शर्त शुर्द्रं वर्तैत विशादप्रभम् ।

Yauvanāśva said: “O Munis! I have got the kingdom, wealth, excellent horses, one hundred illustrious chaste wives.

नारातिक्षु लोकेषु कोउध्यस्त बलवानाम ॥ ५० ॥

आज्ञाकास्तु सामंतत खर्तैने मन्थिणतस्ता ।

I have no enemies in the three worlds; no one is stronger than me. All the Kings and ministers are obedient to my call.

एकं सन्तानजं दुःखं नान्यतश्यामि तापसा: ॥ ५१ ॥

अपुत्रस्य गतिनार्िित स्थगों नैव च नैव च ।

तस्याच्छोभापि विघेज्ञानः सन्तानार्थे भृगतं ततः ॥ ५२ ॥

वेद्रगात्वारितच्ज्ञातपासधा कृतघ्रभाः ।

But O Ascetics! I have no son; this my sonless states is the only cause of my pain and sorrow. It has marred all my happiness. See! The persons that have no son cannot in any way go to Heavens. Therefore I am always being pained for this. You all are ascetic; you have taken great pains to learn the essence of the Veda Śāstras.

इर्हि सन्तानकामस्य वृक्तां ज्ञाला दिशिणुं मे ॥ 53 ॥

कुर्वन्तु मम कार्य वै कृष्णो चन्दिति तपायाः ।

So kindly order me what sacrifice is fit for me to have a son. O Ascetics! If you any pity for me, kindly perform this good work for me."

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला वचनं राजः कृपया पूर्णमानसः ॥ 54 ॥

कारयामासुर्वयास्तस्येशिभिन्द्रवेतानम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the words of the King, they were all filled with pity; and, with fullness of mind, made him to perform the sacrifice whose presiding deity was Indra.

कलगः स्थापितस्तत्र गतपूर्णास्तु वादधैः ॥ 55 ॥

मन्त्रितो वेदनार्थे घुर्जार्थ तस्य प्रपृतः ।

For the sake of the King, that he may get a son born to him, they had a jar filled with water by the Brāhmins and purified and charged that jar with the Vedic Mantrams.

राजा तर्डासूनं प्रविष्टसूचितो निशि ॥ 56 ॥

विज्ञानूद्वा शयानान्स पपी मन्त्रजलं स्वयम्।

The King got thirsty in the night and entered in the sacrificial ground; seeing the Brāhmins asleep, the King himself drank that water, surcharged with the Mantram.

भार्थ्योंर संकृतं विश्रमेमन्त्रित विधिनोद्युक्तम् ॥ 57 ॥

पीतं राजा तृषान्तेन तद्धानान्वुषोतम् ।

The Brāhmins consecrated and kept that water apart, according to due rules, surcharging with Mantrams, for the wife of the King; but the King,

getting thirsty, himself drank that water unconsciously.

སྟུང་ཆོས་ཀྱི་སྐྱོང་ཀྱི་སྐྱོང་ཀྱི་སྐྱོང་ཀྱི་སྐྱོང་

प्रश्रब्छूते रूपे केन पीते जलमिति बिज्ञा: 1

राजा पीते विदित्वा ते नात्वा दैवबलं भक्ति। ५ ९ ॥

Next morning the Brāhmins seeing the jar of water empty, were started very much with fear; the Brāhmins then asked the King: Who drank the water?

श्रौं सामायमासुरीतासे मुनयो गृहान् ।

गर्भे द्वारा नृपतिस्तन्ते मन्बलाद्धि ॥ 60 ॥

ततः काले स उतयः कुझिं भिन्तास्य दक्षिणाम् ।

When they came to know that the King himself drank the water, the Munis thought this to be an act of Daiva (Fate) and completing the sacrifice returned to their abodes. Then the King became pregnant by the power of the sacrificial Mantrams.

पूरं निष्कासयामापूर्णेनितस्य भूपतेः ॥ 6 1 ॥

देवानां कृपया तत्र न ममार महीपितः ।

After some time, the son became fully

developed. Then the King's minister, cutting his right bowel, got the son out. Out of the God's favour, the King did not die.

कं धास्यति कुमारोयं मन्निणध्छुकर्णभूगम् ॥ 62 ॥

तद्वेरे देशिनीं प्रादान्मां धातेत्यवद्बृढ़ः ।

सोउभवढ़लावाजाजा मान्याता पृथिवीपति: ।

तदुर्नसित्तु भूपाल कथिता तव विहरान् ॥ 63 ॥

श्रौत श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणो सत्यमकन्धे

नवमो3ध्याः ।। 9 ।।

When the ministers were troubled with the thought whose milk the child will suck, then Indra spoke out the child would drink (Mān Dhātā) my forefinger and gave his finger into the child's mouth. For that reason his name was Māndhātā. Thus I have described in detail the origin of Māndhātā.

Here ends the Ninth Chapter of the Seventh Book on the story of Kākutstha and the origin of Māndhātā in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER X

On Satyavrata

व्यास उवाच

वधूव चक्रवर्ती स नृपति: सत्यसंगर: ।

मांधाता पृथिवीं सर्वमिजयद्वृत्तीश्व: ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! That King Mānthāthā, true to his promise, conquered one after another the whole world and became the paramount sovereign of all the other emperors and got the title 'Sārvabhauma' (Sovereign of all the earth).

दयवोदय भयत्रता ययुर्गिश्रिष्ठासु च ।

इन्टेनणस्य कृतं नाम जसहसूयिति स्कुटम् ॥ 2 ॥

O King! What more to speak of Mándhāta's influence at that time than this that all the robbers, struck with his terror, all fled to the mountain caves. For this reason, Indra gave him the title 'Trasadasyu.'

तस्य बिन्दुभती भार्यां शगत्थिदो: सुताभवत् ।

पतित्रता सुल्णा च सर्वलक्षणसंयुता ॥ 3 ॥

तस्यानुयाद्वयामास मांधाता ही सुतौ नृप ।

पुकुत्सं सुविख्यातं मुचुकुन्दं तथापपम् ॥ 4 ॥

He married Bindumati, the daughter of Sāsāvindu. Her limbs were proportioned and perfect and so she was very beautiful. Mándhātā had by that wife two sons: (1) the famous Purukustha and (2) Mucukunda.

पुकुत्सातातोउपय: पुरः परमधार्थिकः ।

पितृपतिचरताशुपदूर्वद्धविशस्तादायकः ॥ 5 ॥

Purukustha had his son Anaranya; this prince was celebrated by the name of Brhadaśva. He was very religious and deeply devoted to his father.

हर्यक्षतस्य पुत्रोभपूद्वामैकः परमार्थवित् ।

तस्यात्मजक्षित्यावभूद्रणतास्य चात्मयः ॥ 6 ॥

His son was Haryaśva; he was religious and knew the Highest Reality. His son was Tridhanvā; his son was Aruna.

Book VII Chapter X

अनुषय सुतः श्रीमान्स्यवत् इति श्रुतः ।

सोःभूदित्बधारः कामो मंदात्मा ह्यिततोतुः ॥ 7 ॥

स यापात्मा विध्धपार्यं हतवान्कामपोहितः ।

विवाहै तस्य विधनं स चकार नृपतेः सुतः ॥ 8 ॥

Aruna's son was Satyavrata; he was very avaricious, lustful, wicked and wilful. Once one an occasion that vicious prince, overpowered by lust, stole away the wife of one Brāhmīn and so created an hindrance in his marriage.

मितिला ब्राह्मणात्मत राजानामरणं नृप ।

क्रपुर्णें सुखुःखात्तां हा हातः स्मेति चासकृत् ॥ 9 ॥

O King! The Brāhmins, united in a body, came to the King Aruṇa, bewailing and lamenting and uttered repeatedly: Alas! We are ruined!

पूछ्छ राजा तात्प्रापनुःवितानुस्वारसिनः ।

किं कृतं मम पुत्रेण भवताययुर्भे बिज्ञा : ॥ १ ० ॥

The King addressed to the grieved subjects, the Brāhmins: “O Brāhmins! What harm his been done to you by my son.”

तिश्रण्य द्विजा वाक्यं राजो विनयपूर्वकम् ।

तदोयुस्त्वरणं विषा कृतागरीवंचना घृष्टम ।। १। १।।

Hearing thus the good words of the King, the Dvijas, versed in the Vedas, repeatedly blessed him and said:

बाहाणा ऊचुः

राजस्व सुतेनाए विवाहे प्रहता किल ।

विवाहिता विग्रकन्या जलेन वलिनावं ।। १२ ॥

O King! You are the foremost of the powerful. So your son is like you. Today he has forcibly stolen away during the marriage ceremony a Brāhimin daughter already given over in marriage."

व्यास उवाच

श्रुत्वा तैशा वचस्तव्यं राजा परमधार्मिकः ।

पुत्रमाह वृथा नाम कृते ते दुर्कमर्गा ॥ 13 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The highly religious King hearing the words of the Brāhmins, took them to be true and said to his son: "O One of evil understanding! You have rendered today your name useless by perpetrating this evil act.

गच्छ दूरं सुमन्दात्मनुराचार गृहान्मा ।

न स्थातव्यं तव्या पाप विषये मम सर्वेश ॥ 14 ॥

O Vicious One! Get away from my house! O Sinner! You will never be able to live in my territory!"

कुपितं पितरं प्राह कव गच्छामीति के मुहुः ।

अकृतसमयोवाच भधाकैः सह वर्तेय ॥ 15 ॥

धपचय कृतं कर्म द्विद्वारापहारणम् ।

तस्मातैः सह संसर्ग कृत्वा तिह यथासुखम् ॥ 16 ॥

Seeing his father angry, Satyavrata repeatedly said: "Father! Where shall I go?" He said: "Live with the Cāndālas. You have stolen a Brāhmīn's wife and so have acted like a Cāndāla.

नाई पुत्रेप पुत्रार्थी त्वया च कुलपांसन ।

यथेई राज दुहात्यन्तीर्निजाः कृतत्स्वया ॥ 17 ॥

Go and live with them happily. O Disgrace to your family! I do't like to get issues through you; you have obliterated this family's name.

स निश्रय विमुवांवयं कुपितस्य महात्मनः ।

निश्रकाम पुतात्मास्तारा धघचान्ययी ॥ 18 ॥

So, Sinner go wherever you like. Hearing thus the words from his angry father, Satyavrata instantly quitted the house and went to the Cāndālas.

सत्यसत्सदा तत्र धपाकैः सह वर्तै।

धनुबाणधरः श्रीमान्कवची कर्णालायः ॥ 19 ॥

The prince, wearing his coat of armour and holding bows and arrows, began to spend away his time with the Cāndālas; but he could not get out of his breast his feeling of sympathy and mercy.

यदा निष्कासितः पिता कृपितेन महात्मना ।

गुणानाश वसितेन प्रेरितोस्ती महापिति: ॥ 20 ॥

When he was banished by his liberal angry father, the Guru Vasishta instigated the King to the above purpose.

तस्माल्यद्रतस्तस्मिन्धपत्र कोधसंसुत: ।

वसिषे धर्मशास्त्रेन निवारणपरादुमुखे ॥ 21 ॥

Satyavrata was therefore angry with Vasistha, inasmuch as he, versed in the Dharma Śāstras, did not dissuade the father from banishing his son.

केन्धितकारोगंथ पिता तस्य महीपति: ।

पुर्णार्थेजी तपस्तपुं पुरं व्यक्त्या बनं गतः ॥ 2 2 ॥

His father, then, owing to some inexplicable cause, quitted the city and, for the sake of his son, went to the forest to practise austerities.

न वर्षा नत तस्मिव्यये पाकाशासनः ।

सा द्वारण राजेन्द्र तेनाधर्मेण सर्वथा ॥ 23 ॥

O King! Owing to that sinful act, Indra did not rain at in his kingdom for twelve years.

विश्वामित्तदा दारांसतिमस्तु विषये नृप ।

संभ्यस्य कौशिकीतेरे चचार विपुलं तपः ॥ 24 ॥

O King! Just then Viśvāmitra, too keeping his wife and children in that kingdom, began to practise severe austerities on the banks of the river Kauśikī.

कातात तत संजाता भार्यां वे कौशिकस्य ह ।

कुट्वम्भरणायीय दुःखिता वरवर्गिनी ॥ 25 ॥

बालकान्बुध्याक्रांतान्तस्तदः पश्यतीं भृणम् ।

यामानांश्र नीवाराकरणम पत्रिता ॥ 26 ॥

चिन्यामाम दुःखाती तोकान्बीक्ष्य क्षुणतान् ।

युगे नासित पुरे हादा कं याचे वा कालीक किम् ॥ 27 ॥

न मे प्रातास्ति पुत्राणां पतिमें नास्ति सक्रियों ।

स्टंति बालकाः कामं घिछूरे जीवन्यदा थे ॥ 28 ॥

धनहीनों च मां त्यक्ता तपस्तपुं गत: पति: ।

न ज्ञानाति सम्बोभि दुःखितां धनवर्जितान् ॥ 29 ॥

The beautiful wife of Kuśika then fell into great trouble how she could maintain the family. All the children, pained with hunger, began to cry, begging for Nivāra rice food. The chaste wife of Kauśika became very much troubled in seeing all this. She thought, seeing the children hungry. “Where am I to go now and from whom to beg, and what to do, inasmuch as the King was not then staying in the Kingdom.

बालानां परर्ण के कनरोय पतिता दिना ।

मरिष्यन्ति सुना: सर्वे कुधाय भीडिता भूणम् ।। 30 ॥

एकं सुर्ट तु विक्रीय रबेण किया पुनः ।

पालयामि सुतान्यानेप के विहितो विधि: ॥ 31 ॥

The husband is not also near; so who would protect my children? The boys are incessantly crying. Fie therefore to my life!" She thought also

thus: "My husband has left me in this penniless state; we are suffering for want of money. He does not know these, though he is quite able. Save my husband, who else will support my sons?

सर्वेषां भाग्यं नाङ्धा युक्ते मम विधपर्ये ।

कालस्य कालनायाहं विक्रीणागय तावताम्बम् ॥ 32 ॥

They will all die now of starvation. I might sell one of my sons, whatever I get out of that, I can support the others; this is now my highest duty.

हृदयं कठिनं कृत्वा संचिन्य मनस सती ।

सा दर्भरज्ञा व्यट्याय गले पुत्रं विनिर्मिता ॥ 33 ॥

I ought not to do otherwise and kill all my children; so I will now sell one of my sons to support the others." Thus hardening her mind, she

मृत्यु तलि ही भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुवि भुबी

मृपिनपनी गते बज्द्या मध्यमं पुत्रमौरमू ।

गोष्व सरणाध्याये गृहीत्वा चलिता गृहात् ॥ 34 ॥

The Muni's wife, for the sake of the other children, fastened the middle son by a cord and got out of her house.

दूद्वा सत्यव्रतेनातां तासयी गोकसंयुता ।

पण्ड्छ पूर्वातितां तु किं चिकोर्षिस गोभेने ॥ 35 ॥

The prince Satyavrata saw her distressed with pain and sorrow and asked: "O Beautiful One! What are you now going to do? Who are you?

रूर्तं बालकं कपड़े बदूट्यां नयसि कवानुना ।

किमर्थे चारभर्वांगि सत्वं बृष्टि ममाप्रतः ॥ 36 ॥

This boy is crying; Why have you tied him by a rope round his neck? O Fair One! Speak out truly to me the cause of all this."

སྐྱོད་པའིབ་སྟན་བཟན

विश्वामित्रय भावार्त्रं पुत्रोजयं मे नृपात्रज ।

विकेतुभीरसं कामां गमिष्य विषये सुतम् ॥ 37 ॥

अत्रं नारिति पतिर्वेल्चा नारतलनुं नृप वक्षित् ।

विक्रीणामि क्षुधात्रैंन शेषस्य भरणाय दै ॥ 38 ॥

The Rşi's wife said: "O Prince! I am the wife of Viśvāmitra. These are my sons. I am now going, for want of food, to sell one of these out of my own accord. O King! My husband has gone away to practise tapasyā; I do not know where he has

Book VII Chapter X

gone. There is no food in the house; so I will sell one to support the other sons."

राजोवाच

पत्तितः खू पुत्रं दास्यामि भरणं तव ।

तावदेव पतिसेउत्र वनाच्छैवागमिथिति ॥ ३ ९ ॥

Satyavrata said: “O Chaste One! Save your children. I will bring to you your articles of food from the forest till your husband does not come here.”

यूक्षे तावशामाथागे धस्यं किंशिरितरतम् ।

बन्ययिता गमिथ्यामि सत्यमेततुर्वीयमम् ॥ 40 ॥

इत्युक्ता सा तदा तेन रात्रा कोशिककामिनी ।

विद्वंधे तनयं कृत्वा जगमाभ्रमपण्डलम् ॥ 41 ॥

Daily I will fasten some food on a tree close by your Śārama. This I speak truly." The wife of Visvāmitra, hearing these words of the prince, freed the child of the fastening and took him to her Śārama.

सोउभवहालो नाम गतलव्यान्महातपः ।

सा तु स्वस्याम्पे गत्या मुमोद बालकैवृत्ता ॥ 42 ॥

The child was named afterwards as Gālava, due to his being fastened by the neck. He became a great Rsi afterwards. The Viśvāmitra's wife then felt great pleasure in her home, surrounded by her children.

स्वयस्तुम् बक्न्या च कृपया च परित्युतः ।

विश्वाधियस्य च मुनेः कलत्र नद्वषपार ह ॥ ४३ ॥

Filled with regard, and mercy, Satyavrata duly performed his task and provided daily the family of Viśvāmitra with their food.

वने सितानामृगान्त्वा वराहामहिषांस्त्वा ।

विश्वाधियत्रनाप्यापो मांसं पुवेळ वर्धंध हु ॥ 4 4 ॥

He used to hunt wild boars, deer, buffaloes, etc; and used to take their flesh to the place where used to dwell the wife of Visvāmitra and the children and tie that up to an adjoining trees.

त्रषिष्पती गृहीता तन्मांं पुत्रान्याततः ।

निर्वृति परमां प्राप प्रायव भक्यमन्तुलमम् ॥ 45 ॥

The Rṣi's wife used to give those to her children. Thus getting excellent food, she felt very happy.

अयोध्यां चैव राज्यं च तथैवात:पुरं पुंनि: ।

गते तनुं गुरे तास्मवासिषः पर्यक्षतः ॥ 46 ॥

Now when the King Aruṇa went for tapasyā to the forest, the Muni Vasistha carefully guarded the Ayodhyā city, and the palace and the household.

सत्यव्रतोसि धर्मांना ह्रातिह्वगगाईहः ।

वितुर्जातं समासाया यगुन्नवतावाने ॥ 47 ॥

सत्यव्रतो हाकस्पाच्छ कस्यचित्काराणाङ्गः ।

वसिष्ठे चार्थिकं मन्थुं धारयामास्त्र निव्याद् ॥ 48 ॥

Satyavrata, too, used to sustain his livelihood daily by hunting, according to his father's order; and abiding by Dharma, lived in the forest outside the city. Satyavrata cherished always in his heart, for some cause, a feeling of anger towards Vasiṣṭha. When his father banished his religious son, Vasiṣṭha did not prevent his father.

त्वम्यानं वने विज्ञा धार्मिर्धे च प्रियं सुन्तः।

न वारयामास भूनिर्विशेषः कारणेन है ॥ 49 ॥

पाणिग्रहणमान्जाणां निष्ठा स्यात्सदामे पदे ।

जानत्रसि स धर्माति विग्रदार्यस्पिष्ठे ॥ 50 ॥

This is the cause of Satyavrata's anger. Marriage does not become valid until seven footsteps are trodden (a ceremony); so the stealing away of a girl within that period is not equivalent to stealing away a Brāhmin's wife. The virtuous Vasistha knew that; yet he did not prevent the King.

कर्मिभ्रशिविरसोरन्पे पुणाभावे महीपति: ।

वसिष्ठस्य च गां दोर्धीयमयशूनमध्यागम ॥ 51 ॥

One day the prince did not find anything for hunting; he saw in the forest the cow of Vasistha giving milk. Very much distressed by hunger, the King killed the cow like a dacoit, partly out of anger and partly out of delusion.

तां जयान कुधात्रैनु कोधायोहाच्छ दस्युन्त ।

युङ़े बबंधे तन्मांसं नीत्वा स्वयमभक्षन्त् ॥ 52 ॥

ऋषिपनी सुतान्बतिभोजयामास तन्तद ।

गंकमाना भुगयेति न गोरिति च सुझत ॥ 53 ॥

He fastened part of the flesh to that tree for the wife of Visvāmitra and the remainder he ate

himself. O One of good vows! The Viśvāmitra's wife did not know that to be beef and thought it to be deer's and so fed her sons with that.

वसिष्ठसुत हतां दोर्धी ज्ञाला कुब्द्रसमक्षवी।

दुरात्मिके कृतं पार्य धेनुवातार्थयाचवर्ष ॥ 54 ॥

Now when Vasistha came to know that his cow been killed, he was inflamed with anger and spoke to Satyavrata “O Vicious One! What a heinous crime have you committed, like a Piśāca, by killing the cow?”

एवं ते शंकवः कृता: परंतु त्वरिताक्षय: ।

गोवधात्रहरणान्तियुः क्रोतानथा भूगण ॥ 55 ॥

For the killing of the cow, the stealing of a Brāhmīn's wife and the fiery anger of your father, for these three crimes, let there come out on your head three Śankus or three marks of leprosy as the signs for your crimes.

शिर्याकृपरित नामान वै भृवि ख्यानौ भविष्यात् ।

प्रिशाचरूपयात्मानं दर्शयन्सर्वेदिकानम् ॥ ५६ ॥

From this day you will be widely known by the name of Trišanku and you will shew your Piśāca form to all the beings."

यात्र उवाच

एवं शण्तो वसिष्ठेन तदा सत्यबतो नृपः ।

चचार च तपस्तीत तस्मिनेबाश्रमे स्थितः ॥ 57 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The prince Satyavrata thus cursed by Vaisiṣṭha remained in that retreat and practised severs tapasyā.

कस्माचिद्यमुपिज्ञातु प्राय मंत्रमन्तमम् ।

ध्यायभगवती देखीं प्रकृति परभां शिवान् ॥ 58 ॥

शीत श्रीवैदीभागवते गहापुराणे सत्यमक्क्ये

वशामीस्थाय: 11 10 11

But he got from a Muni's son the excellent Mantram of the Highest auspicious Devi Bhagavatī and became merged in the contemplation of that. Here ends the Tenth Chapter of the Seventh Book on the story of Satyavrata in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevībhāgvatam of 18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

CHAPTER XI

On Trísainku

जनमेजय उवाच

वसिष्ठेन च रणतोउसी विशंकुभूपते: सुतः ।

कर्थं शणाधिनिर्भुक्तसन्ये बृहि महामते ॥ १ ॥

Nanamejaya said: O Intelligent One! Did the prince Trisanku free himself afterwards of the curse inflicted on his by the Muni Vasistha.

व्यास उवाच

सत्यव्रतस्तथा शगत: विश्याचत्यमावतावान् ।

तस्मिवेत्रावासमे तस्थी देवीभक्तिपरायण: ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Satyavrata, cursed by Vasīṣṭha, was transformed into a demonaical state (Piśācatva); but he became a great devotee of the Devi and passed away his time in that Śārama.

क्वार्विष्यपतिस्त्र जल्वा मन्थ नवाक्षरम् ।

होभार्थ बाहुणान्त्वा प्रणायोबार चपित्तः ॥ 3 ॥

One day he repeating slowly the nine-lettered Mantram of the Bhagavati, wished to perform the

Puraścarana ceremony (repeating the name of a deity attended with burnt offerings, oblations, etc.) of the said Mantra, came to the Brāhmins, bowed down to them with great devotion and purity and said:

भूमिदेवाः शुणुत्यं वै वचनं प्रगतस्य मे ।

ऋलिजो मम सर्वैत्रि भवतः प्रभवन्तु है ॥ ४ ॥

“O venerable gods of the earth! Kindly hear me; I with my head bowed down pray to you, that you all be my priests (Rtvijs).

जपस्य च द्वगीशेन होमः काव्यों विधानतः ।

भवाद्धः कार्यसिद्धार्थ बेर्विविद्धः कृपापरेः ॥ ५ ॥

You are all versed in the Vedas; so kindly do for me duly the Homa ceremony equal to one tenth part of Japam, for my success.

सत्यवतोर्भं युपतेः पुत्रो क्रद्धाविद्धांरतः ।

कार्य मन विधात्यां सर्वेथा सुखहेतवे ॥ 6 ॥

Book VII Chapter XI

O Brāhmaṇas! My name is Satyavrata; I am a prince; you ought to do this work for me for my welfare."

तच्छुला ब्राह्मणास्तत्र तमूचुरंगपेतः सुतम् ।

ग्रातस्तं गुर्भाग प्रातर्न प्रिणाचलं त्वयासुभुना ॥ 7 ॥

Thus hearing the prince's words the Brāhmaṇas said: "O Prince! You are cursed by your Guru and you are now turned into a demonaical state.

न यागहुँर्षि सत्माच्चं वैद्यवन्धितकातः ।

प्रियालवमनुग्रणतं सर्वलोकेषु गहिंगम ॥ 8 ॥

You have now no right to the Vedas; especially you are now in the Piśāca state; it is blamed by all the persons; so now you are not fit to be initiated into the ceremony.

व्यास उवाच

तिश्रणम्य चद्रस्तेधां राजा दुःखमवाग इ ।

धिर्जीवितादिं मेउढ़ किं करोरि चने सित्तः ॥ 9 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing them, the prince got very sad not dejected and thought “Fie on my life! What shall I do now in living even in the forest. पिता चाहं परितक्तः शतक्ष शुकण भुशम् ।

राज्यदृष्टः प्रियाचलमुज्ञापतः क्रोगेम किम् ॥ 10 ॥

तदा पृथुत्तां कृत्वा चितां काहैनुपातज्ञः ।

संस्मार चण्डिकां देवीं प्रवेशमनुचित्यन्त्य ॥ 11 ॥

My father has forsaken me; I am banished from the kingdom; again, by the Guru's curse, I have got this Piśaca's state; I therefore can't decide what to do." The prince, then, collecting fuel, prepared the funeral pits for himself, remembered the Caṇḍikā Devī and repeating Her Mantram, resolved to jump into the fire.

सूचता देर्ती महामारा चित्ता प्रवलितां पुरः ।

कृत्वा स्नावा प्रवेश्याँ स्थितः प्रांजितसघातः ॥ 12 ॥

Lighting the pyre in front, the prince bathed and standing, with clasped palms, began to chant the hymns to Mahā Māyā before entering into the fire.

त्राला भगवती तं तु मुर्कामं मतीपतिन् ।

आजगाम तदाकामां प्रत्यक्षं तस्य चात्रातः ॥ १३ ॥

दत्ताथ दर्शनं देती तमुवाच रुपात्मजम् ।

सिंहाक्वा महाराज मेघांभीर्या गिरा ॥ १४ ॥

At this moment, the Devi Bhagavatī, knowing that the prince was ready to burn himself, came instantly to the sport on the back of the lion, by the aerial route. She manifested Herself before him and spoke in a voice deep like a rain-cloud.

देश्याच

किं ते व्यवसितं साधो हताशो गा तनुं त्यज ।

सियो भव महाभाग पित्त ते जरसाधितः ॥ 15 ॥

राज्यं दत्ता वयं तुर्थ्य मन्तातित तपसे किल ।

विषादं त्यज है वीर परधोड़हीन भुपते ॥ 16 ॥

नेतुं त्वामगमिष्यन्ति सचिवाड़ प्रितुस्त्व ।

मत्स्यात्त्यिता च स्वामधिषिच्य नृपासे ॥ 17 ॥

वित्ता कामं बहुतोकं गमिष्येव प्रश्रयः ।

"O Virtuous One! What is all this? What have you settled all these? Never throw yourself in fire; be patient. O Fortunate One! Your father is now aged; he will give you his kingdom and will go to the forest for tapasyā; therefore, O Hero! Do leave your depression of spirits. O King! To-morrow the ministers of your father will come to you to take you there. By My Grace, your father will install you on the throne and, in due time, he will conquer his desires and will go undoubtedly to the Brahmaloka.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्ता तं तदा देही तत्रेवांतत्पयिति ॥ १ १ ॥

राजपुत्रो विरितो मरणात्यवाकततः ।

Vyāsa said: O Fortunate One! Thus saying, the Devi vanished at that spot; the prince, too, desisted from his purpose of entering into the fire.

अयोध्यायां तद्गाल्य नारदेन महात्मना ॥ १९ ॥

यृत्तांतः कथितः सर्वां रोहे सत्वरामवित्तः ।

श्रूला राजार्थे पुरस्य तं तथा मरणोषादम् ॥ 20 ॥

In the meanwhile, the high-souled Nārada went to Ayodhyā and at once informed everything to the King. The King became very sad and began to repent very much, hearing the son's resolve to burn himself.

खेददामाधाय मनसि शुणोच बहुधा नृपः ।

Śrimadaveivbhāgavatam

सचिवानाह धर्मात्मा यूत्रशोकपरिप्लुतः ॥ 21 ॥

ज्ञातं भवद्धित्युयं युजस्य मच चेहितम् ।

The virtuous King, grieved at heart, for his son, said to his ministers: "You all are aware of the turning out of my son.

त्यन्तो मया वने धीमायुग: सत्यवते मा ॥ 22 ॥

आज्ञासौ गतः सदो राज्याहुं: परमार्थवित् ।

I have forsaken my intelligent son Satyavrata; though he was very spiritual and worthy to get the kingdom; yet, at my command, he instantaneously went to the forest.

स्थिततनैद्व विज्ञाने धनहीनः क्षमात्वितः ॥ 23 ॥

वसिष्ठेन तथा शगतः विशासवसृषः कृतः ।

Void of wealth, he, practising forgiveness, passed his time in study, particularly in spiritual knowledge; but Vasiṣṭha Deva cursed him and made him like a Piśāca.

सो5ढ़ दुःखेन संतप प्रवेषुं च हुताशनम् ॥ 24 ॥

उक्तः श्रीमहादेवा निषिद्धः संस्थितः पुनः ।

Very much distressed by pain and sorrow, he was ready to burn himself but the Mahā Devi preventing him, he desisted from this purpose.

तस्मावराष्ठन्तु तं शीघ्रं जेश्धुरं महावलम् ॥ 25 ॥

आश्राय स्वचैत्रत तत्सैवान्वसित्व ।

अभिविष्य सुतं राज्य औरसं पालनक्षमम् ॥ 26 ॥

वनं भार्यामि शान्तोउहं तपसे कृतनिष्ठतः ।

So go hurriedly and, consoling my powerful eldest son, bring him at once to me. I am now calm and quiet and of a retiring disposition; so I am determined to practise tapasyā.

इतुलक्वा मन्त्रिणः सवत्नोषयामास पार्थिवः ॥ 27 ॥

तद्यैवानयान्यहि हि प्रीतिप्रवणमानसः ।

My son is now capable to govern the subjects; I will now install my son on the throne and retire to the forest. So he gladly sent his ministers to his son.

ते गत्वा ते सभाशयाय मन्त्रिणः पार्थिवात्मम् ॥ 28 ॥

अयोध्यायां महात्मानं मानपूर्वे समानयन् ।

The ministers, too, gladly went there and

consoled the prince and, with respect, brought him to the Ayodhyā city.

इद्धला सत्यन्तं राजा दुर्वतैं मलिनांबरम् ॥ 29 ॥

जटापूट्यं कूरं चिन्तानुपवित्यन्ति ।

किं कृत निरुक्तं कर्म मया पुत्रे विवासितः ॥ 30 ॥

राज्यहंशातिमेधावी जानता धर्मनिश्वाम् ।

Seeing Satyavrata with matted hair on his head, with dirty clothes, and thin and worn out with cares, the King began to think within himself “Oh! What a cruel act have I done, though I know everything about religion, in banishing my intelligent son, quite fit to govern my kingdom.”

इति संचित्य मनसा तमालिंसय महीपितः ॥ ३१ ॥

आसने स्वसमीपस्ये समाधान्योपवेशगत् ।

उपविष्टं सुतं राजा प्रेमपूर्वमुवाच ह ॥ ३२ ॥

प्रेमपादादय वाचा नीतिशास्बविशारदः ।

Thus thinking, he embraced his son by his arms and consoling him, made him sit by his throne. The King, versed in politics, then began to speak gladly with suffocated feelings of love, to his son sitting by the side of him.

राजोवाच

पुरु धर्मे मति: कार्यां माननीया मुखोद्वा: ॥ 33 ॥

न्यायगतं धनं प्राहं स्थणीया: सदा प्रजा: ।

नासलं व्वाणि वत्कलं नामर्गे गमनं क्वचित् ॥ 34 ॥

“O Son! Your highest duty is to keep your mind always on religion and to respect the Brāhmins. Never speak falsely anywhere nor follow any bad course in any way.

शिष्योक्ते प्रकतिथां पूजनीयास्पत्थितयनः ।

हन्तया दस्यवः कृता इन्द्रियाणां तथा जयः ॥ 35 ॥

Rather the words of the spiritual good persons ought to be fully observed; the ascetics ought to be worshipped. Senses must be controlled and the wicked cruel robbers are certainly to be slain.

कतैंवं कार्यसिद्धार्थ राज्ञ पुत्र सदैव हि ।

मन्त्रस्तु सदैवा गोप्य: कतैंव्य: सचिविः सह ॥ 36 ॥

O Son! For one's success, one should consult with one's ministers and keep that as secret by all means.

Book VII Chapter XI

नोप्रेष्योरूपोर्षि कृतिना सिंपु: सर्वात्मना सुत ।

न विश्वशेत्यासराकें सचिवं च तथानतम् ॥ 37 ॥

Any enemy, howsoever insignificant he may be, a clever King should never overlook him. The ministers, if they be attached to other masters and if they come round afterwards, don't trust them.

चारा: सर्वे योक्त्या: शगूमिपेयु सर्वथा ।

धर्मे मति: सदा कामी दानं द्वाण्च नियास: ॥ 38 ॥

Spies should be kept to watch friends and foes alike. Shew your living regards to the religion always, and make charitable gifts.

शुक्रवादो न कतंचीय दुर्गसंगं च वजंयेत् ।

यश्या विधिधा यज्ञा: पूजनीया मतृषेय: ॥ 39 ॥

One ought not to argue in vain and always avoid the company of the wicked. O Son! You should worship the Maharsis and perform various sacrifices.

न विश्वेतिस्थं क्वापि क्षेषापुतर्ततं नन् ।

अत्यादारो न कर्तेलो मृगायायां कदाचन ॥ 40 ॥

Never trust women, those who are inordinately addicted to women, and the gamblers. Never is it advisable to be addicted too much to hunting.

घृते मटे तथा गेये नूनं वारवघुपु च ।

स्वयं तद्धिमुखी घ्यात्याजास्तेभ्यघ्र श्वयेत ॥ 41 ॥

Always shew your back to gambling, drinking, music and to the prostitutes and try to make your subjects follow the same.

ब्राहो मुटुर्ते कलिवमुर्थानं सर्वथा सदा ।

स्नानादिके सर्विविधि विधाया विधित्यन्ता ॥ 42 ॥

Early in the morning at the Brāhma Muhūrta every day you should get up from your bed and bathe and perform other analogous duties.

पशास्त्रक: परां पूर्वां भवल्या कुयांस्तुदीक्षितः ।

पृत्रैतज्ञन्मसफल्व पराशकः पदार्चेन्म ॥ 4 3 ॥

O Son! Be initiated by the Guru in the Devi Mantra and worship with devotion the Supreme Force, the Bhagavatī. Human birth is crowned with success by worshipping Her Lotus Feet.

सकूलकृत्वा महापूर्णां देवोपादलनं पिबन् ।

न जातु जननीर्षे गघ्छेदिति विनिश्रधः ॥ 44 ॥

O Son! He who performs once the great Pūjā of

the Mahā Devi and drinks the Caraṇāṃtā water (water with which Her feet are worshipped) has never to enter again in the womb of his mother; know this as certain.

सर्वे दूर्यं महादेवी ग्रहा साकी च सेव हि ।

इति तद्धावभरित्सिहोत्रिभयंवेतसा ॥ 4 5 ॥

That Mahā Devī is all that is seen and She Herself is again the Seer and Witness, of the nature of Intelligence. Filled with these ideas, rest fearless like the Universal Soul.

कृत्वा निविधिं सम्यगंतंवं सदसि डिजान् ।

समादूय च प्रष्टयो धर्मगस्त्विनिणयः ॥ 46 ॥

Do your daily (Naimittik occasional) duties, go to the Brāhimn's assembly and calling on them ask the conclusions of the Dharma Śāstras.

संपूच ब्राह्णान्यूज्ञान्येद्वेलांगपारगान् ।

गोपृष्टिप्यादिकं च देवं पाञेषु सर्वदा ॥ 47 ॥

The Brāhmins, versed in the Vedas and Vedāntas, are objects of venerations and must be worshipped.

अविद्धान्वाजणः कोर्पि नैव पूज्य कदाचन ।

आहारादिर्विक नैव देवं मुख्याय कर्हिंचि ॥ 48 ॥

Give, then, them always according to merits, cows, lands, gold, etc. Do'nt worship any Brāhmin who is illiterate.

न वा लोपाचत्या पुत्र कर्तेल्य धर्मतैरंचन् ।

अतः परं न कतळी क्वचिषिप्रावमाननम् ॥ 49 ॥

ब्राह्मण भूमिद्विष्ठा माननीयाः प्रयन्ततः ।

कारणं क्षिवाणां च द्विजा एव न संशयः ॥ 50 ॥

Don't give to illiterates more than their belliful wants. O Child! Never trespass Dharma, out of covetousness, and remember always not to insult ever after wards any Brāhmanas, The Brāhmins are the cause of the Kṣattriyas, the more so they are the terrestrial gods; honour them with all your care! In this never flinch from your duties.

अन्योर्धनिर्बंबाण: श्रन्मरणो लोहमुचित्यम् ।

तेषां सर्वजंन तैल: स्वासु योगिनु शग्रामति ॥ 51 ॥

Fire comes out of water; the Ksatriyas come out of the Brāhmans; iron comes out of stones. The

powers of these flow then that clash dies away in the source.

तस्मात्राता विशेषेण माननीया मुखोद्वतः ।

यानेन विनयेनैव सर्वेया भूतिभिच्छत ॥ 52 ॥

दण्डनीति: सदा कार्य धर्मशास्त्रास्तार: ।

कोशस्य संग्रहः कार्यों नूनं न्यायगतस्य ह ॥ 53 ॥

इत शीमदैर्धीभागवते महापुराणे सनमकन्मे

एकारशोस्थाया: 11 11 11

Know this as quite certain. The King who wants

his own welfare and improvement must by gift and humility shew his respect especially to the Brāhmins. Follow the maxims of morality as dictated in the Dharma Śāstras. Amass wealth according to rules of justice and fill the treasury.

Here ends the Eleventh Chapter of the Seventh Book about the story of Triśanku in the Mahāpurānam

Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XII

On Vasistha's Course on the Trisāniku

व्यास उवाच

एवं प्रवोधितः पित्रा त्रिशंकुः प्रगतो नृपः ।

तथेति पितरं प्राह प्रेमपतृत्याद्या पिता ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus giving the advice to his son, the King Triśanku was excited with feelings of love and, in a choked voice, said to his father that he would fulfil what he had been ordered.

विप्रापात्रय मन्त्रान्वेक्षास्थिवगताद्न् ।

अधिषेक्षाय संभारात्कार्यामास सत्वम् ॥ 2 ॥

The King then called the Brāhmins, versed in the Vedas and Mantrams, and had all the materials for installation collected quickly.

सलिले सर्वतीर्थानं समानाय्य विश्रापितः ।

प्रकृतीश्र समाहुय सामन्ताभ्युपतितंस्या ॥ 3 ॥

He brought the waters from all the sacred place of prigrimages; he then called together with great respect all the kings.

पूर्णेयेई विधितचत्स्मै ददावासनुपतमम् ।

अभिविष्य सुत राज्ये त्रिशंकुं विधिवित्ति ॥ 4 ॥

On a sacred day, the father installed his son on the throne and gave him, in accordance with due rites and ceremonies, the royal throne.

तृतीयसाम्थां पुण्यं ज्याह भार्याय सुतः ।

वने त्रिशयाग्कृते चचार दुक्षं ततः ॥ 5 ॥

The King then adopted with his wife the third Vānaprastha stage of life and practised a severe tapasyā on the banks of the Ganges.

काले प्रपते ययी स्वर्ग पूजितस्बद्गरीयि ।

इज्ञासनसमीपस्थो राजा रविवस्ता ॥ 6 ॥

Then in due course of time the King went to the Heavens. There he began to shine like a second Sun by the side of Indra, respected by all the gods.

राजोवाच

पूर्वं भगवता प्रोक्तं कथायोगेन सम्रंपत् ।

सत्यव्रतो वसिष्ठेन शगतो दोर्धायिधात्रिकल ॥ 7 ॥

कुपितेन विगारचलं प्रपितेगे गुरुणा ततः ।

कर्थं सुकः विगारचत्वादिव्येतलंशयः प्रभो ॥ 8 ॥

Janamejaya said: O Bhagavan! You spoke before in course of conversation that Satyavrata was cursed by Vasistha on the killing of his cow to become a Piśāca; how then he got himself freed of this curse. There is a doubt on this point. Kindly clear it and oblige. Satyavrata was cursed; hence pronounced unfit to succeed to the throne.

न सिंहासनयोयो हि भयंच्छायसपित्रतः ।

मुनिना मोचितः शापात्केनान्येन च कर्माणा ॥ 9 ॥

एतनैः बृहि विशर्षे शापमोक्षणाकाराणम् ।

आनीतस्तु कर्ष विश्रा स्वगृहे ततुष्णाकृति: ॥ 10 ॥

How was the Muni, by what actions, was he freed of the curse? How could the father bring back to his home his son of the form of a Piśāca? O Viprarṣi! Kindly narrate to me how the Muni was freed of his curse.

Book VII Chapter XII

व्यास उवाच

वसिष्ठेन च शलोउसौ सहा: शैराचतां गतः ।

दुर्षेध्धातिरुद्धर्षः सर्वलोकप्रयंकरः ॥।१।॥

यदेवोपासिता देही भक्न्या सत्यवतेने है ।

तया प्रसव्या राजनिद्व्यदेहः कृततः क्षणात् ॥ १२ ॥

Vyāsa said: Cursed by Vasiṣṭha, Satyavrata became then and there transformed into a Piśāca, very ugly, violent and terrible to all; but when he worshipped the Devi with devotion, immediately the Devi gave him a beautiful divine body.

प्रिशाचत्वं गतं तस्य पारं चैव क्षयं गतम् ।

विषयामा चातितेजस्वी संभृतस्तकृष्यगुत् ॥ 13. ॥

विशेषोप्रि प्रसात्या जातः शफिष्यसदातः ।

पितासपि च कंभूबास्य प्रेमयुक्तत्वगुळ्हा ॥ 14 ॥

By the grace of the Devi, his sins were all washed away and his Piśāca form vanished. Satyavrata, then, freed from his sins became very much vigorous and energetic. Vasiṣṭha also became pleased with him, blessed thus by the Supreme Force; and so was his father, too.

रात्यं शणास धर्मात्मा मृते चितरि पार्थिति ।

ईंजे च विधियैयर्वेईवेद्वेती सतातनीम् ॥ 15 ॥

When his father died, the virtuous Satyavrata became King, governed his subjects and performed various sacrifices and worshipped, too, the Eternal Mother of the Gods.

तस्य पुत्रो बभूवाय हीरुधानः सुगोपनः ।

सक्षणे: ग्रास्किनिर्दैः संवृत्तगतिसुन्द्रः ॥ १६ ॥

O King! Trišanku had a very beautiful son born to him, named Hariścandra, endowed in all his limbs with auspicious signs.

युवराजं सुतं कृत्वा विशंकृ: पृथिवीपितः ।

मानुषेण शरीरेण स्वर्गे भोक्तु मनो द्वे ॥ 17 ॥

The King Trišanku wanted to make his son Yuvarāja (the Crown prince) and then in his that very body while living, enjoy the Heavens.

वसिष्ठसाश्रयं माता प्रणाय विधिवर्षुः ।

उदाच वचनं प्रीतः कृतार्जिनपुद्रतदा ॥ १ ८ ॥

The King went to the Āśrama of Vasiṣtha and

gladly asked him, with folded palms, bowing down before him duly.

राजोवाच

ब्रहुपत्र महाभाग सर्वेन्यविशारद ।

विज्ञिति मे सुमनसा श्रोतुमर्हिति तापस ॥ 19 ॥

“O Ascetic! You are the son of Brahmā, versed in all the Vaidik Mantrams; so you are exceedingly fortunate; now I beg to inform you one thing; hear it gladly.

इच्छा मेउषा सम्यपना खर्लीकसुखाय च ।

अनेवैर शर्जिणे भोगान्भोतुभमातुबान् ॥ 20 ॥

I now desire to enjoy the happiness of the Heavens and all the enjoyments of the Devas, while I am in this body.

अप्यापेधिष्ठ संसासः क्रीडितुं नन्दने बने ।

देवगन्धर्वांगना च झोतखं मधुरं किल ॥ 21 ॥

To enjoy in the Nandana Garden, to live with the Apsarās and to hear the sweet music of the Devas and the Gandharvas, these ideas now have taken a strong hold of my heart.

ययाजय त्वं मदजेनागु तादुर्गेन महामुने ।

यधानेन शनैरिण बस लोकें विशिष्टिण् ॥ 22 ॥

Therefore, O Great Muni! Engage me in such a sacrifice as will enable me, in this very body to live in the Svarloka.

समर्पोर्स मुनिश्रिक कुरु कार्य ममाधान ।

प्रपयाणु मघं कृत्वा देवलोकं दुगासद् ॥ 23 ॥

O Muni! You are fully competent to do this; therefore be ready for this. Have the sacrifice done and let me have quickly the Devaloka, so difficult to be obtained!

वसिष्य उवाच

राजन्मान्यद्रत्न र्वर्गे वास सुदुर्भिः ।

मृत्यक्ष हिं धुवं सर्ग: कथितः: पुण्यकर्माण ॥ 24 ॥

तस्माड्धिपेम सर्वत्र दुर्वभाव्य मनोरथान ।

अपसरीधिष्ठ संवारो जीवमानस्य दुरर्भिः ॥ 25 ॥

कुक घ्यान्महाभाग मृत: स्वर्गमवोभ्यसि ।

Vasistha said: "O King! It is exceedingly hard to live in the Heavens while in this mortal body.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

The departed only live in the Heavens by their merits, this is a known fact. Therefore, O Omniscient One! Your desire is hard to be attained. I am afraid of this. O King! The living men can hardly enjoy the Apsarās. Therefore, O Blessed One! Do the sacrifice first. Then, when you leave this body, you will go to the Heavens."

व्यास उवाच

इत्याकाण्यं वचस्तस्य राजा परमदुभानः ॥ 26 ॥

उवाच वचनं घृयो वसिषं पूर्वीरितम् ।

न त्वं यावजसे बहुत-गवविशेषाण्च मां वहि ॥ 27 ॥

अन्यं पुरोहितं कृत्वा यध्येउई क्रिकल साम्यानम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! The Maharṣī Vasistha was already angry with the King; therefore when he spoke these words, the King heard and became absent-minded. He again spoke to the Maharṣi: “O Brāhmaṇa! If you do not allow me to do the sacrifice, on account of your haughtiness, I will have the sacrifice performed now by another priest.

तद्धूला वचनं तस्य वसियुः कोपसंयुतः ॥ 28 ॥

शशाप भूपति चेति चापड़तलो भव दुपते ।

अनेन त्वं शरीरेण श्रघचो घन सत्वम् ॥ 29 ॥

स्वांकृतन्त पायिष सुरपीधवदूषित ।

बह्यपनीहीद्धिज्ञ राममांगीविदूषक ॥ 30 ॥

न ते स्वर्गाति: पाप मृतस्पायी कर्धचन ।

Vasistha became very angry at the words of the King and cursed him: "O evil-minded One! Be as soon as possible a Cāndāla in this body.
