You have praised exceedingly that King who is a great cheat; but if I cannot prove him to be a liar of the first water, I will lose all my virtues from my birth; but if it be otherwise, then all your virtues will be destroyed. Thus the two Munis quarrelled with each other and making this stake, departed from the Heavens and went to their respective Āśramas.

Here ends the Seventeenth Chapter in the Seventh Book on the freeing of Śunahśepa and the curing of Harisācanda in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevibhāgāvatam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Śrimaddevībhāgavatam

CHAPTER XVIII

On the Quarrel between Harisćandra and Visvānitra

व्यास उवाच

क्वालिन्न् ह्रैश्डनो मुगायांवं चयं गयो ।

अपश्युद्वतीं बालां सुन्दरीं चाठलोचनाम् ॥ १ ॥

तामपृष्छन्महात्राः कामिनीं कर्णापारः ।

पदाप्रविश्रालाश्रि किं रोदिषि वरानने ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Once on a time Haricācanda went out to the forest on an hunting excursion; and, while roaming to and fro, he saw that a very beautiful lady was crying. The King, seeing this, took pity on her and asked: "O Fear One! Why are you in this forest crying alone? O Large-eyed One! Has some one pained you?

कैनसि यीद्धितास्पर्व किं के तुःखं वदाणु मे ।

का च त्व विन्ने घोरे कसने भाती विलाउवा ॥ 3 ॥

न बापते च राज्ये मे ग्रासोर्पि परिगमान् ।

तं ह्मि तरसा कान्ते यस्लां सुदरी कापते ॥ 4 ॥

What is the cause of your sorrow? Express this quickly before me. Why have you come here in this dreadful lonely forest? What are the names of your husband and your father? O Beautiful One! In my kingdom, no demon can give any trouble to another's lady; I will immediately kill him who has given you this trouble.

ब्रहि दुःखं वरातोः स्वस्था भव कृशोदिर ।

विषये मम पापात्या न तिष्ठति सूपधमे ॥ ५ ॥

इति तस्य वचः शूला नारी तं चावकीशूनम् ।

प्रमुखाश्थीय वदान्द्वरिष्ठान्त्रं नृपोतमम् ॥ ६ ॥

O thin-bellied One! Be comfortable; do not weep; tell me why you are in this sorrowful state; know that no sinner can remain within my territory." Hearing the words of the King, the lady wiped out her tears by her hand and began to say:

नारुंवाच

राजन्मां बाधतेःत्यर्थ विश्वामिनो महापुनि: ।

तपः क्रोति यद्योरं मदर्धे कौशिको वने ॥ 7 ॥

तेनाहं दुःलिता राजनिवेषने तव सुझत ।

लिद्धि मां कामनां कान्ता पीडितां गुनिना भूषण् ॥ 8 ॥

"O King! I am Siddharūpini, of the nature of success; to get me, Viśvāmitra is practising terrible austerities. So these troubles have arisen from him, the son of Kuśika. O King! For this reason I am sorry in Your kingdom. O One of good vows! I am a gentle lovely Lady; still that Muni is giving me so much trouble."

राजोवाच

स्वस्था भव शिराणालाङ्ग न ते दुःखं घचिष्यति ।

तमहं वारियधामि मुनिं तापयपरायण् ॥ 9 ॥

इत्याश्रास स्विंशं राजा तस्मा मुनिनिशिधी ।

नत्वा प्रणाय शिरास तमुवाच मृतीपति: ॥ 10 ॥

The King said: “O Large-eyed One! No longer you will have to suffer any more pains. Be patient. I will go and make the Muni desist from his tapasyā.” Thus comforting the lady, the King went hurriedly to the Muni Viśvāmitra and, bowing down to him said with clasped palms: “O Maharṣi! Why are you ailing your body by this terrible severe austerity?

स्वामिक क्रियतेर्न्यर्ष तपसा देहरीबान् ।

क्रिर्भई ते समाप्तम्भे बृहि सत्यं महामते ॥ १ १ ॥

वार्षिक्त तव गाधये क्रौमि सक्लं क्रिक्त ।

उत्तिष्ठिति तस्या तपसाउलमतः परम् ॥ १ १ २ ॥

O Highly intelligent One! For what great noble cause, are you practising this hard tapasyā; speak truly to me. O Son of Gādhi! I will fulfil your desires; there is no need of your practising this severe penance; please get out of it immediately.

विषये मम सर्वेन्र न कर्तव्य सुदारुणम् ।

लोकपीडाकरं घोरं तयः केनापि काहिंघित् ॥ 13 ॥

इर्थं निषिध्य तं राजा विश्वाधिमं गृहं यये।

मनसा कौभयाधाय गतोउसी कौशिको युनि: ॥ 14 ॥

Book VII Chapter XVIII

O Maharṣi! You know everything; so what shall I say anything further? See! If ought not anyone to practise this extremely dreadful tapasyā, causing troubles to the people within my territory." Thus prohibited by the King Hariścandra, the Muni became very angry at his heart and went towards his own hermitage.

स गत्वा चिन्तयामास नृपकृत्यमसाम्यन्तम् ।

वसिष्ठस्य च सम्वादं तपसः प्रतिधेघनम् ॥ 15 ॥

The King, too, went back to his palace. The Muni on his arrival at his hermitage, began to cogitate in his mind “Why has the King unjustly desisted me from my tapasyā and also discussions that took place between him and Vasistha.

कोभाविद्धेन मनसा प्रतीकारमथाकरोत् ।

विश्वित्य बहुधा चिते दानवे घोरियहमूम् ॥ 16 ॥

Vísvāmitra became very angry at his heart and ready to take the vengeance of this. He thought over on many points and created a terrible demon of a dreadful appearance in the form of a boar and sent it to the territory of the King Harścandra.

प्रेयायामास तेहंगं विधाय सूकताकृतीम् ।

सोस्तिकायो महाकाल: कुवैजादं सुदारुकणम् ॥ 17 ॥

That terrible boar, of huge body, entered into the kingdom, raising a dreadful sound. The guards became afraid at his terrible noise.

राजश्रीपवने प्रापत्बासवर्षक्रान्तवत ।

मालतीनां च खण्डानि करनकारां तवैव च ॥ 18 ॥

यृधिकानां च चंदानि कंपर्यंश पुरुषुरुः ।

दन्तेन विलिखन्यमिं समनुमलयते हुमान् ॥ 19 ॥

Entering into the forest, that boar began to whirl round and round and destroy the Málati forest, at another place the Kadamba forest, and at others the Yūthikā forest.

चम्याक्रतैकरीषणद्रामिलकानां च पादपान् ।

करवीरानुर्गीरांश निवर्जान शुभामूदून् ॥ 20 ॥

मुचुकुन्द्रानशोकांश बकुलांसितलकांसला ।

उन्यूल कवतं तत्र चकत मुको वने ॥ 21 ॥

At other places he began to dig up the earth by his tusks and root out the Campaka, Ketakī, Mallikā

and various other trees. At other places again, he rooted out nice gentle Uśira, Karavīra, Mucukunda, Aśoka, Vakula, Tilaka and other trees and so massacred the nice gardens and forests. The forest guards, then, taking their weapons, rushed forward on that boar.

वाटिकारश्काः: सर्वे दुडुषः गश्जपाणयः ।

हाहेति चुकुशुस्तत्र माताकावा भुशतुगतः ॥ 2 2 ॥

Those that were making garlands and the florists became very distressed and uttered uproars of consternation.

बाणैः संताङ्कामानोप्रिय यदा जस्तो न वै भृगः ।

सृकान्यापीट्यामास कोलः कालसम्बृति: ॥ 2 3 ॥

ते तदास्तिपश्याकान्ता राजानं शरणं ययुः ।

तमूचुस्ताई जाहीति वेप्याना भयंकुलाः ॥ 2 4 ॥

That boar, as if an incarnation of Death, though routed out with flights of arrows, could not be terrified; rather when he began to harass the guards very much, they became very much afraid and being very distressed took the refuge of the King and, trembling, said: "O King! Protect us. Protect us." And they cried piteously.

तानगातान्सामलोक्य भयाततो-भूयपतितस्तदा ।

पप्रष्ठ किं भयं कस्मागां चुवंतु समगाताः ॥ 25 ॥

नाहं विभोयि देवध्यो राक्षसेप्यघ्र स्ककाः ।

कस्माद्वयं समुपतं तद्ववृंत् ममाप्राताः ॥ 26 ॥

Seeing the guards terrified and distressed, the King asked them: "Whom do you fear so much and why you are so distressed? Speak truly before me. O Guards! I do not fear the Deva nor the Demons; so tell me who has created this panic amongst you.

हमि चैकेन झाणेन तं शग्रु दुर्भंग किल ।

यो मेउराति: ससुर्यसे लोके पायपति: खलः ॥ 27 ॥

देवो वा दानवो वापि ते नित्रिन शरैः शितैः ।

क्व तिहिति कियद्धूप: कियद्धलसमपित्तः ॥ 28 ॥

I, no doubt, will send that vicious cheat unto the door of Death by this arrow, who has come against me in this world. What sort of enemy is that? What is his form? What is his power and

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

where is he residing now; speak this quickly to me. Be that enemy a Deva or a Dānava, I will slay him immediately by the multitude of arrows."

मालाकारा कचुः

न देवो न च दैत्योर्शित न यक्षो न च क्रिरः ।

क्रीश्रकोले माहाकायो राजर्सिद्धति कानने ॥ 29 ॥

पुष्पब्रान्तिमुद्धून्नोनामूलवाय्यसी ।

विद्यौणं तदूनं सर्वं सूकरेणातिरेहसा ॥ 30 ॥

विशिष्टीलाडितोउमापिर्वैषूद्वलैकुटैतवा ।

न विधपेति महाराज इनुमम्यानुपाद्वत् ॥ 31 ॥

The Mālākāras said: “The enemy is not a Deva, nor a Dānava, Yaksā nor a Kinnara; it is a boar of a huge body that has entered into the forest. Very powerful, he is uprooting by his teeth all the beautiful flower trees; in fact, he is ruining all the gardens and forests. O King! We shot arrows on him, struck him with cudgels and hurled stones at him so much; yet he did not get a bit afraid; rather he turned back to kill us.

व्यास उवाच

इत्याकपर्थे वचस्तेघा राजा कोयसमाकुलः ।

अश्वमार्छा तरसा जगामोपवर्त प्रति ॥ 32 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing these words, the King's fury knew no bounds and, immediately getting on horseback, he went towards the garden and forest.

सै-येन महता युक्तो गजाक्षरथसंयुतः ।

पदातिवृद्धसहितः प्रययौ वनमुतमम् ॥ 33 ॥

ततापष्यन्महाकाले घुयुर्नतं भयानकम् ।

वनं भर्न च संवीक्ष्य राजा कोधयुतोरभवत् ॥ 34 ॥

Then the horsemen, elephant drivers, charioteers and infantry, all followed him. When the King went there, he saw the terrible boar, of a huge body, whirling round and round and making the peculiar sound in the forest; and he witnessed also the destroyed condition of the forest and became very angry.

चार्ये बाणों समारोय विकृष्य च शरासनम् ।

तं इन्तुं सूकरं पायं तरसा समुपामकम् ॥ ३ ५ ॥

समातोक्य च राजानं चापहस्तं रक्षाकुलम् ।

सम्पुखो3प्यद्वत्नर्णं कुर्बेछ्छवं सुराकाणम् ॥ ३ ६ ॥

He then drew his bow and arrows and fell down on him to take away his life. Seeing the King coming angrily towards him with bow and arrows in his hands, the boar began to sound more terribly and ran forward before him. The King saw that boar coming towards him with his mouth wide opened and began to shower arrows upon him to kill him.

तमायानां समालोक्य यराहं विकृतानानम् ।

मुभोच विशिष्टां तमिन्तुकामो महीपति: ॥ 37 ॥

वंचयित्वास्य तद्वाणं सूक्रमस्तसा बलात् ।

निजांगाम महावोगातमूलंच्च नृयं तदा ॥ 38 ॥

The boar immediately made those arrows useless, and very violently and quickly jumped and passed away, over the King. When the boar passed away, the King angrily drew his bow with great care and shot sharpened arrows at him.

गच्छन्तं तं समालोक्य राजा कोयसमन्वितः ।

मुभोच विशिष्ठास्तैष्णांशापाकृष्य यन्तः ॥ 39 ॥

क्षणं दृष्टियर्थ गड़ः क्षणं चादर्शनं गतः ।

कुर्वे-बहुविधायांर सूकरः समुपारन्ति ॥ 40 ॥

One moment the boar came in the King's sight; and at another moment he vanished away; thus the boar began to flee, uttering all sorts of sounds. The King Harisācandra then became very angry and drawing his bow purposed him, mounting on a horse, swift like the wind.

हरिछ्छ्छोरितिकुपिति मुभ्यमानुजगम ह ।

अश्वेन वाप्यवेगेन विकृष्य च शरासनम् ॥ 41 ॥

इतस्ततस्ततः सैन्यमानच्च वनान्तम् ।

एकाकी नृपति: कोलं हजन्तं समुग्रदात् ॥ 42 ॥

The soldiers then entered the forest and scattered hither and thither; the King alone pursued the boar. The sun entered unto the meridian; and the King came to be alone in a lonely forest.

मध्याहूसमये राजा सम्पातो विजने वने ।

तृषितः कृषितोत्थर्थे बधूव श्रांताबानः ॥ 4 3 ॥

सुक्रोद्वर्शन प्राप्तो राजा चिन्तानुरोभवत् ।

मार्गश्छोउतिविधिने दारुको दीनवलिख्यतः ॥ 4 4 ॥

Book VII Chapter XVIII

His horse was fatigued, and he, too, was tired of hunger and thirst. The boar went away out of sight. The King also missed his way in that dense jungle and became greatly absorbed with intense cares and anxieties.

फंक काटीम ब्व गच्छामि न सह्योर्स्ति मे बने ।

अज्ञात्स्वयः कुत्र जजामीति व्यान्चिनत् ॥ 4 5 ॥

एवं चिन्तयतस्तत विधिने जनवर्जिते ।

राजा चिन्तानुरोउपयत्रदौं सुविमलोदकम् ॥ 4 6 ॥

He then began to think. “Where shall I now go? There is none to help me in this dense jungle. Especially I don’t know the right path.” While he was thus thinking, he saw, all on a sudden, a river with clear water in that lonely forest.

वीक्ष्य तां मुवितो राजा पायवित्या तुर्जकम् ।

अश्वान्तीयं विमर्तं पपी पानीयपुतमम् ॥ 47 ॥

जलं पीता नृपतत्र सुखायय मदीयितः ।

इवेप नारं गन्तुं विद्यभेणातिमोहितः ॥ 48 ॥

He became much delighted to see the flowing river and, alighting from horseback, he drank that water and made the horse also drink it.

विश्वामित्रतु सम्पातो बुद्धबाहुपाल्यपूछ ।

ननाम वीक्ष्य राजा तं प्रीतिपूर्वे द्विजोतमम् ॥ 49 ॥

He became much relieved by drinking; and though he was much bewildered not to find the right track, he wanted now to go to his own city.

तमुवाच गाधिरावाः प्रणमन्तं नृपोतमम् ।

स्वस्ति तेउन्मु महाराज किमर्षयिभूष चागतः ॥ 50 ॥

At this moment Viśāmitra came up there in an old Brāhmīn form; the King also looking at him bowed down to the Brāhmīn garbed Viśvāmitra, who then spoke to the King:

एकाकी विजने राजन्कि चिकीर्षितमत्र ते ।

ब्रहि सर्वे स्थिते घृत्वा कारणं नृपस्तम ॥ ५ १ ॥

"O King! Welfare be unto you! What for have you come here? O King! What object have you got in view in this lonely forest? Be calm and quiet and speak everything before me."

राजीवाच

सुक्रोडितमहाकायो बलवायुधकाननम् ।

समुपैल्य ममदांगु कोभतान्युषयाद्वान् ॥ 52 ॥

तं निवारयितुं दुर्ध के कृत्वा च कामुकम् ।

ससै-योर्ड स्वनगराशिणीत मुनिस्तम ॥ 53 ॥

The King said: “O Brāhmīn! One powerful boar of a huge body entered into my garden and spoilt altogether all the gentle flower trees there. To desist that boar, I pursued him with bow in hand and went out of the city.

गतोउसी दूब्यात्यापो मायावी स्वापि वेशवान् ।

पृष्ठतोउदमपि प्रात: सैन्य स्वापि गतं मम ॥ 54 ॥

क्षृषितस्तृषितश्रहं सैन्यश्रत्सिद्वातगतः ।

न जाने पुरामर्ग च तथा सैन्यातिं मुने ॥ 55 ॥

That powerful boar, very swift and, as it were a magician, has escaped my sight and gone away where I do not know. I pursued him and have come now to this place and I do not know where my soldiers have gone. O Muni! Now I am deprived of my men, I am hungry and thirsty.

पंचानं दूर्णय विभो जजामि नगरं प्रति ।

ममात्र भाययोनेन प्राप्तस्त्वं विजने वने ॥ 56 ॥

अयोध्यापिपतिईहां हरिंछन्नोउतिचित्रभूतः ।

राजगुर्यस्य कर्ता च वार्जित्धार्थप्र: सदा ॥ 57 ॥

I do not know which is the road to my city; nor do I know where my soldiers have gone. O Dear Lord! It is to my great fortune that you have come in this lonely forest. Now I want to return to my home; kindly shew me the way. I have completed my Rājasūya sacrifice. I always give everyone whatever he wants. This is known to everybody.

धनेच्छा यदि ते बह्वाय्नाशं द्विजस्तम ।

आगत-त्यव्ययोत्यावां दास्यामि विसुले धनम् ॥ 58 ॥

शी शीर्द्रैषीभागवते महापुराणे सामकक्योडगहरो3शयाः । 181

O Dvija! If you want money for your sacrifice, then come with me to Ayodhyā and I will give you abundance of wealth. I am Hariścandra, the famous King of Ayodhyā.

Here ends the Eighteenth Chapter of the Seventh Book on the origin of the quarrel between

Hariścandra and Viśvāmitra in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, of 18,000 verses, by

Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XIX

On Hariścandra's Kingdom Taken

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुवा भूपतेः कौशिको मुनि: ।

प्रहस्य प्रत्युवाचेदं हरिश्छ्रं तदा नृप: ॥ १ ॥

राजसैतीधीमिदं पुण्यं पावनं पापनाशनम् ।

स्नानं कुक महाभाग चितृणां तपाँगं तथा ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the words of the King Hariścandra, the Maharṣi Kauśika smilingly said: "O King! This Tiratha is very sacred; if one bathes here, one is cleansed of one's sins and virtue springs up. So, highly fortunate One! Bathe in this and do peace-offerings (tarpaṇam) to your fathers.

कालः शुभातमोदसीते तीर्थे सन्ताल विशोधते ।

दानं दद्व्य सफलाउन पुण्यतीर्थे तिपावाने ॥ 3 ॥

प्राय तीर्थे महापुण्यमसन्ताल यस्तु गछविति ।

स भवेदात्माव भूय इति सत्यंभुवोज्विति ॥ 4 ॥

O King! This time is very auspicious and highly meritorious; so take a bath in this sacred Punya Tirtha and make charities as far as it lies in your power. Svāyambhava Manu says: “He, who arriving at a Tirtha capable to give high merits (Punya), does not bathe and make charities, deceives himself; so he is the slayer of his soul, no doubt.

तस्मातीर्वेते राजकुन कु पुचं स्वशक्ततः ।

दर्शरिय्यामि मार्गे ते नतासि नगर ततः ॥ 5 ॥

आगाधिष्यार्ह मार्देशुनार्थ ततवान्य ।

त्वया सहासुधकाकुतस्थ तव दानेन तोपितः ॥ 6 ॥

So, O King! Do meritorious acts as best as you can in this excellent tīrtha. Then I will shew you the way and you will go to Ayodhyā. O Kakuttsha! Today I will be pleased with your gifts and I will accompany you to shew you the way; this I have decided."

तच्छुत्वा वचनं राजा मुनेः कपटपिडतम् ।

वासांस्युतायं विधित्वलनातुमध्याययो नतीम् ॥ 7 ॥

Hearing the deceitful words of the Maharṣi, the

King took off his upper garments and tying the horse on to a tree, went towards the river to bathe according to due rites.

बन्यस्तिवहा हयं युवे सुनिवाक्येन मोहितः ।

अवर्यंभावियोगेन तड्गास्तु तद्धपवत् ॥ 8 ॥

राजा स्नानविधि कृत्वा सन्तयं पितृदेवता: ।

विश्वामिज्ञपावेचे स्वामिन्दानं द्वादि मे ॥ 9 ॥

O King! The accidental combination, that was to have been so (sure to come), so enchanted the King by the Muni's words, that he got himself entirely under the control of the Muni. He duly completed his bath and offered peace offerings to the Devas and the Pitts and then spoke to Visvāmitra. "O Lord! I am now making gifts to you.

यदिच्छसि महाभाग तते दास्यामि साम्यान् ।

गायो भूमिरिंग्चं च गजाध्यथावहान् ॥ १ ० ॥

O Fortunate One! Cows, lands, jewels, elephants, horses, chariots or horses, etc., anything that you like I will give you just now. There is nothing that I cannot give.

नादेवं मे किमयास्ति कुममेतद्रतं पुरा ।

गजसूये मखश्रेष्ठ मुनीनां सन्धिप्राविण ॥ 1 1 1 ॥

तस्पात्विह सम्यापतस्नीर्ध़स्म्यम्यरे मुने ।

यतेजसि वांषितं बृहि ददामि तव वांषितम् ॥ 1 2 ॥

When I performed previously the Rājasūya sacrifice, I took, then, before all the Munis, this vow. So, O Muni! You are also present at this principal Tirtha (place of pilgrimage); so express what you desire; I will give you your desired object."

विश्वासिज्ञ जवाच

मया पूर्वे श्रुता राजकन्नितिते विपुला भुचि ।

वसिद्धेन च सम्योक्ता दाता नासि महीतले ॥ 13 ॥

हरिश्छाने नृपश्रेष्ठः सूर्यवेगे महीपति: ।

तादृषी नृपतिर्द्राता न पृती न भविष्यति ॥ 14 ॥

पृथिव्यां परमोदारक्षिज्ञकृतनयो यथा ।

Book VII Chapter XIX

Vísvāmitra said: “O King! Your glory is spread far and wide in this world; especially I have already heard that there is no second man charitable like you. The Muni Vasisṭha has said: “The King of the solar dynasty, the Trīśanku’s son, Hariścandra is foremost and first amongst the kings in this world and there is no one so liberal-minded as he is; such a king there never was nor ever there will be.”

अतस्तां प्रार्थ्यायस्य विवाहो मेर्सित पार्थिव ॥ 15 ॥

पुरस्य च महाभागा तदर्थे दिहे मे धनम् ।

So, O King! Now the marriage time of my son has arrived; so I pray before you to-day, that you give me wealth to celebrate this marriage."

राजोवाच

विवाहं कुरु विज्ञे-त्र द्वादिं प्रार्थितं तव ॥ १६ ॥

यदिष्ठसि धनं कामं दत्ता तस्यामिन क्रिशुम् ।

The King said: “O Brāhmīn! Yes! Celebrate the marriage ceremony; I will give you your desired wealth. What more can be said than this that whatever wealth you would want, I will give that abundantly. There is no doubt in this.”

व्यास उचाच

इत्युक्तः कीशिकस्तेन वंचनात्तयो मुनि: ॥ 17 ॥

उद्वालय मायां गान्धर्वीं पार्थिवायायदर्शयेत् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing these words of the King, the Muni Kausika became ready to deceive him and originating the Gāndharvi Māyā, created a beautiful youth and one daughter aged ten years and showing them to the King, said “The marriage of these two is to be celebrated today.

कुमार: सुकुमारघ्छ कन्य च एशावर्षिकी ॥ १ ८ ॥

एतयोः कार्यमध्यच कर्तेल्य नृपसत्तम ।

O King! To marry the boys and the girls in the household is to earn more merits than the Rājasūya sacrifice.

राजसूयाधिकं पुणयं गृहस्थस्य विवाहितः ॥ १९ ॥

भविष्यति तत्वाठीव विगुपुरविवाहुतः ।

So today you will get that desired fruit if you make charities for the marriage of this Brāhmīn Youth."

तच्छूला वचनं राजा मायया तस्य मोहितः ॥ 20 ॥

तथेति च प्रतिज्ञाय नोवाचालयं वचस्तथा ।

The King was much enchanted by his Māyā; so no sooner he heard those words, he immediately promised: "That will be done" he did not raise any objection whatsoever.

तेन दर्शितमार्णोंसी नगर प्रति जीमवान् ॥ 21 ॥

विश्वामीजीपि राजानं वंचवितासे उसमं ययो ।

Vísvāmitra then showed the way and the King went to his city. Vísvāmitra, too, thus deceiving the King, went back to his Āśrama.

कृतोज्ञात्विधित्साविद्धामित्रोउवबीजुपम् ॥ 22 ॥

वेदीमध्ये नृपाढ्च त्वं देहि द्वानं यथेवित्यन्त ।

When the King was staying in Agnisālā (cook room), Vīsāvimitra Muni went to him and said: "O King! The marriage rites have been finished; so today give me what I desire in this sacrifice hall."

राजोवाच

किं तेउभीष्ट हिंज बृहि द्वादीय वास्छितं किल ॥ 23 ॥

अदेवमपि संसारे ययः कामोत्रिस्य संग्रतम् ।

व्यर्थ हिं जीवितं तस्य विषयं प्राप्य येन वै ॥ 24 ॥

नोपार्जितं यश: शुद्धं परलोकसुखप्रदम् ।

The King said: “O Brāhmīn! Speak out what you want; now I like to get fame. So if there be anything in the world, that is not to be given by me, if you want, I will give that even to you, no doubt. The mortal, possessing all wealth, if he do not earn good name and fame capable to give happiness to him in his next world, passes his life in vain.”

विश्वाधित्र ज्ञान

राज्यं देहि महाराज वराय सप्तिच्छदूम ॥ 25 ॥

गजाश्रधरलाळं बेदीमाधेउर्जिषायने ।

Víśvāmitra said: “O King! Give to this bridegroom, while within this sacred sacrificial alter, you entire kingdom with the royal umbrella and Cāmara for fanning the king and elephants, horses, chariots, infantry and all the gems and jewels.”

<table><tr><td>व्यास उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>भोहितो मायया तस्य श्रुत्वा वाक्यं मुनेत्रूप: ॥ 26 ॥दतमित्युक्तवाज्ञामयिवाचरं यदृच्छया ।गृहीतमिति तं प्राह विश्वामिजोर्नितिजुतः ॥ 27 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>Vyāsa said: O King! The King Harisćandra was deluded by his Māyā; so no sooner he heard the Muni&#x27;s words, he willingly said without the slightest consideration: “O Muni! I give as you pray, my this vast dominion to you.” The very cruel Viśvāmitra, then, said: “O King! I have accepted your offer; but O Intelligent One! Give now the requisite Daksīnā to complete your gift.</td></tr><tr><td>दक्षिणा देहि राजेन्द्र दान्तयोरां महामते ।</td></tr><tr><td>दक्षिणारहितं दानं निष्कलं मनुरखबीत् ॥ 28 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>Manu says: ‘Gift without Daksīnā is fruitless’ so to get the fruit of your gift give Daksīnā as duly fixed.”</td></tr><tr><td>तस्माहान्कालाय तंथ ययोक्तां देहि दक्षिणाण् ।</td></tr><tr><td>इत्युक्तस्तु तदा राजा तमुवाचातिविस्मतः ॥ 29 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>The King was exceedingly surprised to hear this and said: “O Lord! Kindly say what amount of wealth am I to give to you as Daksīnā.</td></tr><tr><td>बृहि कियवद्रतं तुभ्यं देथे स्वामिन्यासंधुत् ।</td></tr><tr><td>दक्षिणानिक्यं साधो वद्र तावत्वमाणकम् ॥ 30 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>O Saint! Say the value of your Daksīnā. O Ascetic! Don’t be impatient; I will give you the Daksīnā to that amount, no doubt.”</td></tr><tr><td>दानपूर्वै प्रदायामि स्वथो भव तपोधन ।</td></tr><tr><td>विश्वामिस्तु तद्धुत्वा तमाह मेदेनीपतिन् ॥ 31 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>Hearing this, Viśvāmitra told to the King: “At present give me two and a half loads of gold as Daksīnā.</td></tr><tr><td>देशभात्रयं सार्थ दक्षिणां देहि साम्यत्न ।</td></tr><tr><td>दास्यामीति प्रतिशूल तस्ये राज्ञातिविस्मतः ॥ 32 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>The King Hariścandra became greatly amazed and promised: “I will give you that;” he then anxiously mounted on his horseback and became ready to go quickly.</td></tr><tr><td>तदैव सैनिकासस्य वीक्ष्याणा: समाता: ।</td></tr><tr><td>इद्यात महीपतिं ज्ययं तुडुवुसे मुताविता: ॥ 33 ॥</td></tr></table>

At this time, his soldiers who lost their roads in quest of their king, came to him. They were very glad to see him; but, seeing him anxious, they began to praise him in great haste.

व्यास उवाच

बुत्वा तेनां वचो राजा नोकवा किंविदच्छूभाणुम् ।

चित्नयन्स्वकृतं कर्म ययावन्तःपुरे ततः ॥ 34 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing their words, the King did not say anything, good or bad; but thinking on his own doing, entered into the zenānā.

किं मया स्वीकृतं दानं सर्वेयं वत्समर्पितम् ।

वढ्धितोई जिंजेनात्र यने पाटच्छररित ए 135 ॥

Oh! What have I promised to give? I have made a gift of all that I have; I am cheated in this matter by the Muni like one robbed by a thief in a wilderness.

राज्यं सोपवक्ता तसे मया सर्व प्रतिश्रुतम् ।

भात्रयं सुवर्णस्य साधं च दक्षिणा पुनः ॥ ३६ ॥

My dominion including my dress I have promised to give to him. Moreover I will have to pay besides two and a half loads of gold.

कि कंपोरि मतिभंधीन न ज्ञातं कवटं मुनेः ।

प्रतितिरोई सहसा ब्राह्मणेन तपत्थिता ॥ 37 ॥

न जाने देहवाक्याँ वै हा देह किं पविष्यति ।

इति चिन्तापरो राजा गृहं प्रापोजीतिवहून: ॥ 38 ॥

My brain seems to have been completely destroyed. What to do now? I did not know the cunningness of the Muni. Therefore I am cheated by this deceitful Brāhmīn. It is next to impossible to understand the work of Daiva. Oh! My Fate! What will happen to me now?' Very much bewildered, the King entered in the interior of the palace.

पर्ति चिन्तापरं दृष्टवा राजी पप्रष्छ कारणम् ।

कि प्रभो विमान भाति का चिन्ता बूहिं समाप्तम् ॥ 39 ।

वन्तापुत्र: समायातो राजसूय: कृत: पुरा ।

कसमच्छोचित राजेज्ञ शोकस्य कारणं चद ॥ 40 ॥

The queen seeing her husband immersed in cares, enquired into the cause, thus: "O Lord! Why have you become so absent-minded? Kindly say what you are thinking now? O King! The son has

Book VII Chapter XIX

come back from the forest before you completed your Rajasüya sacrifice; why then are you in grief now? Kindly speak out the cause of your sorrow.

नारातिथिंघते क्रापि बतलावनुबैलो3पि वा ।

वरणोपरि सूमंतुः: कृतकृत्योपि भृतले ।। 4 1 ।।

चिन्ताव क्षीयते देहो नासि चिन्तासमा भृति: ।

त्यन्तां तंपगशर्तै स्वयो भव विश्वेषण ।। 4 2 ।।

तिशगय चिंशावावयं प्रीतिपूर्वं नाराधितः ।

प्रोवार किंचिचित्नाता: कारणं च रुषाणुभम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

Nowhere is your enemy, strong or weak; only Varuna was angry with you; now he is also very satisfied. So there is nothing further for you to do or to think. O King! Owing to cares, this body gets weaker and weaker, day by day. So nothing is like cares to lead one to death." When his dear wife said so, the King expressed to her somewhat the cause of his cares, good or bad.

भोजनं न चकारासी चिन्ताविष्टस्या नृप: ।

सुत्वारिज शयने रुष्रे तेषे निद्धि न भूमिपः ॥ 4 4 ॥

प्रातरस्थाय चिन्तातों वासलेन्यादिका: क्रिया: ।

But the King was much absorbed with his cares so that he could not eat nor sleep though his bedding was perfectly white and clear. Early in the next morning, when, getting up from his bed, he was doing anxiously his morning duties, Viśvāmitra came up there.

करीति नृपतित्साविद्धधार्मि: समाप्ततः ॥ 4 5 ॥

क्षता निविदितो राते भृति: सर्वस्वहारकः ।

आगत्योबाच राजानं प्रणामन्ते पुनः पुनः ॥ 46 ॥

When the sentinel informed the King of the arrival of Vísvāmitra, he gave order for him to enter. Vísvāmitra, the Looter of his all and everything, came before him and told the King who repeatedly bowed down to him:

विश्वासि उवाच

राजस्पत्न सर्वसाथे मे देहि वाचा प्रतिश्रुतम् ।

सुवर्णी सूश राजेत्र सत्यवाभ्यव सामप्रम्न।। ४ ७ ॥

“O King! Now leave your kingdom and give me the gold that you promised as Dakṣinā and prove that you are truthful.”

हरिश्रान्त्र जवाच

स्वामिजार्जं तद्वेदे मे मया दर्श किलाप्राना ।

त्यक्ताप्रान्य ग्रविष्यामि मा चित्ता कुर्य कीकीरक १। ४। ८। १।

Harīścandra said: “O Lord! I have given you this vast dominion of mine; so my Kingdom has now become yours; I am leaving this Kingdom and going to somewhere else.

सर्वस्वं मम ते ब्रह्म-गृहीतं विधिविष्ठो ।

सुवर्णदक्षिणं दातुभगतकां हुधाना हिज ॥ 49 ॥

दानं ददायि ते ततवधान्ये स्याद्धानागमः ।

O Kauśika! You need not think a bit for this. O Brāhmaṇa! You have taken my all according to the technical rule; so now I am unable to give you Dakṣinā.

पुनःश्रुकात्योगेन तदा दास्यामि दक्षिणाम् ॥ 50 ॥

इन्युक्तना नृपति: प्राङ्ग पुरं भाष्यां च माध्यांम् ।

राज्यमसी प्रदर्ते वे मया वेधां सुविस्तम् ॥ 51 ॥

If, in time, wealth comes to me, I will at once give you your Daksinā." Saying him thus, the King told his wife Śaivyā and his son Rohita "In this Agnihotra room I say that I have given my vast dominion to the Muni Viśvāmitra."

हस्यश्रधार्यसुवर्ते तनहिंमसपित्वम् ।

त्वक्वा श्रीण शरीराणि सर्वे चास्ये समर्पितम् ॥ 52 ॥

त्वक्वाप्राधोगं याभियाधिम कुत्रधिद्नगढ़े ।

गृह्ञ्तिवं दुनिः सम्यप्राज्यं सर्वेसगृद्धिमत् ॥ 53 ॥

Elephants, horses, chariots, gold and jewels all I have given to him along with my kingdom. What more than this that save us three, everything else I have given to him. "O Maharṣi! Take fully this prosperous dominion; we are going somewhere else to a forest or a mountain cave."

इत्याभाष्य सुतं भार्यां हरिश्रान्नः स्वमंदिता ।

विनिर्भतः सुधामतिमा मानसर्वत द्विज्ञातमम् ॥ 54 ॥

The exceedingly virtus Hairiscandra spoke thus to his wife and son, and, paying respects to the Muni, went out from his house.

ब्रजतं भूपतीं वीक्ष्य भारामुग्रावुभावपि ।

चिन्तातुरी सुतीरामयौ जामनु: श्रुतस्तदा ॥ 55 ॥

Seeing the King going thus away, his wife and son, afflicted with cares, followed him with their sad faces.

हाहाकारो महानासीत्रगारे वीक्ष्य तांतस्था ।

घुळ्गु: ग्राणिनः सर्वे साकेतपुरावासिनः ॥ 56 ॥

Seeing thus, all the inhabitants of Ayodhyā cried aloud, and great consternation and uproar arose in the city.

हा राजन्कि कृतं कर्म कुतः कलेशः: सभागतः ।

बिड्जोरिस महाराज विधिनासपिड्जनेन ॥ 5 57 ॥

O King! What is this act that you have done? How has this suffering come to you! O King! The great Fate, without any consideration, has certainly deceived you.

सर्वे बणीस्तादा दुः:ख्यामानुयुक्तं महीपतिम् ।

विलोक्य भार्यया साधु पुंशेण च महान्यान । ५ ८ ॥

The Brāhmanas, Kṣattriyas, Vaísyas and Śūdras, all the four Varṇas gave vent to their sorrows, when they saw the King going away with his wife and son.

निर्विदुर्बाहणं तं तु दुराचारं भृतीकसः ।

धूर्तीर्यमिति भार्योत दुःखाती बाहाणादयः ॥ ५९ ॥

निराल्य नगरातस्मादिष्यामित्रः क्षितीक्षान् ।

गच्छन्तं तमुवाचेदं समेत्य निरुक्तं वचः ॥ 6० ॥

The Brāhmins and the other inhabitants of the city, all were afflicted with sorrows and began to

abuse the vicious Brāhmaṇa saying that “He is a cheat, etc.”

दक्षिणायाः सुवर्णं मे दत्ता गच्छ नाराधिप ।

नाहं दास्यामि वा बृहि मया तर्चकं सुवर्णकम् ॥ 6 1 ॥

O King! Give the gold for Dakṣinā and then go; or say that you will not be able to give and I will then not take the Dakṣinā.

राज्यं गृहाण वा सर्वे लोपश्छीद्ध विवर्ते ।

दर्तं चेन्मन्यसे राजन्देहि यद्यतप्रतिश्रुतम् ॥ 62 ॥

Or if you entertain within yourself any greed, then take back all your Kingdom. O King! If you think that you have really made this gifts, then give what you have promised."

एवं बुद्रतं गाधेयं हरिश्रान्ने महीपाति: ।

प्राणियन्त सुर्जीन्तात्मा कृतांजलिसुपुटोत्रबीत् ॥ 63 ॥

श्रिति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणो सतमस्कन्ने

एकोनिरिशोरध्यात: 11 19 11

The son of Gādhī was saying so, when the King Harisācandra very humbly bowed down to him with folded palms and said to him.

Here ends the Nineteenth Chapter of the Seventh Book on the taking away of Harisācandra's Kingdom

in the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of

18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XX

On Harisćandra Paying His Dakṣinā

हरिश्रद्र जवा

अदत्ता ते हिरणयं वै न कार्तियामि भोजनम् ।

प्रतिज्ञा मे मुनिश्रेष्ठ शिवादं त्वज सूत्रत ॥ १ ॥

सूर्यवैशसमुद्धतः क्षत्रियोई महीधाति: ।

राजसूयस्य यज्ञस्य कर्ता बाधित्धतो नृपः ॥ २ ॥

Haricścandra said: “O Muni! I will not take my food until I pay you your Daksīnā in gold; know this to be my resolve; therefore O One of good vows! Discard all your anxieties for Daksīnā. I am the King of the Solar dynasty; especially since the time I have completed my Rājasyva Sacrifice, I give to every man whatever he desires.

कर्थं कांगीय नाकारं स्वामिन्दच्चा यदृष्ठया ।

अवश्यमेव दातव्यमूर्णं ते ब्रिजसभा ॥ ३ ॥

स्वयंथो भव प्रदाययामि सुवर्णे मनसेरितम् ।

कक्षित्कालं प्रतीक्षस्य यावतुाप्यायामध्यं धनम् ॥ ४ ॥

So, O Lord! How can it possible that I will not give what I have voluntarily promised myself? O Best of Dvijas! I will certainly pay off your debt. I must give you the gold as you desire; be calm and patient; but you will have to wait one month; and on getting the money I will pay it off to you."

विश्वामित्र जयार

कुतत्ते भविता राजन्यग्रापिततः परम् ।

गर्त राज्यं तथा कोशो बलं चैवाधर्मायनम् ॥ 5 ॥

Book VII Chapter XX

Viśvāmitra said: “O King! Kingdom, treasury and strength are the three sources of income; but you are now deprived of all these. Whence, now, do you expect to get gold? O King! Vain are your hopes to get money; what am I do now? You are now wealthless and how can I, out of greed, give you trouble?

ब्रह्म3र्षा ते महीपाल धनार्थ किं केलोमहम् ।

निर्वर्न त्वां च लोभेन पीडयामि कर्वं पूण १। ६ ।।

तस्माकवय भूपाल न दास्यामिति साम्यन्त् ।

त्वत्सा3र्गां महतीं कामां गच्छाम्यहमतः परम् ॥ 7 ॥

यथेधं जत्र तजेन्द्र भावीपुत्रसमयित्नः ।

सुवर्ण नासि किं तुभ्यं ददामीति वत्थानु ॥ 8 ॥

O King! Better say “I will not be able to give you Daksinā,” and I will then quit my strong expectation and go away as I like. And you, too, can think that you have no gold, so how can you give money and so you can go wherever you like with your wife and son.”

यास उवाच

गच्छन्वायमिदं श्रुत्वा बाहुपान्यच च भूपति: ।

प्रत्युवाच मुनिं बह्नार्थयैं कुरु ददायमहम् ॥ 9 ॥

यम देहो3लिति भार्थाया: पुत्रस्य च हानामयः ।

क्रीत्वा देहे तु ते नृनमुर्णं दाययामि ते द्वित्र ॥ १० ॥ १

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing these words of the Muni, at his time of departure, the King said: "O Brāhmaṇa! Be patient and I will certainly give you your Dakṣinā. O Dvija! My wife, son and I myself are all health; so selling these, I will give you the money; there is no doubt in this.

प्राहकं पश्य विघ्रें दातागत्यां पुरि प्रभो ।

दासभावं गमियामि सदारोड़ं सपुत्रकः ॥ १ १ ॥

गृहाण काँचनं पूर्ण साधं भारद्वयं मुने ।

मौल्येन दत्ता सर्वांत्र: सन्तुषो भव भूधर ॥ १ २ ॥

O Lord enquire whether there is anyone who can purchase us and I will agree to become the slave with my wife and son. O Muni! You can sell all of us and the price you get, you can take two and a half loads of gold out of that and be pleased.

इति बुवझगामाय सह पन्या सुतान्वितः ।

उपया कान्तया सार्थे यांकरः स्वयम् ॥ 13 ॥

या दृष्ट्वा च पुरीं त्यमां मनसो हादकारिणीम् ।

उवाच स कृतार्थोंत्रिम पुरीं पश्यन्सुवर्चसम् ॥ 14 ॥

Thus saying, the King went to Benares where Śaṅkara was staying with his dear consort Umā. The King saw the beautiful city, the sight of which makes one's heart dance with more joy and he said that he had become blessed.

ततो भागीर्याँ प्राय्य सन्ताल देवादितर्पणम् ।

देवार्थनं च निर्वर्त्यं कृतवाचन्वित्वजेकानम् ॥ 15 ॥

Then he went to the banks of the Bhāgirathi and bathed in the Ganges and offered peace-offerings (Tarjana) to the Devas and the Pitrṣ and completing the worship if his Iṣṭa Deva (his own Deity) looked around where he would go.

प्रविष्य वसुधापालो दिव्यां वाराणसी पुरीम् ।

नैषा मनुष्यभृक्तेति शूलपाणे: परिघ्रः ॥ 16 ॥

जगाग पद्धनां कु:वातीः सह पन्या समाकुलः ।

पूर्णी प्रविष्य स नृपो विश्वासमकौत्ता ॥ 17 ॥

The King entering into the beautiful city of Benares began to think that no human being is protecting this city but Śiva Himself is protecting it. So if he lives there, he would not be living in a city which has been given away by him to Viśvāmitra.

दूरुषोय मुनिश्रेशे ब्राह्मणं दक्षिणार्थिनम् ।

तं दूर्वा समनुप्राप्तं विनयावानतोभवत् ॥ 18 ॥

The King, then, distressed much with pain and troubled and being very much bewildered, began to journey on foot with his wife and son and entered into the city and placed his confidence.

प्राह्चे वैवांजलिं कृत्वा हरिष्ठज्ञो महामुनिम् ।

इमे प्राणा: सुतक्षाय प्रिया पत्नी मुने मम ॥ 19 ॥

येन ते कृत्यमस्वलागु गृहाणाछ द्वितोजनम् ।

यच्चान्यत्कार्यमसामभित्तन्यमारख्यातुमहाँति ॥ 20 ॥

At this moment he saw the Muni Viśvāmitra, wanting Dakṣinā and humbly bowed down and spoke with folded palms: "O Muni! My dear wife, my son and I myself are living here; you can take

any of us and have your work done; or say what other work we will have to do for you."

विश्वभित्र उवाच

पूर्णे: स मासो घरू ते दीवातां मम दक्षिणा ।

पूर्व तस्य तिमितं हि सम्यते स्वयचो यदि ॥ 21 ॥

Vísvāmitra said: “You promised that you would pay Dakṣinā at the end of one month; and today that one month is completed; if you remember, then give me the Dakṣinā.”

राजोवाच

ब्रह्मात्यापि सम्पूर्णां मासो ज्ञानतोष्वल ।

तिह्लेकदित्धार्थं चतत्प्रतीक्ष्व स्तवपत्र नावपरम् ॥ 22 ॥

The King said: “O Brāhmaṇa! You are wise and are endowed with the power of tapas (asceticism); as yet one month is not complete; still half a day is remaining; wait till then; and no longer.”

विश्वामित्र उवाच

इवमस्तु महाराज आगमिष्याम्यई पुनः ।

शपं तव प्रदायामयि न चेदघा प्रवच्छसि ॥ 23 ॥

Vísvāmitra said: “O King! Let it be. I will come again and if you do not give me then, I will curse you.”

इत्युक्तावश यव्ये विगे प्रजा चाधिंतयन्तव ।

कवयमये प्रव्छामीय दक्षिणा या प्रतिश्रुता ॥ 24 ॥

कृतः पुष्णित निप्राणि कुजार्थं याप्रमतं मा ।

प्रतिश्रः प्रदुष्टो मे तन याङ्गा कर्थ भवेत् ॥ 25 ॥

Thus saying Vísvāmitra went away. The King then thought within himself how he would pay him back what he had promised. There is no influential friend of mine in this Benares city who can help me with money; where then can I get the requisite money.

राजा वृत्तिचयं प्रोक्ते धर्मशास्त्रेय निश्छितम् ।

यदि प्राणानियुक्त्रामि हृधादया च दक्षिणम् ॥ 26 ॥

I am a Kṣattriya. Pratigraha (begging or accepting any gift) is forbidden to me and how can I beg or accept any gift! According to the code of Dharma, the offering of sacrifices (on one's own behalf), studying, and giving are the three duties ordained to a King.

ब्रह्मस्वहा कृमि: पापो भविष्याय्यभापம்: ।

अथवा प्रेततां यास्ये वर एलासयिक्रयः ॥ 27 ॥

And if I die not paying a Brāhmin's Daksīnā, I will be polluted with the sin of stealing a Brāhmin's property and I will then be born a worm or will became a Preta. So to sell myself (and pay off the debts) is better than this.

सूत उवाच

राजानं व्याकुलं दीनं चिन्तयानयधोगुखम् ।

प्रत्युवाच तदा पत्नी बाध्यगद्धया शिरा ॥ 28 ॥

त्वज चिन्तां महाराज स्वधर्ममैनुपालय ।

प्रेतबढ़र्नीयो हि नर: सत्यबहिष्ठकृतः ॥ 29 ॥

Sūta said: O Rṣis! When the King was thus thinking humbly with his face bent downwards, and in a distracted state of mind, his wife spoke to him with tears in her eyes and in a voice, choked with feelings: "O King! Discard all cares and keep your own Dharma, Truth.

नात: परतंर धर्मं वदन्त पुरुषस्य च ।

यारुणं पुरुषवधाया स्वस्यत्यानुपालनम् ॥ 30 ॥

He who is divorced from Truth is forsaken like a Preta. O Best of all men! To keep one's Truth is one's Dharma; there is no other Dharma superior to it; so the sages declare.

अंगिहोजमपीर्ति च दानाचा: सकताः क्रिका: ।

भवन्ति तस्य वैफल्यं वावयं व्यसानुतं भवेत् ॥ 31 ॥

सत्यमन्त्यमुपृदितं धर्मशास्तेषु धीयातम् ।

ताराणायानुं तद्यतन्तायाकृतानमान् ॥ 32 ॥

He whose words turn out false, his Agnihotra, study, and gifts and all actions become fruitless. Truth is very much praised in the Dharma Śāstra and this Truth raises up and saves the virtuous souls. Similarly falsehood, no doubt, drags a vicious man to hell.

शताश्रधीधानदार्य तारासूरं च पार्थिव: ।

कृत्वा राजा सकृत्यगदिसत्यवचनाच्छ्युतः ॥ 33 ॥

The King Yayāti performed the Horse sacrifice, and the Rājasūya sacrifice and went to Heavens but once he spoke falsely and so he was dislodged from the Heavens."

Book VII Chapter XX

<table><tr><td>राजोबाच</td></tr><tr><td>दंशबुद्धिकरशायं पुत्रसिद्धति बालकः ।</td></tr><tr><td>उच्जतां वक्तुकामासि यद्यावयं गजगामिनि ॥ 34 ॥</td></tr></table>

The King said: “O Thou, going like an elephant! I have my son who will multiply my line; speak out what Thou wishest to say.”

पत्नुवाच

<table><tr><td>राजन्माधुदस्तयं ते पुंपां पुत्रफलाः स्थित्यः।</td></tr><tr><td>तन्मां प्रदाय चित्ने देहै विज्ञाय दक्षिणम् ॥ 35 ॥</td></tr></table>

The Queen said: “O King! The wives are meant for sons (your having me has been fulfilled as there is your son). So sell me for the money value and give the Dakṣinā to the Brāhmin. Let you not deviate from the Truth.

व्यास उवाच

<table><tr><td>एतुद्धास्यपुरुष्य ययी मोहं महीपितः ।</td></tr><tr><td>प्रतिलप्य च संर्तां वै विलालपातिङ्ग:खितः ॥ 36 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>महदुर्द:खिमिं पंद्रे याच्यमेवे ब्वीथि मे ।</td></tr><tr><td>किं तब सित्तसंलापा मम पाप्यव खितृतः ॥ 37 ॥</td></tr></table>

Vyāsa said: Hearing this, the King fainted. Afterwards regaining consciousness, he wept with a grievous heart. "O gentle One! What you have uttered just now has caused me much pain; am I such a Sinner as to forget entirely all your conversations and your sweet smiles!

हा हा त्यका कव्य योगयं वकुनेतख्छुचिधितः ।

<table><tr><td>दुर्बान्यमेतदुचनं कवं वदसि भाविमी ॥ 38 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>इत्युक्ता नृपतिश्रेशो न धीरो द्राविक्रवे ।</td></tr><tr><td>निप्पात मतीषुहे मृच्छेधानिपरितुष्टः ॥ 39 ॥</td></tr></table>

Alas! O Sweet-smiling One! You ought not to speak such words. O Fair One! How have you been able to utter these harsh words not fit to be spoken! Speaking thus, the King became impatient at the idea of selling his wife and fainted and fell to the ground.

<table><tr><td>शयानं भुवि तं दृष्ट्या मृच्छ्यायपि महीपतिम्।</td></tr><tr><td>उवाचेदे सुकरणं राजपुत्री सुदुःखिता ॥ 4 O ॥</td></tr><tr><td>हा महाराज कस्वेदयमध्यानादुपगताम् ।</td></tr><tr><td>यस्त्वं निपतिती भूमौ रंकवच्छरणोचितः ॥ 4 1 ॥</td></tr></table>

Seeing him fainted and lying flat on the ground, the Queen became grievously hurt and spoke with great compassion. "O King! Whose evil have you done that you have fallen into this calamity? Alas! He who is accustomed to sleep in a room adorned with carpets is today like an humble man, sleeping on the ground!

येनैव कोडिणे शितं विग्राणामपवर्जितम् ।

स एव पूथिवीनाथो भूवि स्वर्धिति मे पति: ॥ 4 2 ॥

हा कई किं तवानेने कृतं देव महीक्षिता ।

यदिद्रोप्रेस्तुल्योउर नीत: पापामिमां दशाम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

The King who gave crores and crores of golden mohurs to the Brāhmins, that same King, my husband is lying now on the ground! Alas! What a painful thing! O Fate! What has this King done to you that You have thrown this Indra and Upendra like King in this dire calamity!"

इत्सुकता सापि सुश्रेणी भूढ्छिता निप्पणत ६ ।

भर्तुईःखामहाशेगणासहोनातिपीक्षिता ॥ 4 4 ॥

शिग्यूर्धेखा शुभाधिष्टः प्राह वाक्यं सुवृत्ति: ।

तात तात प्रदेहानं मातमें देहि भोजनम् ॥ 4 5 ॥

धूने बल्वंतजी जाता जिह्जागे मेर्तिगुधात् ॥ 4 6 ॥

इति शीमदैबीभागते महापुर्णणे सप्तमकन्धे हीक्षान्द्रोपाखाने

विशोराध्यय: 11 20 11

Thus saying, the beautiful queen (of good hips) very much grieved by the sight of her husband's pain fell down unconscious on the ground. Then the boy prince, seeing father and mother both senseless, lying on the ground, became very much troubled, and, becoming hungry, cried "O Father! O Father! I am very hungry; give me food to eat; O Mother! O Mother! My tongue is being parched; give me food to eat" and the boy began to weep repeatedly.

Here ends the Twentieth Chapter of the Seventh

Book on the earnestness of Harisācanda to pay

off the Dakṣinā in the Mahāpurānam

Śrimaddevībhāgāvatam, of 18,000

verses, by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XXI

On the Sorrows of Harisćandra

सूत उवाच

एतस्मिन्नरते प्राणत विश्वामिजे महातपः ।

अन्तकेन समः क्रूष्टो धर्न सर्व याचितु हदा ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! At this moment, the Muni Viśvāmitra, endowed with his power of tapas, came up there, very energy as if the God of Death, to ask of his wealth.

तमातोलंच हरिश्रान: परपात भुवि मूर्दितः।

स वारीण तपाध्यूक्ष राजानामिदमवबीत् ॥ 2 ॥

उत्तिष्ठोत्ति राजेन्द्र स्वां दद्वेश्छदक्षिणाम् ।

ऋणं धारयतां दुःखमहान्यदिन वर्धेत ॥ 3 ॥

Seeing him Hariścandra fallen thus senseless on the ground, Viśvāmitra, then, began to sprinkle water on his body. O King! The man who is involved in a debt his troubles increase day by day. So get up and pay your promised Dakṣinā.

आणाययान: स तदा हिमगीतेत वारीणा ।

अवाय्य चेतनां राज विश्वामिवमेव्य च ॥ 4 ॥

पुनर्भौं सभायेदे हाय क्रोधं ययौ मुनि: ।

सभाशयस्य च राजानं वाक्यमात हिंजीतमः ॥ 5 ॥

The King, thus sprinkled with water, cold as snow, regained his consciousness; but, seeing Visvāmitra, he fainted again. At this, the Dvija Visvāmitra consolet him and angrily spoke to him thus:

विश्वासित्र उवाच

दीयतां दक्षिणा सा मे यदि दीयमेवक्षेसे ।

सत्येनाकं: प्रतपति सत्ये तिष्ठिति मेदिवि ॥ 6 ॥

सत्ये चोकाः परो धर्मे: स्वर्गः सत्ये प्रतिष्ठितः ।

“O King! If you want to maintain your steadiness, give, then my Dakṣinā. Look! It is Truth that makes the Sun shine; It is the Truth that has stationed this Earth in its position; what to speak more, even the Svarga is established on Truth; so the greatest Dharma lies in Truth.

अधोपेधसहं सु तस्यं च तुलया धृतम् ॥ 7 ॥

अश्धोपेधसह्याद्धि सत्यमेकं विशिष्यते ।

अथवा किं ममेतेन प्रोक्रेनान्तिप्र प्रयोजनम् ॥ 8 ॥

If the fruit of the thousand Asvamedhas be held in one pan and Truth be held on the other pan of the balance, then Truth outweighs the thousand horse sacrifices or what need I to speak all about this!

मदीयां दक्षिणां राजन दास्यति भवान्यदि ।

अस्ताचलगते हर्कि ग्रण्यामि त्यामतो धूमम् ॥ 9 ॥

इन्युक्ना स ययी विघ प्रा जाचीम्द्यातृः ।

दुःखीभूतोउवनी निःस्वो नृसंगं सुविनाउर्दितः ॥ 10 ॥

O King! If you fail to give my Dakṣinā before the Sunset, I will, no doubt, curse you. Saying this, Viśvāmitra went away. The King also became very terrified. The wealthless King was pained by the words of the Munī; but he was more troubled with the thought how he would pay him and keep to Truth.

सूत उवाच

एतस्मिन्नरो तत्र ब्राह्मणो वेदपारगः ।

ब्रह्मणैबहुभि: सार्थ निर्वयी स्वगृहाद्रिः ॥ 11 ॥

ततो राजी तु तं दूद्वा आयांतं तापसं चित्यत् ।

उवाच वाक्यं राजानं धर्मार्थसहितं तदा ॥ 12 ॥

त्रयाणामयि वर्णानं चिता बाहाणं उद्यन्ते ।

चितुरद्यं हि पुर्णे प्रतील्यं न संग्रयः ॥ 13 ॥

तस्मादयं प्रार्थनीयो धनार्थमिति मे मति: ।

Sūta said: O Rṣis! At this time, a Brāhmīn, skilled in the Vedas, with many other Brāhmīns, started out of his house, at that very place. The queen, then seeing the Brāhmīn ascetic close by, addressed the King in words reasonable and in accordance with the Dharma, O Lord! A Brāhmīn is considered the father of the other three Varnas (i. e., Kṣattriyas, Vaiśyas, and Śūdras) and a son can certainly take the father's things; so it is my intention that you beg your wealth from this Brāhmīn.

Book VII Chapter XXI

राजोवाच

नाहं प्रतिग्रहं कांक्षे क्षत्रियोर्हं सुमध्यमे ॥ 14 ॥

याचनं खलु विघाणां क्षत्रियाणां न विहते ।

गृहर्ि विघो घनानां पूजनीयोर्स्त सर्वदा ॥ 15 ॥

तस्मादुग्नरै याव्यः स्यात्स्विधायां विशेषत: ।

The King said: “O One of thin waist! To beg suits the Brāhmanas; it is prohibited to the Kṣattriyas; I being a Kṣattriya do not wish to take anything as gift.” The Brāhmins are the Gurus of all the Varnas. So they are always to be respected. It is not proper to beg from a Brāhmin; especially the Kṣattriyas never ask anything from the Brāhmins; it is totally prohibited.

यजनाध्ययनं दानं क्षत्रियस्य विधीयते ॥ 16 ॥

शरणागतानामययं प्रजानां प्रतिपालनम् ।

न चायेवं तु चकच्छं देहिती कृपणं चचः ॥ 17 ॥

ददामीचेय मे देवि हदये निहित चतः ।

अर्जित कृत्रचिद्धन्वं ब्राह्माणया ददायमम् ॥ 18 ॥

Offering oblations, study, gift and the governing of subjects and protecting those that take refuge is the Dharma of the Kṣattiryas but they would never, never, ask any other man “Give, give,” and utter these words indicative of humility O Devi! The words “I am giving you” are impressed within my heart; so I will earn money from some other source and give that to the Muni.

पल्युवाच

काल: समविषयक: परिघवसमामानाद: काल: ।

काल: करोति पुरुषं दातारं याचितारं च ॥ 19 ॥

विशेषा विद्या राजा कुब्दोनालिवसीमा ।

राज्याविरस्तः सीख्याच पश्य कालस्य वेधिए् ॥ 20 ॥

The Queen said: “O King! Time keeps some men in one and the same state; again it throws others into troubles; Time it is that give respect to one and again it is Time that gives disrespect to others, Time it is that makes one a donor and it is the same Time that makes another a beggar. See even the Rsi Viśvāmitra, learned and endowed with the strength of Tapasa, becoming angry has deprived you of your kingdom and happiness and

has thus done quite an irreligious act in the shape of tormenting others. You can now judge in this the wonderful workings of Time."

राजोवार

असिना तीक्ष्याधोगे वरं निहा हित्य काता ।

न तु मानं परित्यज्य देहि देवीनि पार्थितम् ॥ 21 ॥

क्षयिगोर्भ महाभागे न याचे किंचिद्यद्वहम् ।

द्वात्मि वारुं तिन्चं हिं पुजवीयाँतिजं धनम् ॥ 22 ॥

The King said: “I would rather cut off my tongue into two pieces by a sharp sword than I would quit my Kṣattrīya pride; and I would never be able to utter the words ‘Give, give,’ O Forunate One! I am a Kṣattrīya; so I never ask anything of anyone. I always say that, by the strength of my arms, I will earn money and pay off my debt.”

पत्युवाच

यदि ते हि महाराज याचितुं न क्षमं मनः ।

अहं तु न्यायाती दत्ता देदेरिपि सवासबेः ॥ 23 ॥

अहं शास्या च पल्या च रथ्या चैव महावृते ।

मन्मौल्यं संगृहीत्वाय गुर्वंधे: संगृदीयात्म ॥ 24 ॥

The Queen said: “O King! Indra and the other Devas have given me over duly to your hands. So I am your religious (legal) wife; especially I have got education and I ought to be protected. Therefore O Luminous One! If you do not like to beg then you can sell me and pay off your Dakṣinā.”

एतद्वावस्यमुपश्यत हरिश्रउते महीपति: ।

कई कदमिती प्रोष्य विललापावितु: बित्त : II 25 ||

भार्यां च भयः प्राहेदं क्रिस्तां वचनं मम ।

विश्रणापानिदन्यत्वासीचलमुपयास्यसि Ⅱ 26 ||

The King Harisācandra became grieved very much to hear these words and lamented, saying "O What a painful thing is this! What a painful thing is this!" His wife again spoke: "O King! Will we, afterwards, be burnt by the fire of curse from a Brāhmin and thus lowered very much? So keep my word now."

न युत्रतैलोनं च महद्धेतोनं राजस्थेतोनं च भोगहेतोः ।

दद्वस युवंधितो मया तं स्वस्वतं तं स्वस्त कंकव ॥ 27 ॥

इति शीमदैवीभागवते महापुराणे सनमककभे इरिक्रजीपालयाने

एकविशो3ध्याः ।। २१ ।। १।

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

You are selling me, not because that you are infatuated with desire for gambling nor you are deprived of all knowledge by enjoyments in worldly things nor you are selling me owing to avert the danger of your kingdom. It is that you are selling me to pay off the debt to your Guru. So

nothing sinful a fault will be incurred by you. So sell me and keep to Truth and the fruits thereof." Here ends the Twenty-first Chapter of the Seventh Book on the description of the sorrows of Hariścandra in the Mahāpurānam, Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXII

On Selling Harisćandra's Wife

व्यास उवाच

स तया नोष्टामान्तु राजा पल्प्या पुनः पुनः ।

प्राह भई क्रोपोय्य चिवकयं ते सुनिष्टुणः ॥ १ ॥

नृशंसीरपि यत्कर्तु न शक्यं तत्करोम्यहम् ।

यदि ते भाजते वाणी वतृभीद्बसुनिव्युम् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the Queen Mādhavī requested repeatedly the King, He said: "O Good-Auspicious One! When you have not met with any scruple to utter clearly these harsh and cruel words, I will do that act now which the most ruthless persons do not dare to do.

एवमुक्लवा ततो राजा नाचा नागरमातुः ।

अवतार्य तदा रोगे तां भार्याँ नृपसत्तभः ॥ ३ ॥

वायामाद्रकण्डस्तु ततो वचनमब्रवीत् ।

भो भो नागरिका: सर्वे शुणुध्यं चर्चनं मा ॥ ४ ॥

Saying this, the King went with his wife, very distressed, to the city. Placing her on the public road, the King cried out in a voice choked with feelings and eyes full of tears: "O Citizens! Hear you all. Do any one of you require any maid-servant?

कस्यचिद्धादि कार्य स्याहास्या प्राणोष्टया मन ।

स ब्जीतु त्वायतुची यावत्सं धार्यायम्हमू ॥ 5 ॥

तेउबुब्यपिडता: कस्त्वे पर्नी विक्रेतुभागतः ।

This lady is dearer to me than my life. If any of you be able to offer price of her as I will declare, then let him give it out quickly." The Pandits then said: "Who are you? Why are you come here to sell your wife?"

राजोवाच

किं मां पृष्छथ करत्वं भो नृशंसोहममानुष: ॥ 6 ॥

गढ़सी वार्जिस कठिनस्ततः पापं करोभ्यमू।

The King said: “Are you asking me of my introduction? Hearthen: I am a heartless brute and not fit to be called a man; or I am a Rāksasa; nay, I am more than that; I am prepared to do this sinful act.”

व्यास उवाच

तं शब्दं सहसा शुचा कौशिको विग्रहपधूक् ॥ 7 ॥

युद्धलयं समाधाय श्रिंश्रत्यभाषत ।

समर्पयस्य मे दासीमहं केता धनपूदः ॥ 8 ॥

अस्ति मे वित्तमतुलं सुकुमारी च मे प्रिया ।

गृहकर्म ने शक्योति कतृम्यमात्रच्छ मे ॥ 9 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing this, Kauśika suddenly assumed the form of an old man and came out atā spoke to Hariścandra: “I am master of boundless wealth; so I am able to give you the money you want; I am ready to purchase the maidservant by giving an equivalent wealth. Better give me the maidservant.

अर्ह गृह्मीय दासीं तु काति दास्यामि ते धनम् ।

एवमुक्ते तु विश्रण हरिश्रान्य भुपते: ॥ 10 ॥

विद्यौर्ण तु मनो दुःखात्र चैन किंकितदायित् ।

My wife is exceedingly delicate; she is unable to do all the household work; so let me have the maid. But say quickly what value am I to pay?" When the Brāhmin spoke this, Hariścandra felt his heart, as it were, torn asunder; so he could not for the moment speak anything.

Book VII Chapter XXII

विष्य उवाच

कर्मण्ड्य वेयोरूपशीलानां तव योचितः ॥ १ १ ॥

अनुक्पादिं वित्त गृहाणाये मेउलालम ।

धर्मोगतेषु यदूद्धुं तिजयो मौल्ये नरस च ॥ १ २ ॥

द्विश्रिललक्षणोपेता दक्षा शीलनुणान्विता ।

कोटिमील्यं सुवर्णस्य तिजयः पुंसलतावृष्टिन् ॥ १ ३ ॥

The Brāhmaṇa said: “Take an equivalent amount of money according to the age, beauty, qualifications and capabilities of your wife and hand her over to me. Hear about the prices of the male and female servants as written in the Dharma a Śāstras: The price of a female servant clever, good, well-qualified and possessing thirty-two auspicious qualities is one Koti gold mohurs; and the male servant similarly qualified fetches one Arbuda gold mohurs.”

इत्याकर्ण वंचलस्य हरिङ्गज्ञे महीपति: ।

दुःखेन महात्रियहो न चैन किंचिद्वबवित् ॥ 14 ॥

ततः स विगो नृपते: पुरातो बल्कलोपरि ।

धर्न निताया केशेषु धुत्वा राजीमकर्षेत् ॥ 15 ॥

Hariscandra became very much pained to hear the Brāhmīn speaking thus; but he could not say anything. The Brāhmīn then placed in front of the King the money over a bark and caught hold of the hair of the Queen and was ready to drag Her.

राज्यवाच

मुंच मुंचार्य मां सही यावत्यश्यामर्थे सुतम् ।

दुर्लंभे दर्शन विध प्रपत्रस्य पविष्यति ॥ 16 ॥

पर्यहे पुरु मोर्मेव मारतं दायरतां गताम् ।

मां माम्याश्री राजपुत्र न सूष्यासई त्वायाउ्धून ॥ 17 ॥

ततः स बालः सहसा दूद्वा कहां तु माततः ।

समप्यधावद्येति. वदन्माशुवितोचनः ॥ 18 ॥

The Queen said: “O Ārya! Let me see once the lotus-face of my son; leave me once. O Brāhimin! Please see that it will be hard for me again to see this boy. O Son! Behold! Your mother is now a slave. So, Prince, do not touch me. I am not fit now to be touched by you. The boy, then, seeing the mother suddenly snatched away, cried out “O

Mother! O Mother!" and followed her with tears in his eyes.

हृते वक्षं समाकर्ध-काकपक्षधरः सखल्न ।

तमागतं द्विजः कोधादुलमध्याहनत्तदा ॥ १९ ॥

वदंतसाधपि सोःयेति नैव मुबुक्ति मात्तम् ।

That boy tumbled at every step still he caught hold of the mother's clothing by his hand and began to accompany her. The Brāhmīn, seeing this behaviour of the boy, became impatient with anger and began to beat him. Still the boy wept, saying, Mother! Mother! and never quitted the hold of his mother.

राज्यवाच

प्रसादं कुक मे नाव क्षिणीचेमं हि वाकाम्का ॥ 20 ॥

क्रीतापि नाहं घचिता विवैनं कार्यसाधिका ।

इल्थं मामल्यभायाया: प्रसादं कुक मे प्रभो ॥ 21 ॥

The Queen said: "O Lord! Have mercy on me and purchase this boy also. Though you are purchasing me, yet without this boy I will not be able to do your work. My fate is bad; therefore this calamity has happened. Shew this favour to me.

वाहाण उवाच

गृह्धातं चितमेत्ते दीयतां मम वाकालः ।

स्तीपुंसो धर्मशास्त्रजैः कृतमेव हिं वेतनम् ॥ 22 ॥

शर्ते सहसं लक्षं च कोटिपोल्थं तथापरेः ।

द्वार्जिलतक्षणोपेता दक्षा शीलगुणान्विता ॥ 23 ॥

कोटीपोल्थे स्थितः प्रोक्तं पुचस्य तथातर्भुम् ।

"The Brāhmaṃ said: Take this money and give me the boy too. For the Wise in the Dharma Śāstras fix such to be the prices of a female and a male. The other Pandits make differences in the prices, e.g., one hundred, one thousand, one lakh, one crore and so on, according to the different qualifications. But for the female, who is skilled in all actions modest, of good behaviour, and well qualified and, on whose body the thirty-two auspicious signs are seen, her price is one Koti gold mohurs and for a man qualified, once Arbuda gold mohurs.

Śrimaddevibhagavatam

सूत उवाच

तथैव तस्य तहितं पुरः खिलं पटे पुनः ॥ 24 ॥

प्रगृह्ठ बालकं मात्रा सहैकस्थमबन्यन्त् ।

Sūta said: O King! The Brāhmin then gave over the price of the boy as decided, in gold mohurs in front of the King over a bark and then tied both the mother and son.

प्रतस्थे स गृहं क्षिप्र तथा सह मुखास्थितः ॥ 2 5 ॥

प्रदक्षिणु तु सा कृत्वा जानुभयं प्रगत स्थित ।

बाष्ययर्गेकूल दीना तिवंदं बचनवम्बबीत् ॥ 26 ॥

यदि दर्तं यदि हृतं ब्राह्मणातर्पिता यदि ।

तेन पुर्णेय ने भर्ती हरिश्रूरेजस्तु वै पुनः ॥ 27 ॥

He, then, gladly and without any delay, carried them to his home. At the time of departure, the Queen circumambulated the King and, kneeling down, bowed down to him and, in that state of humility, began to speak: "If ever I have done any charities, if ever I have poured oblations on the Fire, if ever I have satisfied the Brāhmins, then, by that virtue, Hariścandra will again be my husband."

पदयोः पतितां द्वृदा प्राणेध्योर्पि गरीयसीम् ।

हाहेति च वद्वाजा बिललापाकुलैन्द्रिया ॥ 28 ॥

विद्युक्तेयं कंवं जाता सत्यशिलगुणान्विता ।

यूबच्छायाउपि यूबं तं न जातीत कदाचन ॥ 29 ॥

Seeing his wife, dearer then his life, fallen on his feet, the King became very distracted and lamented, crying “Alas! Alas! The shadow of a tree never leaves the tree; but you being verily modest and endowed with all qualifications, are now separated from me.”

एवं भाषी वहित्वा5थ सुसंवेब्धे परस्यम् ।

पूत्र च तनुयाचेदे मां लवे हित्वा क्र यास्यसि । 30 ॥

Speaking thus reasonably with his wife, the King said to his son: "O Child! Where will you go, leaving me here?

कां दिवां प्रति यास्यामि को मे दुःखं चिवायेत् ।

राज्यलायो ने मे दुःखं चलवासे न मे दुःखं ॥ 31 ॥

वत्पूत्रेण विल्योगो मे एवमाह स प्रपृति: ।

Where shall I go now? and who will stop my miseries? The King, then, spoke to the Brāhimin:

“O Brāhim! The pain that I experience in the separation from my son, I did not feel on the occasion of quitting my kingdom or on my being exiled in a forest.”

सद्धुरीभ्याय हि सदा लोके भाग्यां भविति हि ॥ 32 ॥

मया त्यक्ताप्रस कल्याणि दुःखेन विविधोबितता।

इद्वाकुकंशसम्भूतं सर्वेनाज्यसुवोचिनम् ॥ 33 ॥

मामीद्धरो पतिं प्राय्य दासीभावं तला हुसि ।

“O Auspicious One! The husband, good natured in this world, nourishes always his wife and keeps her always in comfort and happiness. But I am such a bad husband of yours, as I have left you and made you float in the sea of sorrows, Born in the Iksyāku family, I inherited the kingdom and its pleasures; but, Alas! Your getting such a husband has now been reduced to slavery!

इनुगो मज्जमानं मां सुमहचोकारमारे ॥ 34 ॥

को मापुढ़रते देखे पीर्णाण्ड्वयान्धितरंः ।

O Devi! I am merged in this ocean of sorrows and troubles. Who will rescue me, by narrating this ocean of sorrows and troubles. Who will rescue me, by narrating this story of the Purāṇas!"

सूत जयाच

प्ययतस्तस्य तार्जरें: कशापाते: सुदारुकर्णे: ॥ 35 ॥

घातविचा तु विघ्रोगे नेतुं समुपचक्रमे ।

Sūta said: O King! The Brāhmīn, then, began to take away the queen and the boy, whipping them, in the face of the King.

नीयमानी तु तो दूर्वा भार्थियुगे स पार्थिवः ॥ 36 ॥

बिलतपालितु:खातिं नि: श्वर्योषां पुतः पुनः।

Seeing his wife and son being dragged away in that state, the King's pain knew no bounds and be frequently sighed and sighed and bitterly wept aloud.

यां न वायुर्गे वास्डविद्यो न चउने न पृथकजना: ॥ 37 ॥

दूर्धवतः पुरा पर्नीं सेयं दासीत्यमानात ।

सुर्वंक्षीरप्रमुक्तोर्गे सुकुमाराकरांगृति: ॥ 38 ॥

संग्रापो विक्रयं बालो चिछ्याममसु सुर्भर्तिमम् ।

Alas! My dear wife, whom the Moon, the Sun, Wind or any other body could not see ere this, has

Book VII Chapter XXII

become now reduced to slavery today! Oh! How beautiful and gentle are the fingers of my child? He has been sold off to-day, being born in the Solar Dynasty? Alas! Fie on my foolish understanding!

हा प्रिये हा शिशो वल मामानार्थवर दुनंवः ॥ ३ ९ ॥

देवाधीदान्शा प्राणो न पृतोरंभि तथापि चिक् ।

Oh my Dear! Oh my child Rohitāśva! Your this wretched condition is due to my Anāyra irrespective bad maxims! Oh! Through the mockery of the Daiva, I have got this distress! Fie on Me!

सृत उवाच

एवं वलिपणो राजो3प्र विशोधेनतरपीयत ॥ 40 ॥

वृक्षगोहाविधिस्तुगैस्तावाद्य त्वरान्वतः ।

अयानंतर मुनिवेशुस्त्वाजगाम महातपाः ॥ 41 ॥

सशिष्यः कीशिकन्नोउसी निशुः कूरवर्गनः ।

Vyāsa said: The King was lamenting thus when the Brāhimn disappeared with them, in the very tall trees and walls of palatial buildings. At this time the cruel fiendish Muni, endowed with great power of asceticism came there quickly, accompanied by his disciples.

विश्रामित्र उवाच

या तयोक्ता पुरा राजनाराज्यस्य दक्षिणा ॥ 4 2 ॥

तां ददस्य महाबाहो यदि सत्यं पुरस्कृतम् ।

Vísvāmitra said: “O One of mighty arm! If you think it your duty to respect Truth, then pay me the Dakṣinā of Rājasūya sacrifice that you promised before.”

हरिश्रत्र उवाच

नमस्क्तोम राजर्ष गृहाणेमां स्वदक्षिणम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

राजसूयस्य यागस्य सा यमोक्ति पुरानंत्रय ।

Hariścandra said: “O Rājarsī! I bow down to Thee. O Sinless One! Now take the Dakṣinā of the Rājasūya Sacrifice that I promised to pay you before.”

विश्वामित्र उवाच

कृती लक्ष्मीद्रं उद्यं दक्षिणार्थ प्रदीयते ॥ 44 ॥

एतदाचक्ष्व तजेन्द्र यथा उद्यं त्वयाजतिन् ।

Viśvāmitra said: "O King! Whence have you

collected these gold Mohurs that you are now paying me as my Dakṣinā. How have you earned this? Say."

राजोवाच

किम्नेन भहागभ कथितेन तवानघ ॥ 4 5 ॥

गोकस्तु वधौने विश्र शुनेनानेन सुत्रत ।

Harīścandra said: "O Dvija! O Sinless One! What use is there in telling this to you. It will increase agony by hearing O One of good vows."

ऋषिकवाच

अशगतं नैव गृह्ठामि शनमेव प्रयच्छ मे ॥ 46 ॥

इध्यसायानं राजकव्यस्य यथातथम् ।

Víśvāmitra said: “I won’t accept money earned not rightly. Give what you have acquired by rightful means. Say truly how you have acquired it.”

राजोत्ता

मया देखी तु सा भार्यं विक्रीता कोटिसामितः ॥ 47 ॥

निषेक: पुत्रो रोहितोख्यो विक्रीतोउबुदंसख्याय ।

विश्रैकाद्य कोट्यास्तं सुवर्णस्य गृहाण मे ॥ 48 ॥

Harísćandra spoke: “O Brāhmīn! I have sold my wife the Devi Mādhavī for one kotī gold mohurs and my son for ten kotīs of gold mohurs. So take this eleven Kotī Gold Mohurs from me.”

सूत उवाच

तिद्धां स्वल्यमालक्ष्य दारविक्रियसभ्यम्बम् ।

शोकाधिपूर्ति राजानं कुपितः कौशिकोउर्वतीं ॥ 49 ॥

Sūta said: Seeing the gold collected out to the sale of wife and son very small, and seeing the King overpowered with pain and sorrow, Kauśika angrily spoke:

त्रहिषकार्य

राजसूयस्य नृत्रस्य नैषा भवति दक्षिणा ।

अन्यदुन्याद्य हिंग्धं मम्पूर्णं वेन सा भवेत् ॥ 50 ॥

क्षत्रवन्धो मेमांगं त्वं सरुषी यदि दक्षिणाम् ।

मन्यसे तर्हि तक्षिंषं पर्य त्वं मे परं बतम् ॥ 51 ॥

तपसोउस्य सुतालस्य ब्राह्मणस्यात्मस्य च ।

मघृषावास्य चोप्रय सुब्द्रस्याध्ययनस्य च ॥ 52 ॥

"O King! The Dakṣinā of the Rājasīya Sacrifice

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

cannot be so small; so collect quickly other money to complete it. O Vilest of Kṣaṭtrīyas! If you think this much to be proper for me, see first the enormous power of mine that I possess of my tapasyā, practised duly of my pure Brāḥmaṇaṇhood, of my violent power and of my chaste study and then you can pay my Dakṣinā.”

राजोबाच

अन्यदास्यामि भगव-काल: क्रश्रित्रीभ्यताम् ।

अभूर्वोयाति विक्रीता पत्नी पुरुषं बालकः ॥ ५३ ॥

Hariścandra said: "O Bhagavan! I have sold just now my wife; and so wait for some time and I will collect more gold and will pay that to you."

विश्रामिक उवाच

चतुर्भागः स्थितो योउं दिवस्यस्य नाराधिप ।

एष एव प्रतीक्ष्यो मे वक्त्वयं नोरर्त तव्या ॥ 54 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीर्षागवते महापुराणे सतमककन्धे

द्विश्चितोश्वाय: 11 22 11

Vísvāmitra said: "O King The fourth part of the day is now remaining; I will wait till then. After this you won't expect any other reply from me."

Here ends the Twenty-second Chapter of the Seventh Book on the selling of Harisācandra's wife in the

Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam,

of 18,000 verses, by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXIII

On Hariscandra as a Slave of the Cāndāla

व्यास उवाच

तेवेयुक्न्वा राजानं निर्थुर्ण निरुर्णं वचः ।

तदादाय धर्म पूर्ण कृपितः कौशिको ययो ॥ 1 ॥

विश्वामिति गते राजा ततः शोकमुपागतः ।

शासोच्छ्वासं मुहुः कृत्वा प्रोवाचोच्छौधामुः ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Speaking these harsh and cruel words to the King, the Muni took that money and went away. When Viśvāmitra went away, the King Hariścandra became very much perplexed with sorrows and sighed frequently. He then began to say with his bent downwards.

वितन्मैतेन यस्यातिर्याय प्रेतेन गच्छति ।

स बबौतु त्वरायुक्तो यामे तिहिति भासकरः ॥ 3 ॥

"Suffering from constant pain and troubles, I am now turned into a Preta; if any body finds me serviceable, he may purchase me with value in gold as proper; but he should do this quickly before the sun sets."

अथाजगाम त्वरितो धर्मश्रांडालक्षपघुक ।

युगीभी विकृतोरकः समशुतो दन्तुरोउष्णी ॥ 4 ॥

कृष्णो लब्योद्रः सिनधः कालाः पुर्वाधामः ।

हस्तजजरयिष्ठ शवामार्यैरलंकृतः ॥ 5 ॥

Dharma, then, assuming the form of a heartless Cāndāla came there quickly to test Hariścandra.

The body of that low mean person was of a black colour, his air looking ferocious, his belly elongated, body emitting stench odours, teeth very long, and his face, covered with beards. He had one bamboo in his hand; in his neck, the bones of the dead were hanging and his chest was very distorted.

चाण्डल उवाच

अहं गृह्वामि दास्तवे भूत्याशः सुगहान्माम ।

क्षिप्रामावङ्क्य मौल्यं किमेत्ते संप्रदीयते ॥ 6 ॥

The Cāndāla said: "I am in urgent need of a servant; I will keep you as my slave; say, then, quickly what is, your price?

व्यास उवाच

तं ततद्रभायालक्ष्य क्रूरुष्टि सुनिभुणम् ।

वदन्तमित्रु:शीलं कल्वमिव्याद पार्थिवः ॥ 7 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the cruel, extremely ferocious and heartless Cāndāla said thus, the King Harisācandra was surprised to see his appearance and said: "Who are you?"

वाण्डाल उवाच

चाण्डालोड़हिंह ख्यातः प्रवेरिति नृपोतम ।

शासनं सर्वदा तिह मृतदैलापहारकः ॥ ८ ॥

Book VII Chapter XXIII

The Cāṇḍāla said: “O King! I am the famous Cāṇḍāla, Pravīra; you will have to remain always subject to me and to collect the clothes of the dead persons.”

एवमुक्तिदा राजो वचनं चेदमब्रवीत् ।

ब्राहण: क्षत्रियो वांरि गृह्ञान्तिति मतिमां ॥ 9 ॥

उनमाधोनो। धर्म मध्यमय च मध्यमः ।

अयमयाधमश्रेव इति प्राहूर्भनीषिणः ॥ 10 ॥

Hearing his word, the King said: "I want to be purchased by a Brāhmin or a Kṣattriya. See! The sages say, that the Dharma of good people is excellent; the Dharma of the persons intermediate is middling; and the Dharma of the mean is depressing. You belong to low and mean class. So my Dharma cannot be observed if I remain in your house."

चाण्डल उवाच

एवमेव त्वया धर्मः कथितो नृपसत्तम ।

अविवर्धा त्वया राजस्थानोकर्त ममाप्रातः ॥ १ १ ॥

The Cāndāla said: “O King! This is the Dharma of yours now mentioned by you; then why did you mention that anybody can purchase you); without any previous consideration, you spoke before me.

विद्यारयित्वा वो बृते सौधीर्ध तपणे नतः ।

सामान्ययेन तत्रोफमविराचर्य त्वयानउनच ।। २ ॥

यदि सत्य प्रमाणं ते गृहीते3सि न संशयः ।

He who speaks with preconsideration stains his desired object; but, O Sinless One! You did not consider and you spoke that ordinarily. However, if I take your words that you spoke first to be true, then you are no doubt, purchased by me."

हशिश्र उवाच

असत्यारकें गछलेसघ हुके नराधम: ॥ १३ ॥

तन्त्रश्छालता सार्थी न वरा मे हास्त्वलता ।

Harīscandra said: “The villain that speaks untruth, goes downright to a terrible hell; so to become a Cāndāla is far better for me than to use an untrue word.”

व्यास उवोच

तस्यैवं वदतः प्राप्तो विश्वाधिम्न स्त्वोनिधिः ॥ 14 ॥

क्रोधापार्थिवृत्ताक्षः प्राहे चेदं नाराधिपम् ।

चण्डालोयं मनस्यं ते दातुं चितमुपस्थितः ॥ 15 ॥

कमासा दीयते महामगेशा यजदक्षिणाः ।

Vyāsa said: O King! When the King was speaking thus, the ascetic Viśvāmitra arrived there out of anger and impatience; he rolled his eyes and said: "This Cāndāla is come to give you your desired money; why, then, are you not giving me the remnant of my Dakṣinā!"

राजेश्वर

भगवन्युर्विंशोत्यामातानं वेधि कौशिका ॥ १६ ॥

कर्थ चाण्डालादास्तवं गमिष्ये वितकामतः ।

Harīścandra said: “O Kauśika! Nothing is unknown to you. My this body is born for the Solar Line; how then can I accept this slavery of a Cāndāla!”

विश्रामित ज्ञाव

यदि चण्डालयितं त्यमातयिक्रमजं मम ॥ 17 ॥

न प्रज्ञास्यि चेताईं ग्रणयामि त्वामसंगयम् ।

चण्डालतादयव विज्ञाद्रेहि मे दक्षिणाधनम् ॥ 18 ॥

विना चण्डालम्भुना नान्यः कर्धिछ्जनप्रदः ।

धनेनाईं विज्ञा राजक्र यास्यामि न संग्रहः ॥ 19 ॥

इदानीमेव मे विन्तं न प्रदास्यसि चेषुः ।

दिनेउर्धयिकाणेसे तत्वां शापानिनना दहे ॥ 20 ॥

Víśvāmitra said: “If you do not sell yourself to a Cāṇḍala, be certain that I will just now put you under my curse. Give me immediately my Dakṣinā, be it whether from a Cāṇḍala or from a Brāhmaṇa. There is no other purchaser at present than this Cāṇḍala. But know this as certain that I won’t go back until I get my money. O King! If you do not give me money just now, then when half the Ghatikā of the day is remaining, I will burn you up by my fire of anger.”

व्यास उवाच

हरिश्रस्ततो राजा मृतविष्छुतर्जीवितः ।

प्रसीक्षिति वदन्यादौ ऋषेजैग्राह विहृतः ॥ 21 ॥

Šrimaddevīthāgavatam

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing these words of Viśvāmitra, the King became almost dead; bewildered with fear, then he clasped the feet of the Rṣi and said, “Be friendly, please.”

हरिश्रनु उवाच

दासोउस्यातोंस्म दीनोस्म त्वद्धकघि सिक्षेपतः ।

प्रसादं कुरु विषर्षं कर्धक्षण्डालसंकरः ॥ 2२२ ॥

भवेयं विज्ञेशेण तव कर्मकौरोअरः ।

तवैव मुनिग्यातून प्रेयश्रितानुवर्तकः ॥ 2३ ॥

Harisācranda said: “O Vipraṣi! I am now very humiliated and have become very afflicted and distressed. Especially I am your Bhakta, I am your servant; so begraciously pleased and free me from this painful companion of a Cāndāla. O Muni! In lieu of my remnant Dakṣinā, I will be your obedient slave; I will do your work and follow your commands.”

विश्वामित्र वाच

एवमसु महाराज ममेव भव किंकतः ।

किन्तु मढ़चनं कार्य सर्वदैव नारायि ॥ 24 ॥

Vísvāmitra said: “O King! You are then my slave, you will obey always my commands.”

व्यास उचाच

एवमुक्ते5थ वचने राजा हृंसमन्त्रितः ।

अभ्यन्त पुनर्जतितामानां प्राह कौशिकम् ॥ २ ५ ॥

त्वाद्रेशं कर्णिषामि यदैवाहं न संशयः ।

आदेशय द्विजश्र किं कौरोमि ततावन ॥ २ ६ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When Viśvāmitra said so, the King, out of joy, thought that he regained his life and said to Kauśika. “Always I will obey your words; now order me what work I will have to do.”

विश्वासि उवाच

वाण्डालागच्छ महस्मालैयं किं मे प्रच्छसि ।

गृहाण दासं मौल्येन मया दृत्तं तवापुर्ण ॥ 27 ॥

नासि दासेन मे कार्यं विस्तागा वर्तैं मग ।

Viśvāmitra, then addressed the Cāṃḍala and said: “O Cāṃḍala! Come to me and give me the price for this slave. I am now handing this slave

over to you; give me the price and take him. I want money; I have no need for a servant."

व्यास उवाच

एवमुक्ते तदा तेन ध्यपो हृद्यानसः ॥ 28 ॥

आगत्य सम्रिधो तूर्ण विश्वासीमतभाषात ।

Vyāsa said: O King! When Viśvāmitra spoke thus, the Cāndāla, overflowed with joy, came immediately to the Rṣi Viśvāmitra and said:

वाण्डाल उवाच

दशयोजनविस्तीर्ण प्रयागस्य च मण्डले ॥ 29 ॥

भूमि रूपमयीं कृत्वा दायले तेर्द हिकोतमा ।

अस्य विक्रयोनेयमार्तिक्ष प्रहता तवया ॥ 30 ॥

“O Dvija! The relief that you have given me by selling this servant, for that I will give you the ten Yoyanas wide land of Prayāga Manḍalam, covered over with jewels.”

व्यास उवाच

ततो रनसहस्वाणी सुवर्णिभागीचितिकः ।

चाण्डालेन प्रदत्तानि जग्राह डिजसत्तमः ॥ 31 ॥

हरिश्छ्रैतया राजा निर्विकारमुखोपभवत् ।

अम्पन्त तथा ध्यायिष्ठिभागे हि ये पति: ॥ 32 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Cāndāla then gave me thousand gems, one thousand jewels, one thousand pearls and one thousand gold mohurs and Viśvāmitra took them. No signs of distraction nor unpleasantness were visible on the face of the King Hariścandra rather he laid hold of his patience and thought within himself.

तत्तेवे मया कार्य यदयं कारिथयिति ।

अर्थात्रिषे सहसा वागुवाचारगीरिधि ॥ 33 ॥

अनुगोर्भस महाभाग दत्ता सा दक्षिणा स्वाय ।

तातो दिवः पुच्यष्टिः पपात नृपपूर्विन ॥ 34 ॥

"Vísvāmitra is now my master; I will do any work that he puts me in." At this time, the incorporeal voice, the voice of the fourth dimensional space, sounded from the Heavens: "O Fortunate One! You are freed from the Dakṣinā, the debt before that you promised to give me." A shower of flowers fell on the head of the King from the Heavens.

Book VII Chapter XXIV

साधु साधिति ते द्वेशः प्रोषु: मैंरा महीज्ञसः ।

हर्षण महत्ताप्रिद्धो राजा कौशिफकमबवीत् ॥ 35 ॥

At this time the powerful Indra and the other hosts of the Devas praised the King, saying: "Sádhu! Sádhu! Well-done, Well-done." The heart of the King was then filled with intense joy and the King then said to Kauśika.

त्वं हि माता पिता वोव त्वं हिं कवभूमंहमते ।

यदर्थं पोचितोर्डे ते क्षणाच्चैवानूणी कृतः ॥ ३६ ॥

किं करोषि महाबाहो श्रेयो मे वचनं तव ।

एवमुनेकं तु वचने नृपं मुपित्यभात् ॥ ३७ ॥

“O Intelligent One! You are a greater benefactor to me then my father, mother and friend as you have freed me in a moment from my debts. So, O mighty armed one! Your words are beneficial to me. Now order what am I to do.”

विश्वामित्र उवाच

चाण्डालतवचनं कार्यमेधापृथिते ने तृप ।

स्वस्ति तेजस्तिति ते प्रोथ्य तदादाय धनं यदो ॥ 38 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणो सतमस्कन्धे हरीक्षन्नीयास्ताने

यजीवितोउधाच्या: 11 23 11

When the King said so, Viśvāmitra then said: "Go and observe from to-day the words of the Cāndāla. Let good befall on you!" Thus saying, the Maharṣi Viśvāmitra took the money given by the Cāndāla and went away to his own place.

Here ends the Twenty-third Chapter of the Seventh Book on the King Hariścandra's acknowledgment of the slavery of the Cāndāla in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, of 18,000 verses, by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VII Chapter XXIV

CHAPTER XXIV

On Hariscandra in the Burning Ghaṭa of Kāśī

शौनक उवाच

ततः किमकारोत्राजा चापड़ालस्य गृहे ततः ।

तद्वृष्टि सुतत्वयं त्वं पृष्ठतः सत्वरं हिि मे ॥ १ ॥

Śaunaka said: O Sūta! Now describe as quickly as you can in detail what the King Harisācandra did afterwards in the house of the Cāṇḍala.

सूत उवाच

विश्वामित्र नते विश्रे धषणचो हृद्यानसः ।

विश्वामित्रय नतुद्वयं दत्ता बद्यूधा नरेशमू ॥ 2 ॥

असत्यो यास्यसीत्युक्त्वा एड्डेनाताडयत्त्वा ।

दणडगुहारसंघनमतीव्याकुलेत्रदूसम ॥ 3 ॥

Sūta said: When Viśvāmitra went away, the mind of the Cāṇḍala was filled with joy. He already gave to Viśvāmitra that amount of jewels; so he tied now the King and, telling him “Do you now stand on the path of falsehood? began to beat him with sticks.

इखबन्धुविद्योगतर्भानीय निजपकरणो ।

निगड़े स्थापयित्वा तं स्वयं सूचाय पिज्ञतः ॥ 4 ॥

निगडस्थततो राजा वसंश्राण्डालपकरणे ।

अत्रपाने परित्यज्य सदा वै तद्गोचयत् ॥ 5 ॥

The King was already very much tired of the bereavements from his dear ones; now being beaten by the Cāndāla, his senses were lost. In this state the Cāndāla took him to his house and fastened him with a chain. Then the Cāndāla's troubles were over and he fell asleep. The King lived in the Chāndāla's house in that state fettered by a chain; but he did not take any food there.

तन्यी दीनमुखीं दृष्ट्या बालं दीनमुखं पुरः ।

मां सत्यत्सुखाविष्टा मोक्षायिष्टानी नौ नृपः ॥ 6 ॥

उपारचित्ते विधाय दच्चा विलं प्रतिश्रन् ।

रोदमानं सुतं वीक्ष्य मां च साधोधायिष्टिते ॥ 7 ॥

Incessantly he wept for his wife and son and others. “Alas! That thin lady, seeing the sad face of her son is now remembering me with a morose face. She is now perhaps thinking, with a grieved heart, that whenever the King will get the money, he will pay off the promised money to the Brāhimn and then will free us from this yoke of slavery.

ततापर्थं ब्रजामीति रुदंतं बालकं पुनः ।

तात तातेति भाषांत तथा सम्बोध्रायिषिति ॥ 8 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

न सा मां मृगाशाङ्गी वेति चापड़ालतां गतम् ।

राज्यनाशः सुईच्यागो भार्यान्तनयिक्वतः ॥ 9 ॥

Alas! When will that day come when will he see me and this crying child and speak with us. When the son will cry, saying I will go to my father; father! When will he come and speak with the child? That fawn-eyed gentle woman does not know that I am now placed under a Cāṇḍala. Alas I am deprived of my kingdom, friends; and I have sold away my wife and son; now I am bound in the chain of slavery of Cāṇḍala.

तत्रप्राप्तालत तेच्यमहो तु:ख्यप्यमरा ।

एवं स निवसत्रित्यं स्मरंश दयितां सुतम् ॥ 10 ॥

Alas! So many miseries have fallen on me all one after another." Thus thinking incessantly about his dear consort and son, the King passed his days in the house of that Cāndāla.

निनाय दिवसाजाजा चतुगे विधिपीढितः ।

अथाहि पछमे तेन निगाऊभोचितो नृपः ॥ ११ ॥

वाण्डालेनानुशिष्ट धृतरैलापहाणे ।

कुब्देन पर्कैवर्कैयैर्निर्भर्य च पुनः पुनः ॥ १ २ ॥

Four days passed; and on the fifth day the Cāndāla came there and rebuked the King with very harsh words and freed him from his fastenings and said: "Go to the burial ground and collect the clothing of the dead bodies.

कायगश्छ दक्षिणे भागे स्मशानं विद्यते महत् ।

तद्रस्मय यथान्यां न त्यायं तत्वया कविचत् ॥ 13 ॥

इमं च जर्जैं, दण्डें गृहीत्वा याहि मा विम्य।

वीरबाहोरयं दण्ड इति घोपस्व सर्वतः ॥ 14 ॥

There is a wide Śmaśāna (burial ground) on the southern part of Kāśī; go and protect that and whatever is due to you, justly take that; do not leave it. Take this Jarjara club and go there quickly. Say to all that you are the messenger of Virabāhu and this staff is his."

सूत उवाच

कर्मियोघ्रदय काले तु मृतवैलापहारकः ।

हरिश्छोरोभवहाजा गरणाने तद्धानानुः ॥ 15 ॥

चाण्डालेनानुशिष्टु मृतवैलापहारिणा ।

राजा तेन समादिष्टो जगाम शवमिन्दिम् ॥ 16 ॥

Sūta said: O Rṣis! Thus Hariścandra became a Cāndāla's servant and was engaged in collecting the cloths of the dead persons. Thus ordered by the Cāndāla, whose duty was to collect the rages of the dead bodies, the King went to the burial ground.

पुर्यस्तु दक्षिणे देशे विद्यानं भयानकम् ।

गशवान्यसमाकर्णि दुर्गन्थं बहुधूमकम् ॥ 17 ॥

ग्शगानं घोरस्वादं शिवाशतसमाकूलम् ।

गृधगोपायुसंकर्णी शबृन्दपरिवारित् ॥ 18 ॥

To the south of the city Kāśī, was situated the dreadful Śmaśāna, scattered over with the garlands of the dead, bad odours were emitting on all sides and it was covered all over with smoke. Hundreds of jackals were yelling there and the ground was being reverberated by their yells. Vultures, jackals and dogs were at many places dragging the dead bodies.

अश्वस्यंघातसंकीर्ण महादुर्गन्यसंकुलम् ।

अर्धद्धरणशाव्यानि विकसईतपंचिक्षिः ॥ १९ ॥

हर्सतीवाराम्यस्यकावयरेवं व्यवस्थितः ।

नानामुत्सुहरादं महाकोलाहलाकुलम् ॥ 20 ॥

At other places were scattered heaps of bones; the whole ground was covered with the putrid smell of the dead. At some places it seemed that from within the funeral pyre, the half-burnt dead bodies were laughing wildly with their teeth wide open from their mouths. Thus the dead bodies looked terrible when being placed under fire. Lots of dead bodies were brought there and there was a great tumultuous uproar made by the cries of their friends and relatives.

हा पुत्र मित्र हा बन्धो धारलंस एवाया भे ।

हायले भारिनेयहं हा मातुल पितामह ॥ 21 ॥

मातामह पित: पौत्र क्र नातो २येही झांधव ।

इति शब्दे: समाकाणीं भवेर: सर्वदैहिनम् ॥ 22 ॥

जलनामासवमापेद्वष्यपिति ध्यनिसंकुलम् ।

Oh! My son! My friend! My relative! My brother! My child! My dear wife! Oh! My cousin!

Book VII Chapter XXV

Oh! My grandfather! Oh! My father! My grandson! My acquaintance! Where hast thou gone leaving me here! Come once and let me have a sight of thee! With such dreadful sounds as these, the burial ground was being echoed.

Flesh, marrow, fat all were being burnt in the fire and a peculiar sound Son, Son was, being produced there and creating voidness in the minds of the people.

अनंडठटवटाग्बो। शेरो यत्र जायते ॥ 23 ॥

कल्यांतसद्गाकारं मशगर्न तलुवारुणम् ।

स राजा तन संग्रपाते दुःखादेवमशोचत ॥ 24 ॥

The fire was burning with a crackling noise. Thus the Śmaśāna looked very terrible as if the universe was being destroyed at the end of a Kālpa. The King Harisācandra arrived there; and, with extreme pain, he began to give vent to his sorrows.

हा ध्याना मजिलगो यूयं क्त तराझं कृतोचितान् ।

हा प्रिये तूम मे बाल मां वलवता मन्दभायकम् ॥ 25 ॥

बाहुपास्य च कोपेन गता यूयं क दूरतः ।

बिता धर्म मनुव्याणां जायते न सुभं कचित् ॥ 26 ॥

"My ministers, servants! Where are you all now? Where is kingdom that I got by a succession of inheritance! O my Son! O my dear wife! Where are you staying now, at what a long distance, leaving me here out of the Brāhmin's anger. Without Dharma man can never get auspicious fruits. So men should carefully earn Dharma.

यन्तते धारयेतस्मातुभको धार्मेव हि ।

इल्लें चिन्तयन्तत्र चाण्डालोत्के पुनः पुनः ॥ 27 ॥

मलेन दिधस्वर्गः शवानां दर्पेने जबन् ।

लकुटाकारकल्पक्ष धार्वक्षापि ततस्ततः ॥ 28 ॥

The King, whose body was covered with dust and dirt, thought thus repeatedly; and at last, remembering the Cāndāla's words, went out in
